{"id": "65e3a18284ee82c4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:01:01.908958+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Finite terminal-block improvements wash out under the Zhang–Jiang ConPD rule.\n\nDefine phi(x,y)=(-x-y+sqrt((x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)))/2 and psi(x,y)=(3-2y+sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9)))/4 for x,y>=0. Define S_circle(0)=S_bullet(0)=0 and, for n>=1,\nS_circle(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+phi(S_circle(k),S_circle(n-k-1))+S_circle(n-k-1)],\nS_bullet(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+psi(S_circle(k),S_bullet(n-k-1))+S_bullet(n-k-1)].\nWrite B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nFor a nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t)/L.\n\nLet E be any nonempty finite set of finite-length nonnegative schedules, allowing the empty schedule. For b in E, write m_b for its length and s(b) for the sum of its entries. Define\nC_E=max_{b in E} max{0,sqrt(2s(b)+1)-sqrt(B_{m_b})}.\nConstruct a schedule by choosing b in E and an integer r>=0, setting H_0=b, and, for j=1,...,r, choosing any nonnegative schedule a_j of length k_j>=0 with s(a_j)<=S_circle(k_j) and setting\nH_j=[a_j, psi(s(a_j),s(H_{j-1})), H_{j-1}].\nIf N=m_b+sum_{j=1}^r(k_j+1), then every such schedule satisfies\nW_N(H_r)>=1/(sqrt(B_N)+C_E)^2\n=U_N^ZJ (1+C_E/sqrt(B_N))^(-2).\nMoreover B_N>=2N+1. Consequently the lower-bound ratio tends to 1 uniformly over all schedules in this construction as N tends to infinity. In particular no fixed positive relative asymptotic improvement below U_N^ZJ can be obtained solely by using a fixed finite library of improved terminal blocks while retaining the displayed ConPD rule and the primitive-block sum caps. This is a statement about that construction class, not the unrestricted minimax problem.", "evidence": "All arguments below are elementary and self-contained; no previously verified or external theorem is invoked.\n\n1. A lower bound valid for every nonnegative schedule.\nFix any length n>=0 and any h in [0,infinity)^n. Put s=s(h)>=0 and tau=1/(2s+1). In dimension one, for any fixed L>0 define\nf(z)=L z^2/2 when |z|<=tau, and f(z)=L(tau|z|-tau^2/2) when |z|>=tau.\nThe two expressions and their first derivatives agree at z=+tau and z=-tau. The derivative is L times z clipped to [-tau,tau]. This derivative is nondecreasing, so f is convex. Clipping is 1-Lipschitz: if u<=v, its increment lies between 0 and v-u, as can be checked from the three intervals separated by -tau and tau. Thus f has L-Lipschitz derivative. It is differentiable everywhere, has minimizer x_star=0, and f_star=0. Take x_0=1.\nNonnegativity of h implies every partial sum lies in [0,s]. An induction on i yields x_i=1-tau sum_{t=0}^{i-1}h_t: indeed this formula implies\nx_i>=1-tau s=(s+1)/(2s+1)>=tau,\nso f'(x_i)=L tau and the next gradient update gives the same formula at i+1. This also handles s=0 and equality at the boundary. Thus\nx_n=(s+1)/(2s+1), f(x_n)=L tau x_n-L tau^2/2=L/(2(2s+1)).\nSince |x_0-x_star|^2=1, this legal instance proves W_n(h)>=1/(2s(h)+1), including n=0.\n\n2. Scalar form of one join.\nFor x,y>=0 put A=x+1>=1 and D=2y+1>=1. Direct substitution into the definition of psi gives\n2[x+psi(x,y)+y]+1\n=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2\n=((sqrt(D)+sqrt(D+8A))/2)^2.\nDenote the middle expression by G(A,D) and define T_A(q)=(q+sqrt(q^2+8A))/2 for q>=0. Then sqrt(G(A,D))=T_A(sqrt(D)). Both G and T are increasing in each of their displayed arguments.\nFor q>=p>=0, rationalizing the difference of square roots gives\n0<=T_A(q)-T_A(p)\n=(q-p)/2 [1+(q+p)/(sqrt(q^2+8A)+sqrt(p^2+8A))]\n<=q-p,\nbecause the denominator in the fraction is at least q+p; when q=p=0 the difference is zero without division. It follows also by interchanging q and p that T_A is 1-Lipschitz. In particular if q<=p+C for C>=0, monotonicity and this inequality imply T_A(q)<=T_A(p)+C.\nAll bridge steps are nonnegative: since x,y>=0, sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9))>=2y+1, the formula gives psi(x,y)>=1.\n\n3. A reference recursion dominated by the exact benchmark.\nFix a construction from the statement. Write N_0=m_b, N_j=N_{j-1}+k_j+1 and A_j=1+s(a_j). Its actual masses D_j=2s(H_j)+1 satisfy\nD_0=2s(b)+1 and D_j=G(A_j,D_{j-1}).\nSet reference masses E_0=B_{m_b} and E_j=G(A_j,E_{j-1}).\nWe prove E_j<=B_{N_j} by induction. The assertion is equality at j=0. If it holds at j-1, then the primitive-block sum cap and monotonicity of G give\nE_j<=G(1+S_circle(k_j), B_{N_{j-1}}).\nIn the defining maximum for S_bullet(N_j), choose the valid index k=k_j; the remaining index is N_j-k_j-1=N_{j-1}. By the identity in part 2 this choice yields\nB_{N_j}>=G(1+S_circle(k_j), B_{N_{j-1}}).\nThis establishes the induction without any assumed structure for the a_j schedules beyond their length and sum cap.\n\n4. Uniform comparison with a finite seed library.\nBy definition of C_E, sqrt(D_0)<=sqrt(E_0)+C_E. Applying the last assertion of part 2 inductively with A=A_j shows sqrt(D_j)<=sqrt(E_j)+C_E for every j. Part 3 gives\nsqrt(2s(H_r)+1)=sqrt(D_r)<=sqrt(B_N)+C_E.\nApply the legal Huber lower bound in part 1:\nW_N(H_r)>=1/D_r>=1/(sqrt(B_N)+C_E)^2.\nThis proves the claimed finite-horizon inequality.\n\n5. Divergence and uniformity.\nThe maximum defining B_n includes k=0, so B_n>=G(1,B_{n-1}). Since sqrt(D^2+8D)>=D for D>=0, G(1,D)>=D+2. Starting with B_0=1 gives B_n>=2n+1 by induction.\nBecause E is finite, C_E is a finite constant independent of the depth r, of the chosen seed, and of all the a_j schedules. Thus\n(1+C_E/sqrt(B_N))^(-2)>=(1+C_E/sqrt(2N+1))^(-2)->1.\nExplicitly, for epsilon in (0,1), when C_E>0 choose any integer N_epsilon>=0 for which\nsqrt(2N_epsilon+1)>=C_E/((1-epsilon)^(-1/2)-1);\nif C_E=0 take N_epsilon=0. Then for every constructed H_r of any length N>=N_epsilon,\nW_N(H_r)>=(1-epsilon)U_N^ZJ.\nThis threshold is independent of the constructed schedule. Were a fixed delta in (0,1) and an unbounded sequence of constructed schedules to satisfy W_N(H_r)<=(1-delta)U_N^ZJ, choose epsilon=delta/2 and N>=N_epsilon, obtaining a contradiction. The conclusion is restricted to the displayed construction; it imposes no assertion on arbitrary schedules outside that class.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_terminal_seed_washout", "scope": "restricted-class obstruction; reference scalar masses are separately subscripted E_j.", "C_E": "Maximum positive square-root mass improvement of a seed in E over the same-length benchmark.", "H_j": "Schedule obtained after j successive displayed ConPD joins from the seed H_0=b.", "a_j": "Nonnegative prefix schedule of length k_j and sum at most S_circle(k_j).", "m_b": "Length of terminal schedule b.", "N_j": "Length of H_j.", "A_j": "One plus the sum of a_j.", "D_j": "Actual mass 2s(H_j)+1.", "E_j": "Reference mass sequence starting at B_{m_b} and using the same G joins.", "G": "Scalar mass join G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.", "T_A": "Square-root mass join q maps to (q+sqrt(q^2+8A))/2.", "tau": "Positive Huber threshold 1/(2s+1) for total stepsize sum s.", "x_star": "A minimizer of the smooth convex objective.", "f_star": "The minimum objective value f(x_star).", "ConPD": "The schedule join [a,psi(s(a),s(b)),b] defined explicitly in the statement."}}
{"id": "8df4e164bc233246", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:05:50.794800+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C3 concrete ordered-cut witness: an asymmetric Huber function gives the exact one-checkpoint lower bound W_n(h) >= (h_j-1)^2/[(1+sum_{t<j} h_t)^2(1+2 sum_{t>j} h_t)] for every h_j>=1, with no spacing, magnitude or zero-step exclusions.", "evidence": "Self-contained candidate, not a verified fact. Normalize L=1, x0=1, x*=0. Write P=sum_{t<j}h_t, b=h_j>=1, T=sum_{t>j}h_t. Put c=1/(P+1), a=c(b-1)/(2T+1). For b>1 define f'(x)=max(-a,min(c,x)), f(0)=0; it is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, so f is legal and minimized at0. Every pre-checkpoint iterate is 1-c times its prefix sum, at least c; thus gradients there are c and x_j=c. The checkpoint gives x_{j+1}=-c(b-1)=-a(2T+1). Every suffix iterate stays in the left affine region, with derivative -a, since the final point is -a(T+1)<=-a and nonnegative steps move right monotonically. Hence f(x_n)=a^2(T+1)-a^2/2=a^2(T+1/2); twice this is c^2(b-1)^2/(2T+1), exactly the displayed bound. If b=1, the bound is0 and can be witnessed by the zero function. No machine computation or external result is used. The sequence order enters through P and T. This is a usable split witness, but taking the maximum of these one-cut bounds has no established comparison to the exact Zhang-Jiang profile; any such target-strength assertion remains open, not a technical lemma.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {}, "glossary": {"P": "Sum of the steps before the selected zero-based checkpoint j.", "b": "Selected step h_j, assumed at least1.", "T": "Sum of the steps after the selected checkpoint.", "c": "Positive right Huber slope 1/(P+1).", "a": "Left Huber slope magnitude c(b-1)/(2T+1).", "C3": "Ordered single-checkpoint asymmetric-Huber lower-bound mechanism."}}
{"id": "b0a9e2cb6cd019f7", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:07:52.837788+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Orthogonal-moment obstruction for randomized gradient-descent adversaries. Define c_0=0 and c_{k+1}=c_k+2+2 sqrt(c_k+1) for all integers k>=0, and define beta_n=4/(1+sqrt(1+2c_n))^2. Let n>=1 be an integer, L>0, and h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}) in [0,infinity)^n be any predetermined schedule with finite entries. Consider any probability distribution on legal instances in a common finite-dimensional Euclidean space: each realization consists of a convex differentiable f:R^d->R with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and an initial point x_0 with ||x_0-x_star||=1. For each realization let x_{i+1}=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i), 0<=i<n, and set g_i=grad f(x_i)/L, including g_n. Assume all data used in expectations are measurable and E<g_i,g_j>=0 whenever 0<=i<j<=n. Then\nE[2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)] <= beta_n <= 2/n^2.\nThe deterministic conclusion follows by taking a point-mass distribution: every legal trajectory whose n+1 gradients are mutually orthogonal has normalized last-iterate objective error at most beta_n. Moreover n^2 beta_n tends to 2. Consequently, for any positive comparison sequence V_n with n^2 V_n tending to infinity, every family of such randomized adversaries has expected normalized error divided by V_n tending to zero, uniformly over schedules and distributions satisfying the assumptions. This bounds the strength of the orthogonal-moment adversary class; it is not an upper bound on the unrestricted worst-case error W_n(h), and it does not resolve the project's unrestricted minimax target.", "evidence": "All expectations below refer to the distribution in the statement. No external result is invoked without a derivation.\n\n1. Normalize and prove the required smooth-convex inequality.\nIn each realization define F(x)=(f(x)-f(x_star))/L. This is convex, has 1-Lipschitz gradient, has minimum value0 at x_star, and its gradient at x_i is g_i. Its gradient at a minimizer is0: if a differentiable function has a minimum, each directional derivative there is0.\nFor any differentiable function H with 1-Lipschitz gradient and any vectors u,v, the fundamental theorem of calculus along the line segment gives\nH(u+v)-H(u)-<grad H(u),v>\n=integral_0^1 <grad H(u+tv)-grad H(u),v>dt <= ||v||^2/2.\nFor fixed y define H(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z>. Convexity and grad H(y)=0 imply H(y)<=H(z) for all z. Apply the preceding inequality at u=x and v=-(grad F(x)-grad F(y)). Since grad H(x)=grad F(x)-grad F(y), this gives\nH(y)<=H(x)-||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.\nRearranging proves, for every x,y,\nF(x)>=F(y)+<grad F(y),x-y>+||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2. (I)\nAll uses of smooth convex interpolation below are this necessary inequality, now proved directly.\n\n2. Integrability and notation.\nWrite X=x_0-x_star, p_i=E F(x_i), q_i=E||g_i||^2, and a_i=E<X,g_i> for 0<=i<=n. The notation p_i denotes an expected value, whereas F and g_i can be random. Since ||X||=1 in every realization, E||X||^2=1.\nThe recursion and the Lipschitz gradient imply\n||x_i-x_star||<=product_{t=0}^{i-1}(1+h_t)\nand ||g_i||<=||x_i-x_star||. Empty products equal1. The line-segment bound in part1 at x_star gives 0<=F(x_i)<=||x_i-x_star||^2/2. Thus every expectation used here is finite even though the finite steps have no uniform upper bound.\nIf some q_i=0, nonnegativity implies g_i=0 almost surely. Then x_i is a minimizer almost surely by convexity, and all later iterates equal x_i. Therefore p_n=0, and the required inequality follows since beta_n>0. It remains to handle q_i>0 for every i.\n\n3. Lower bounds forced on the initial displacement.\nFrom (I) against the minimizer, p_n>=q_n/2. Set b=p_n-q_n/2>=0.\nApply (I) with x=x_i, y=x_{i+1}. The GD identity is x_i-x_{i+1}=h_i g_i. Expected orthogonality gives E<g_{i+1},g_i>=0 and E||g_i-g_{i+1}||^2=q_i+q_{i+1}. Hence\np_i-p_{i+1}>=(q_i+q_{i+1})/2\nfor each 0<=i<n. Summing these inequalities from i through n-1 yields\np_i>=p_n+q_i/2+sum_{k=i+1}^{n-1}q_k+q_n/2\nwhen i<n.\nApply (I) with x=x_star and y=x_i. It gives\nE<g_i,x_i-x_star>>=p_i+q_i/2.\nThe recursion gives x_i-x_star=X-sum_{t<i}h_t g_t. Expected orthogonality therefore gives E<g_i,x_i-x_star>=a_i. For every i, including i=n, we conclude\na_i >= T_i, where T_i=b+sum_{k=i}^n q_k>0. (II)\nFor i<n this follows by combining the last two displayed inequalities; for i=n it is a_n>=p_n+q_n/2=b+q_n=T_n.\n\n4. A finite-dimensional second-moment inequality.\nExpand the nonnegative expectation of a squared norm:\n0<=E||X-sum_{i=0}^n(a_i/q_i)g_i||^2\n=1-2sum_{i=0}^n a_i^2/q_i+sum_{i=0}^n a_i^2/q_i\n=1-sum_{i=0}^n a_i^2/q_i.\nThe cross terms in the double sum vanish by expected orthogonality. Since a_i>=T_i>0 and q_i>0, (II) gives\n1>=sum_{i=0}^n T_i^2/q_i. (III)\nThis step works directly in the original finite-dimensional space and does not require that any random sample's gradients be orthogonal.\n\n5. Eliminate the intermediate gradient energies exactly.\nFor 0<=k<=n, claim\nsum_{i=0}^{k-1} T_i^2/q_i >= c_k T_k,\nwhere the empty sum for k=0 is0. The claim holds at k=0. Suppose k<n and it holds at k. Since T_k=q_k+T_{k+1},\nc_k T_k+T_k^2/q_k\n=(c_k+1)q_k+(c_k+2)T_{k+1}+T_{k+1}^2/q_k.\nThe nonnegativity of\n(sqrt(c_k+1)*sqrt(q_k)-T_{k+1}/sqrt(q_k))^2\nbounds the sum of the first and third terms below by 2sqrt(c_k+1)T_{k+1}. Therefore the last display is at least c_{k+1}T_{k+1}, proving the induction.\nTaking k=n in this claim and adding the final term gives from (III)\n1>=c_n T_n+T_n^2/q_n\n=q_n z(z+c_n),\nwhere z=T_n/q_n=1+b/q_n>=1.\nSince 2p_n=2b+q_n=q_n(2z-1), it follows that\n2p_n <= (2z-1)/(z(z+c_n)).\nPut r=(1+sqrt(1+2c_n))/2. Then c_n=2r(r-1) and r>=1. The exact identity\nz(z+c_n)-r^2(2z-1)=(z-r)^2>=0\nimplies\n(2z-1)/(z(z+c_n))<=1/r^2=beta_n.\nBecause ||x_0-x_star||^2=1 in each realization, 2p_n is the expected normalized error in the statement.\n\n6. Estimate and asymptotics for beta_n.\nAn induction gives c_k>=k^2: it holds at0; if it holds at k, then\nc_{k+1}=c_k+2+2sqrt(c_k+1)>=k^2+2+2k>=(k+1)^2.\nThe relation c_n=2r(r-1)<=2r^2 now gives beta_n=1/r^2<=2/c_n<=2/n^2 for n>=1.\nFor the asymptotic assertion put s_k=sqrt(c_k+1), so s_0=1 and\ns_{k+1}=sqrt((s_k+1)^2+1).\nRationalization gives\ns_{k+1}-s_k=1+1/(sqrt((s_k+1)^2+1)+s_k+1).\nThus s_k>=k+1 and\ns_{k+1}-s_k<=1+1/[2(k+2)].\nSumming shows\nk+1<=s_k<=k+1+(1/2)sum_{j=2}^{k+1}1/j\n<=k+1+(1/2)log(k+1),\nwhere the last bound follows by integrating 1/t on intervals [j-1,j]. Consequently s_k/k tends to1 and c_k/k^2 tends to1. Substitution into beta_n gives n^2 beta_n tending to2.\nFinally, 0<=expected normalized error/V_n<=beta_n/V_n, which tends to0 whenever n^2V_n tends to infinity. Every bound depends only on n, giving the asserted uniformity. No interchange of infimum and expectation has occurred.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max_orthogonal_moment_obstruction", "status": "selected_for_verification", "scope": "specific adversary-class obstruction; not resolution of P", "artifact": "workers/max/local_memory/orthogonal_moment_candidate.md", "external_context": "arXiv:1502.05666 Corollary1; necessary inequality proved directly here"}, "glossary": {"c_k": "Nonnegative scalar sequence c_0=0, c_{k+1}=c_k+2+2sqrt(c_k+1).", "beta_n": "Orthogonal-moment error cap 4/(1+sqrt(1+2c_n))^2.", "F": "For each sampled instance, normalized objective (f-f(x_star))/L.", "X": "Random initial displacement x_0-x_star, with norm1 almost surely.", "g_i": "Normalized random gradient grad f(x_i)/L.", "p_i": "Expected normalized objective value E F(x_i).", "q_i": "Expected squared normalized gradient norm E||g_i||^2.", "a_i": "Expected initial-gradient inner product E<X,g_i>.", "b": "Nonnegative terminal excess p_n-q_n/2.", "T_i": "b plus the sum of q_k over i<=k<=n.", "r": "Scalar (1+sqrt(1+2c_n))/2.", "s_k": "Scalar sqrt(c_k+1) used to estimate c_k.", "V_n": "Any positive comparison sequence such that n^2 V_n tends to infinity.", "W_n": "Unrestricted normalized worst-case objective error from the project; this result does not upper-bound it.", "h": "The fixed finite nonnegative stepsize schedule.", "x_star": "Chosen minimizer in each sampled instance.", "x_i": "The iterate after i gradient-descent steps.", "f": "A sampled convex differentiable objective whose gradient is L-Lipschitz."}}
{"id": "f5617e82f47bbdb0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:08:14.171978+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C2 exact cut interface is a pair of polyhedral interpolation envelopes with an explicit Moreau witness; this is a growing-state reformulation, not yet a profile comparison.", "evidence": "Full self-contained support derivation: research/max3_exact_cut_envelopes.md. For L=1 transform each jet by y_i=x_i-g_i and z_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Store A(u)=max_i[z_i+<g_i,u-y_i>] and B(v)=max_i[<v,y_i>-z_i], including optimum(0,0,0). New jet at x, gradient g, y=x-g is admissible iff A(y)<=z<=<g,y>-B(g). Update A by the new support plane and B by the new lifted-point plane. The two inequalities retain every crossing interpolation constraint before maximization. The finite polyhedral function phi=A generates a legal global f(x)=min_u[phi(u)+||x-u||^2/2], explicitly interpolating all jets; the file proves smoothness and realizability. Exact GD updates and terminal value 2z_n+||g_n||^2 give W_n(h) after sup over all dimensions for unit initial distance. The state complexity can grow with n. Any uniform comparison to U_n^ZJ remains theorem-strength absent a new invariant; no phi/psi recurrence is being asserted and this generic support calculation is not submitted for verification.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_exact_cut_envelopes.md", "mechanism": "two-sided polyhedral history hulls", "status": "exact interface candidate; no profile reduction"}, "glossary": {"A": "Maximum of retained affine support planes in transformed point space.", "B": "Maximum of retained lifted-point planes in gradient space.", "y_i": "Transformed sampled point x_i-g_i.", "z_i": "Transformed value f_i-||g_i||^2/2.", "phi": "The final polyhedral convex function A whose Moreau envelope interpolates the jets."}}
{"id": "79da55e26c4a7406", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:10:43.620451+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Candidate exact rational seed and obstruction for a weakened primitive potential. The one-step schedule h=[3/2] satisfies the generalized primitive certificate V_h(3/2)>=0 defined below, and its coefficient 3/2 is sharp for that schedule. It has a larger sum than the saturated primitive one-step seed [sqrt(2)], but a positive potential deficit. Under the explicit symmetric generalized-ConPP rule below, every seed with any fixed positive deficit has total stepsize sum Theta(n), and its true objective worst case is Theta(1/n). Thus this particular attempt to amplify an improved-sum seed loses the silver exponent; it cannot disprove (P). This is an unverified candidate package, reserved for main-agent prioritization because max4 has used the initial-wave verification allowance.", "evidence": "Work at L=1; replacing f by f/L restores arbitrary L without changing normalized steps. For any finite nonnegative schedule h of length n, let s=sum_i h_i and let x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). Define\nV_h(C)=sum_{i=0}^{n-1} h_i[2(f_i-f_n)+||g_i||^2+2<g_i,x_0-x_i>]-||x_n-x_0||^2-C||g_n||^2.\nThe certificate V_h(C)>=0 is required for every finite-dimensional convex 1-smooth f and every initial point. Here C>=0. Define Q_{i,j}=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nPreliminary interpolation inequality. For completeness Q_{i,j}<=0 follows without an external theorem: apply the descent inequality F(u-grad F(u))<=F(u)-||grad F(u)||^2/2 to F(z)=f(z)-<g_i,z>. Convexity gives that x_i minimizes F, because grad F(x_i)=0. Hence F(x_j)-F(x_i)>=||g_j-g_i||^2/2, which is precisely -Q_{i,j}>=0. The descent inequality itself follows by integrating the gradient along a segment and using 1-Lipschitz continuity. Summing Q_{i,j}<=0 and Q_{j,i}<=0 also gives the usual cocoercivity inequality.\n\n1. Explicit rational seed.\nFor h=[3/2], direct substitution x_1=x_0-(3/2)g_0 gives\nV_h(3/2)=3(f_0-f_1)-(3/4)||g_0||^2-(3/2)||g_1||^2\n=4(-Q_{1,0})+(-Q_{0,1})+||g_1+g_0/2||^2>=0.\nAll conic multipliers are positive rational numbers and the remaining term is an explicit squared norm. On f(z)=z^2/2, x_0=1, x_1=-1/2, equality holds. Increasing C would decrease V by a positive multiple of ||g_1||^2, so C=3/2 is the maximum valid coefficient for this seed.\nIts sum s=3/2 exceeds sqrt(2), whereas its deficit from the saturated primitive coefficient is d=s(s+1)-C=9/4>0.\n\n2. Exact generalized composition.\nSuppose a and b have sums x and y and certificates V_a(C_a)>=0 and V_b(C_b)>=0, with C_a,C_b>=0. Choose 1<=alpha<y+2 and form c=[a,alpha,b]. Let p be the endpoint index of a and q the endpoint of c; b starts at p+1, with x_{p+1}=x_p-alpha g_p. Define\nd0=y+2-alpha,\nA0=y+3alpha-2alpha^2,\nB0=2C_b+y+alpha.\nFor b, evaluated with initial point x_{p+1}, the following exact identity holds:\nV_b(C_b)-sum_{i=p+1}^{q-1}b_{i-p-1}(Q_{i,p}+Q_{i,q})-[(alpha-1)Q_{p,q}+Q_{q,p}]\n=d0(f_p-f_q)-A0||g_p||^2/2-B0||g_q||^2/2.\nThe left side is nonnegative: V_b>=0, all Q<=0, b_i>=0 and alpha-1>=0. This identity can be checked by setting w=x_{p+1}-x_q=sum b_i g_i: all terms involving internal function values cancel, the w cross terms cancel between the two groups of interpolation inequalities, and the remaining gradient coefficients are the ones displayed.\nConsequently\n2(f_p-f_q)>=(A0/d0)||g_p||^2+(B0/d0)||g_q||^2.\nDirect expansion of V_c gives\nV_c(C_c)=V_a(C_a)+V_b(C_b)+2(x+alpha)(f_p-f_q)\n+(C_a+alpha-alpha^2)||g_p||^2+(C_b-C_c)||g_q||^2.\nTherefore the sufficient conditions\nC_a>=alpha^2-alpha-(x+alpha)A0/d0,\nC_c=C_b+(x+alpha)B0/d0\nyield V_c(C_c)>=0. These conditions provide a constructive exact upper-bound certificate using only the prior two certificates and nonnegative interpolation multipliers.\n\nWrite P=alpha^2+(x+y)alpha-(xy+2x+2y+2).\nFor the equality choice in the first sufficient condition, define d_a=x(x+1)-C_a and d_b=y(y+1)-C_b. Algebra gives\nd_a=-(x+alpha)P/(y+2-alpha),\nd_c=(x+alpha+y)(x+alpha+y+1)-C_c\n=d_a+[(2x+y+alpha+2)/(y+2-alpha)]d_b.\nThe deficit upper ceiling d>=0 for any valid certificate follows by evaluating V_h(C) on a Huber function with all iterates in its affine region: then all gradients equal a nonzero vector and V_h(C)=[s(s+1)-C]||g||^2. This verifies the role of the Huber-saturated value s(s+1).\n\n3. Symmetric recursive construction and exact dynamics.\nStart with any seed h^(0) of length n_0 and sum s_0>0 satisfying V_{h^(0)}(C_0)>=0, 0<=C_0<s_0(s_0+1). In particular the rational seed above qualifies.\nAt level j put r_j=s_j+1 and\ne_j=[s_j(s_j+1)-C_j]/r_j^2,\np_j=1-e_j=(C_j+r_j)/r_j^2.\nThe initial assumptions give 0<e_0<1 and 0<p_0<1. Let t_j be the unique number in (0,sqrt(2)-1) satisfying\np_j=t_j^2(3+t_j)/(1-t_j).\nThe right side is continuous and strictly increasing for 0<t<sqrt(2)-1, starts at zero, and equals one at the upper endpoint.\nChoose alpha_j=1+r_j t_j and set\nh^(j+1)=[h^(j),alpha_j,h^(j)].\nUse the equality case of part 2 to define C_{j+1}. The relation defining t_j is exactly the first equality condition: with x=y=s_j and alpha=1+r_j t_j,\nP=r_j^2(t_j^2+2t_j-1), x+alpha=r_j(1+t_j), y+2-alpha=r_j(1-t_j),\nand hence the required normalized deficit is\ne_j=(1+t_j)(1-2t_j-t_j^2)/(1-t_j)\n=1-t_j^2(3+t_j)/(1-t_j).\nAll bridge steps exceed one and remain below y+2. The formula C_{j+1}=C_j+(s_j+alpha_j)(2C_j+s_j+alpha_j)/(s_j+2-alpha_j) is nonnegative. The certificate is consequently valid at every level by part 2. No state other than the current GD iterate is used; the whole finite schedule is fixed in advance.\n\nLengths and shifted sums obey the exact identities\nn_j+1=(n_0+1)2^j,\nr_{j+1}=(2+t_j)r_j.\nThe deficit identity in part 2 gives\ne_{j+1}=4e_j/[(1-t_j)(2+t_j)^2].\nEquivalently,\np_{j+1}=p_j R(t_j), where R(t)=(3+t)/[(1-t)(2+t)^2].\nTo check the second identity, use p_j=t_j^2(3+t_j)/(1-t_j) and expand the first identity.\n\nFor 0<t<sqrt(2)-1 one has\n(1-t)(2+t)^2-(3+t)=1-t-3t^2-t^3\n=(1-t)[1-t^2(3+t)/(1-t)]>0,\nso 0<R(t)<1. Thus p_{j+1}<p_j and hence t_{j+1}<t_j. Furthermore\nd(log R(t))/dt=1/(3+t)+1/(1-t)-2/(2+t)\n=2(t^2+4t+1)/[(3+t)(1-t)(2+t)]>0.\nLet kappa=R(t_0)<1. Then p_j<=p_0 kappa^j. Since t_j^2(3+t_j)/(1-t_j)>=3t_j^2,\nt_j<=sqrt(p_0/3) kappa^(j/2).\nConsequently the sum of the t_j is at most sqrt(p_0/3)/(1-sqrt(kappa)), and\nr_0 2^j<=r_j\n=r_0 2^j product_{i=0}^{j-1}(1+t_i/2)\n<=r_0 2^j exp(sqrt(p_0/3)/(2(1-sqrt(kappa)))).\nThus s_j=Theta(n_j), with constants depending only on the fixed seed. This establishes exact failure of silver-exponent amplification for every positive-deficit seed under this specified rule.\n\n4. True objective behavior and comparison with the actual benchmark.\nThe Huber construction proved in fact_id 0be8b54e0a33381c, proof part 1, applies to every nonnegative schedule and gives W_{n_j}(h^(j))>=1/(2s_j+1). Thus the schedules have a lower bound of order 1/n_j.\nThey also have an upper bound of order 1/n_j from their certificate. Indeed let beta_j=(1+sqrt(1+4C_j))/2. Cancelling the x_star terms in V_h(C)>=0 gives\n||x_0-x_star||^2/2+sum_i h_i Q_{i,star}-s(f_n-f_star)\n>=||x_n-x_star||^2/2+C||g_n||^2/2.\nAdd beta Q_{n,star}-beta(f_n-f_star) and use beta^2=C+beta. The right side becomes ||x_n-x_star-beta g_n||^2/2. Since all Q_{i,star}<=0 and all their coefficients are nonnegative,\nW_n(h)<=1/(s+beta).\nAs beta_j>=1, the upper and lower bounds together give W_{n_j}(h^(j))=Theta(1/n_j).\n\nFor an explicit comparison with the exact DP benchmark, set lambda=1+sqrt(2) and rho=log_2(lambda). The primitive DP in fact 0be8b54e0a33381c has shifted join\nF(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt(A^2+6AB+B^2))/2, so F(A,A)=lambda A. Its primitive sums are nondecreasing because appending the empty primitive block adds phi(x,0)>=1. Hence S_circle(2^k-1)>=lambda^k-1 by induction. For any n>=1 choose k with 2^k<=n<2^{k+1}. The dominant recurrence at split n-1, with empty terminal block, gives S_bullet(n)>=S_circle(n-1). It follows that\nB_n=2S_bullet(n)+1>=2lambda^k-1>=2 n^rho/lambda-1.\nTherefore the ratio W_{n_j}(h^(j))/U_{n_j}^ZJ grows at least as a positive constant times n_j^(rho-1) and tends to infinity. This is a failure of the specified scalar-potential amplification route, not a universal lower bound and not an impossibility statement about other richer potentials.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_generalized_primitive_potential", "fact_ids": ["0be8b54e0a33381c"], "research_path": "research/max4_generalized_primitive_deficit.md", "status": "complete candidate argument; unverified, initial-wave quota exhausted"}, "glossary": {"V_h(C)": "Intrinsic generalized primitive certificate explicitly defined in the proof.", "C": "Nonnegative endpoint gradient-norm coefficient in V_h(C).", "Q_{i,j}": "Smooth-convex interpolation expression with sign Q_{i,j}<=0, defined in the proof.", "d_a": "Deficit x(x+1)-C_a of the left block.", "d_b": "Deficit y(y+1)-C_b of the right block.", "d_c": "Deficit of the concatenated block.", "r_j": "Shifted sum s_j+1 of the level-j schedule.", "e_j": "Normalized coefficient deficit [s_j(s_j+1)-C_j]/r_j^2.", "p_j": "Complementary normalized coefficient (C_j+r_j)/r_j^2=1-e_j.", "t_j": "The unique root in (0,sqrt(2)-1) of p_j=t_j^2(3+t_j)/(1-t_j).", "R(t)": "Quality-ratio function (3+t)/((1-t)(2+t)^2).", "kappa": "Seed-dependent contraction ratio R(t_0)<1.", "beta_j": "Coefficient (1+sqrt(1+4C_j))/2 used to complete an objective bound square.", "h^(j)": "Explicit finite schedule built by symmetric generalized-ConPP doubling.", "s_j": "Sum of entries of h^(j).", "n_j": "Length of h^(j).", "lambda": "Silver number 1+sqrt(2).", "rho": "Exponent log_2(1+sqrt(2))."}}
{"id": "90e2504de7621d07", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:10:57.268871+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Exact single-cut Huber lower bounds are asymptotically insufficient for the Zhang–Jiang profile.\n\nFor any integer n>=0 and h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}) in [0,infinity)^n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, all x_0!=x_star, and GD x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and f_star=f(x_star). Put s(h)=sum_{t=0}^{n-1}h_t. For 0<=j<n let P_j=sum_{t<j}h_t and T_j=sum_{t>j}h_t, and let (u)_+=max(u,0). Define\nK_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s(h)), max_{0<=j<n} (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]},\nwhere the inner maximum is omitted at n=0.\nThen W_n(h)>=K_n(h) for every such schedule. Every nonzero term indexed by j is attained as the normalized error of an explicit one-dimensional convex L-smooth instance with a minimizer.\n\nFor x,y>=0 define\nphi(x,y)=(-x-y+sqrt((x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)))/2,\npsi(x,y)=(3-2y+sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9)))/4.\nDefine S_circle(0)=S_bullet(0)=0 and, for n>=1,\nS_circle(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+phi(S_circle(k),S_circle(n-k-1))+S_circle(n-k-1)],\nS_bullet(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+psi(S_circle(k),S_bullet(n-k-1))+S_bullet(n-k-1)].\nPut B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, and p=log_2 3.\nFor all n>=0, S_circle(n)+1<=(n+1)^p and B_n<=1+4n^p.\nFor every integer n>=2, the schedule ell^(n)_t=t+1, 0<=t<n, satisfies\nK_n(ell^(n))<=16/n^2 and\nK_n(ell^(n))/U_n^ZJ<=16(1+4n^p)/n^2 -> 0 as n->infinity.\nConsequently this particular maximum of the global Huber witness and all these single-cut witnesses cannot itself yield a uniform asymptotic lower bound (1-o(1))U_n^ZJ. This is only a limitation of K_n; it makes no small-error assertion about W_n(ell^(n)) and does not resolve the unrestricted minimax target.", "evidence": "## Legal single-cut witness\n\nScaling permits L=1. Fix j with b=h_j>1; put P=P_j,T=T_j,\nc=1/(P+1), a=c(b-1)/(2T+1). Define f(0)=0 and\nf'(x)=max(-a,min(c,x)). This derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and\n1-Lipschitz. Hence f is differentiable, convex, 1-smooth, and minimized at0.\nStart at x_0=1. Up to the checkpoint all gradients equal c: nonnegative steps\ngive x_i=1-c sum_{t<i}h_t>=1-cP=c for i<=j. Thus x_j=c, and the checkpoint\nsends x_{j+1}=c(1-b)=-a(2T+1).\n\nOn the suffix all gradients equal -a. Every suffix partial sum is at most T,\nso each iterate is at most -a(2T+1)+aT=-a(T+1)<=-a. Therefore\nx_n=-a(T+1), f(x_n)=a^2(T+1/2), and\n\n\\[\n2f(x_n)=a^2(2T+1)=\\frac{(b-1)^2}{(P+1)^2(2T+1)}.\n\\]\n\nThe initial squared distance is1. Multiplying f by L proves the same normalized\nstatement for every L>0. Terms with h_j<=1 are zero and require no construction.\nThe global Huber term is proved in predecessor fact 0be8b54e0a33381c, proof\npart 1, including zero and empty schedules. Taking a finite maximum preserves\nthe lower bound, because the worst-case function may depend on h and j.\n\n## A crude exact-recursion bound sufficient for the separation\n\nSet p=log_2 3, C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.\nThe primitive recurrence becomes\n\n\\[\nC_n=\\max_{0\\leq k<n}\\Omega(C_k,C_{n-k-1}),\\qquad\n\\Omega(a,b)=\\frac{a+b+\\sqrt{(a+b)^2+4ab}}2.\n\\]\n\nThe function Omega is increasing in both nonnegative arguments. When a>=b>=0,\nthe inequality sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab)<=a+3b follows by squaring, with the right\nsquare exceeding the left by8b^2. Thus Omega(a,b)<=a+2b.\nFor real u>=v>0, put r=u/v>=1. The derivative of\n(r+1)^p-r^p is p[(r+1)^(p-1)-r^(p-1)]>0, so\n(r+1)^p-r^p>=2^p-1=2. Consequently\nOmega(u^p,v^p)<=u^p+2v^p<=(u+v)^p.\nSymmetry handles v>=u. Since C_0=1, induction on n using\nu=k+1, v=n-k proves C_n<=(n+1)^p.\n\nThe dominant mass recurrence, obtained directly from psi and also displayed\nin predecessor 0be8b54e0a33381c, is\n\n\\[\nB_n=\\max_{0\\leq k<n}G(C_k,B_{n-k-1}),\\qquad\nG(a,d)=2a+\\frac d2+\\frac12\\sqrt{d^2+8ad}.\n\\]\n\nSince sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=d+4a for nonnegative a,d, G(a,d)<=d+4a.\nStarting with B_0=1, induction and the primitive bound give, for m=n-k-1,\n\n\\[\nG(C_k,B_m)\\leq1+4m^p+4(k+1)^p\\leq1+4n^p.\n\\]\n\nThe last inequality uses u^p+v^p<=(u+v)^p for p>=1 and nonnegative u,v;\nthis follows, when v>0, by the increasing function (r+1)^p-r^p being at\nleast its value1 at r=0, and is equality when v=0. Hence B_n<=1+4n^p.\n\n## Linear schedule defeats the single-cut functional\n\nFor h_t=t+1, the total sum is n(n+1)/2, so the global Huber term is at most\n1/n^2. At checkpoint j, P_j=j(j+1)/2 and (h_j-1)_+=j.\nWhen j>=n/2,\n\n\\[\n\\frac{j^2}{(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)}\n\\leq\\frac{4}{j^2}\\leq\\frac{16}{n^2}.\n\\]\n\nWhen j<n/2, the integers satisfy j+1<=(n+1)/2 and j+2<=(n+3)/2. Thus\n\n\\[\n1+2T_j=1+n(n+1)-(j+1)(j+2)\\geq(3n^2+1)/4\\geq n^2/2.\n\\]\n\nAlso 1+P_j>=j, so this cut term is at most2/n^2. This includes j=0,\nwhere the numerator is zero. Combining the cases gives K_n<=16/n^2.\nBecause U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, the ratio is at most16(1+4n^p)/n^2 and tends to0.\nAll estimates are analytic and valid for every integer n>=2.\n\nThe schedules ell^(n) are only counterexamples to the strength of K_n. No\nsmall upper bound on their actual W_n is asserted; they may be very unstable.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_one_cut_limit.md", "predecessor_fact": "0be8b54e0a33381c", "status": "selected initial-wave verification candidate", "mechanism": "exact single-cut asymmetric-Huber witness and scoped impossibility"}, "glossary": {"K_n": "The explicit maximum of the global Huber bound and all displayed single-cut terms.", "P_j": "Sum of steps preceding the selected index j.", "T_j": "Sum of steps following the selected index j.", "ell^(n)": "Length-n schedule with t-th entry t+1 for 0<=t<n.", "p": "The exponent log_2 3, used only in the crude exact-recursion estimate.", "C_n": "The primitive mass S_circle(n)+1.", "Omega": "Symmetric primitive mass join Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2.", "c": "Right Huber slope 1/(P+1) in the single-cut construction.", "a": "Left Huber slope magnitude c(b-1)/(2T+1) in the single-cut construction.", "b": "The selected step h_j>1 in the single-cut construction.", "P": "Abbreviation P_j inside the single-cut construction.", "T": "Abbreviation T_j inside the single-cut construction."}}
{"id": "01ba3c4b54116bb5", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:15:11.324175+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Anticorrelated Markov polytope: an explicit legal persistent-error construction for every constant H>=2, together with an exact (2,3) obstruction to the variable-step local update.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED SUPPORT CONSTRUCTION, not a fact. Fix integer n>=1 and H>=2, put r=-1/(H-1). In R^{n+1} choose an orthonormal basis e_0,...,e_n and define v_0=e_0, v_{i+1}=r v_i+sqrt(1-r^2)e_{i+1}. Then ||v_i||=1 and <v_i,v_j>=r^{|i-j|}. Put x_0=e_0, x_i=e_0-H sum_{t<i}v_t, K=conv(0,v_0,...,v_n), and f_K(x)=max_{u in K}(<x,u>-||u||^2/2). This is a concrete finite-dimensional candidate, with 0 a minimizer and initial distance1.\n\nFor completeness, projection characterization establishes global smoothness: the unique maximizer is u=Pi_K(x), by completing the square. The inequalities <x-u,v-u><=0 and <y-v,u-v><=0 for u=Pi_K(x),v=Pi_K(y) imply ||u-v||^2<=<x-y,u-v>, so the projection is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maxima gives 0<=f_K(y)-f_K(x)-<u,y-x><=<v-u,y-x><=||y-x||^2, proving differentiability with gradient u. Convexity follows from the maximum of affine functions of x, and f_K>=0, f_K(0)=0 since 0 belongs to K.\n\nSince Hr/(1-r)=-1, geometric sums give <x_i-v_i,v_j>=0 for j>=i, and <x_i-v_i,v_j>=(H-2)(r^{i-j}-1)<=0 for j<i. Also <x_i-v_i,v_i>=0. These inequalities check every vertex of K, including0, and imply <x_i-v_i,u-v_i><=0 for all u in K. Thus Pi_K(x_i)=v_i, the entire GD recursion is legal, and f_K(x_i)=<x_i,v_i>-||v_i||^2/2=1/2. Its normalized terminal error is exactly1 for every n. Randomly rotating K and x_0 gives a distribution of legal instances with the same error1; the random construction of the covariance is a way to specify the geometry, not an extra lower-bound gain.\n\nThe naive variable-step mutation r_i=-1/(h_i-1), v_{i+1}=r_i v_i+sqrt(1-r_i^2)e_{i+1} for h_i>=2 is NOT valid in general. Take h=(2,3). Then v_0=(1,0),v_1=(-1,0),v_2=(1/2,sqrt(3)/2), x_0=(1,0),x_1=(-1,0),x_2=(2,0). Every local PSD innovation variance is nonnegative, ||v_i||=1, and <x_i,v_i>=1. But <x_2-v_2,v_0-v_2>=3/2>0, so the old supporting vertex invalidates projection. In fact no convex 1-smooth function minimized at0 realizes the data: optimum interpolation forces f_i-f(0)=1/2 for all i, whereas the pair (x_2,v_2),(x_0,v_0) requires f_2-f_0>=<v_0,x_2-x_0>+||v_2-v_0||^2/2=3/2. Thus this specific local Markov rule fails a nonadjacent interpolation test. The calculation is self-contained; the related support-function/Moreau construction was read in arXiv:2609.09152v2 Section3.1 and AppendixA.2.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max_negative_markov_polytope", "status": "constant-step example complete; naive varying-step rule refuted", "evidence_status": "self-contained unverified derivation", "fact_ids": []}, "glossary": {"v_i": "Candidate deterministic gradient vectors whose Gram matrix has the displayed AR(1) covariance.", "K": "Convex hull of0 and v_0,...,v_n.", "f_K": "The explicitly defined conjugate-smoothed support function.", "H": "The constant stepsize, at least2.", "r": "The negative correlation -1/(H-1).", "e_i": "An orthonormal coordinate basis."}}
{"id": "f18bd083739517ba", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:15:54.910220+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Explicit ordered Chebyshev schedules defeat the combined witness class of ALL convex quadratics plus the global and every single-cut asymmetric-Huber bound: J_n(h^(n))/U_n^ZJ tends to0.", "evidence": "# An explicit ordering defeating quadratics plus all single-cut Huber witnesses\n\nStatus: complete candidate derivation, unverified. The initial-wave verification\nallowance is already used by fact b98bb807d514e80d. This strengthening is for\nmain-agent prioritization. Worker: max3.\n\n## Statement and scope\n\nLet K_n(h) be the verified single-cut functional in fact b98bb807d514e80d.\nFor a schedule h define its exact quadratic-class worst case\n\n\\[\nQ_n(h)=\\max_{0\\leq\\lambda\\leq1}\n\\lambda\\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}(1-h_t\\lambda)^2,\n\\qquad J_n(h)=\\max\\{K_n(h),Q_n(h)\\}.\n\\]\n\nHere Q_n is exactly the normalized worst case over convex quadratics in any\nfinite dimension with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L, after scaling\nL=1. Indeed diagonalizing their symmetric Hessian makes the normalized error\na weighted average of the displayed scalar expression, and each scalar value\nis attained by a one-dimensional quadratic. Thus J_n(h)<=W_n(h).\n\nFor every integer n>=3 put theta=pi/(2n+1) and\n\n\\[\na_k=\\frac1{\\sin^2(k\\theta)}\\quad(1\\leq k\\leq n).\n\\]\n\nDefine h^(n) by listing a_k with even indices k in decreasing index order,\nfollowed by a_k with odd indices k in increasing index order. For example the\nindex order at n=7 is (6,4,2,1,3,5,7). This is a fully explicit positive,\nfinite, predetermined schedule. Then\n\n\\[\nQ_n(h^{(n)})=\\frac1{(2n+1)^2},\\qquad\nK_n(h^{(n)})\\leq72\\sin^2\\theta\n\\leq\\frac{72\\pi^2}{(2n+1)^2}.\n\\]\n\nUsing B_n=1/U_n^ZJ<=1+4n^p with p=log_2 3<2 from verified fact\nb98bb807d514e80d, it follows that\n\n\\[\n\\frac{J_n(h^{(n)})}{U_n^{ZJ}}\n\\leq\\frac{72\\pi^2(1+4n^p)}{(2n+1)^2}\\longrightarrow0.\n\\]\n\nThis rules out proving the target by taking only the maximum of all quadratic\nwitnesses, the global Huber witness, and the single-cut witnesses in K_n.\nIt does not give any upper bound on W_n(h^(n)); a nonlinear history-dependent\nwitness could yield a much larger error.\n\n## Exact quadratic calculation\n\nDefine polynomials T_0(x)=1, T_1(x)=x and\nT_{r+1}(x)=2xT_r(x)-T_{r-1}(x) for integers r>=1. The cosine addition formula\nand induction give T_r(cos u)=cos(ru). The recurrence also shows that T_r\nhas degree r and parity r. Write M=2n+1. Then T_M is odd and\nT_M'(0)=M sin(M pi/2)=(-1)^n M, by differentiating the cosine identity\non -1<x<1. Therefore\n\n\\[\nR_n(\\lambda)=\\frac{(-1)^nT_M(\\sqrt\\lambda)}{M\\sqrt\\lambda}\n\\]\n\nextends to a polynomial of degree n with R_n(0)=1. For each k=1,...,n,\nT_M(sin(k theta))=cos(M pi/2-k pi)=0. These give n distinct positive\nroots of R_n, hence\n\n\\[\nR_n(\\lambda)=\\prod_{k=1}^n\\left(1-\\frac\\lambda{\\sin^2(k\\theta)}\\right).\n\\]\n\nFor lambda in [0,1], |T_M(sqrt(lambda))|<=1. It follows that\nlambda R_n(lambda)^2<=1/M^2. At lambda=1, T_M(1)=1 and equality holds.\nPermutation of the step factors leaves the product unchanged, proving Q_n.\n\n## Bounds on neighboring step sizes in the stated ordering\n\nAll a_k are at least1 and decrease with k. The function sin u/u decreases\non 0<u<=pi/2, since its derivative has numerator u cos u-sin u and the\nderivative of that numerator is -u sin u<=0. Hence whenever k+2<=n,\n\n\\[\n\\frac{a_k}{a_{k+2}}\n=\\frac{\\sin^2((k+2)\\theta)}{\\sin^2(k\\theta)}\n\\leq\\left(\\frac{k+2}{k}\\right)^2.\n\\tag{1}\n\\]\n\nThe first even-indexed step and the last odd-indexed step have index at\nleast n-1. Since n>=3 implies (n-1)/(2n+1)>=1/4, both are at most2.\nAlso a_2>=a_1/4 and a_3>=a_1/9, by the same decreasing-sinc argument\n(or the double-angle identity for a_2).\n\n## Every single-cut term remains small\n\nFor a selected position j, write b=h_j, P=P_j, T=T_j as in the verified\ndefinition of K_n. Replace (b-1)_+ by b when an upper estimate is needed.\n\n1. A step before the central largest step a_1 has even index. If it is the\nfirst step, b/(P+1)<=2. Otherwise its preceding step is a_{k+2}; by (1),\nb<=4a_{k+2}<=4P. Thus b/(P+1)<=4 in either case. Its suffix contains a_1,\nso its cut term is at most16/(1+2a_1)<=8/a_1.\n\n2. At a_1 the prefix contains a_2>=a_1/4 and the suffix contains\na_3>=a_1/9. Therefore its cut term is at most\n\n\\[\n\\frac{a_1^2}{(a_1/4)^2(2a_1/9)}=\\frac{72}{a_1}.\n\\]\n\n3. After a_1, the prefix always has P>=a_1. For a step a_k that is not\nthe last step, k is odd and k>=3, and its suffix contains a_{k+2}.\nBy (1), a_{k+2}>=a_k/4. Its cut term is at most\n\n\\[\n\\frac{a_k^2}{a_1^2(2a_k/4)}\n=\\frac{2a_k}{a_1^2}\\leq\\frac2{a_1}.\n\\]\n\nAt the last step b<=2, hence (b-1)^2<=1 and the cut term is at most\n1/a_1^2<=1/a_1.\n\nFinally the global Huber term is at most1/(1+2a_1)<=1/(2a_1).\nThe maximum of these bounds is72/a_1=72 sin^2 theta. Since sin(theta)<=theta,\nthis proves the claimed estimate, entirely analytically for every n>=3.\n\n## Source grounding\n\nThe standard trigonometric definition of T_r is documented at NIST DLMF\n18.5.1, https://dlmf.nist.gov/18.5. The polynomial identities used here are\nderived above from their recurrence, so no external theorem is used as a\nblack-box proof step. Matlas was queried for the weighted Chebyshev product\nidentity and did not return a useful exact hit.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_chebyshev_cut_obstruction.md", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "status": "complete candidate; unverified; initial-wave quota exhausted", "mechanism": "explicit chronological permutation of a stable quadratic polynomial", "excluded_scope": "only max of quadratic and specified single-cut/global Huber witness classes"}, "glossary": {"Q_n": "Exact normalized worst case over convex quadratic objectives: max_{0<=lambda<=1}lambda product_t(1-h_t lambda)^2.", "J_n": "Combined lower-bound functional max(K_n,Q_n), with K_n from verified fact b98bb807d514e80d.", "h^(n)": "Schedule of reciprocal-sine-square Chebyshev steps listed by decreasing even indices followed by increasing odd indices.", "a_k": "Chebyshev step 1/sin^2(k*pi/(2n+1)); in this candidate only.", "theta": "The angle pi/(2n+1).", "T_r": "Chebyshev polynomial defined by T_0=1,T_1=x,T_{r+1}=2xT_r-T_{r-1}.", "R_n": "Degree-n polynomial (-1)^n T_{2n+1}(sqrt(lambda))/((2n+1)sqrt(lambda)).", "M": "Odd integer 2n+1."}}
{"id": "b1615bf75df9b476", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:25:37.232364+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Cross-term fusion screen, beyond the earlier scalar-deficit rule: (i) the rational motif [3/2,3/2] has an exact saturated primitive certificate with nonlocal interpolation multipliers, proving that cross terms can repair scalar deficits; (ii) nevertheless pairing any known ZJ objective block of length n with its reverse cannot improve the primitive DP capacity at length 2n, because S_circle(2n)+1>2S_bullet(n)+1 for n>=1; (iii) an explicit asymmetric two-bridge rule promising a larger three-block capacity fails already on the primitive block [sqrt(2)] by a legal one-dimensional Huber example. These are unverified route-screening findings, not target results.", "evidence": "Notation. Put P_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, with exact DP definitions and normalized worst-case W_n as in fact 0be8b54e0a33381c. Define homogeneous scalar joins\nF(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt(A^2+6AB+B^2))/2,\nG(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2\nfor positive A,B,D. The exact recurrences are\nP_0=B_0=1,\nP_n=max_{k+l+1=n} F(P_k,P_l),\nB_n=max_{k+l+1=n} G(P_k,B_l).\n\n1. A real cross-term repair, with no bridge.\nFor a nonnegative schedule h of length n and sum s, define the intrinsic potential\nV_h(C)=2 sum_{i<n} h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_{i<n}h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-C||g_n||^2.\nThis is the same potential as in candidate 79da55e26c4a7406: the cross terms from the GD displacement cancel exactly.\nLet Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0 for convex 1-smooth functions, with x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i g_i.\nFor h=[3/2,3/2], s=3 and C=s(s+1)=12, the following formal identity holds:\nV_h(12)=(7/2)(-Q_10)+(-Q_01)+14(-Q_21)+(17/2)(-Q_12)+(-Q_20)+(1/2)(-Q_02).\nAll six multipliers are nonnegative. To check it, put u=g_0,v=g_1,w=g_2, x_1=x_0-(3/2)u and x_2=x_1-(3/2)v. Function coefficients become 3(f_0-f_1)+6(f_1-f_2); squared-gradient coefficients become -3||u||^2/4-3||v||^2/4-12||w||^2; and all three cross coefficients cancel. This is precisely V_h(12).\nThus a bridge-free two-block proof with retained history inequalities repairs the deficit of the one-step [3/2] seed. It is a concrete demonstration that the scalar-deficit obstruction does not forbid richer fusion. However its mass is 4, below P_2=F(1,1+sqrt(2)), so this test seed is not an improvement.\n\n2. Exact capacity screen for pairing ZJ objective schedules with their reversals.\nWe prove the scalar inequality\nF(A,F(D,A))>G(A,D) for A,D>0.\nBy homogeneity set A=1 and d=D/A. Let q=F(1,d), z=F(1,q), g=G(1,d). The defining quadratic identities give\nd=q(q-1)/(q+1), q=z(z-1)/(z+1),\nso d=z(z-1)(z^2-2z-1)/[(z+1)(z^2+1)] and z>1+sqrt(2)>2.\nThe larger root g>2 satisfies d=g-4+4/g. Direct expansion yields\n(z-4+4/z)-d=4/[z(z+1)(z^2+1)]>0.\nThe function t maps to t-4+4/t is strictly increasing for t>2, since its derivative is 1-4/t^2>0. Hence z>g and the displayed inequality follows after restoring A.\n\nWe now prove P_{2n}>=B_n for every n>=0, strictly for n>=1. At n=0 both equal 1. For n>=1 choose a maximizing split k+l+1=n for B_n. By induction P_{2l}>=B_l. The primitive DP permits the two joins\nP_{2n}>=F(P_k,F(P_{2l},P_k))\n>=F(P_k,F(B_l,P_k))\n>G(P_k,B_l)=B_n.\nAll indices are valid: the inner length is 2l+k+1 and the outer length is 2l+2k+2=2n. This is a pure algebraic property of the actual DP, not an assertion of unrestricted schedule optimality.\n\nIf h is a ZJ objective schedule of length n and sum S_bullet(n), the concatenated schedule [h,reverse(h)] has length 2n and shifted sum 2S_bullet(n)+1=B_n. Even if a new richer proof established that this concatenation is primitive, its capacity would still be strictly below P_{2n}, so every subsequent ordinary ConPP/ConPD construction using it can replace it by the existing same-length primitive DP block with a greater scalar capacity. Thus this fusion route using only existing ZJ objective blocks cannot furnish the requested strict improvement. General f/g-to-primitive closure was not assumed or claimed.\nA nonbasic f/g seed of length n would need shifted pair mass strictly larger than P_{2n} even to escape this exact capacity screen, and then would still need a valid primitive certificate.\n\n3. A hostile test of a different, direct three-block join.\nChoose exact constants c=1/4 and d=(-5+5sqrt(17))/8-1=(5sqrt(17)-13)/8. Then 0<d<1 and\nc d (3+c+d)=1.\nLet M=3+c+d. A tempting homogeneous triple-primitive rule, on a primitive block a of shifted sum A=1+s(a), is\nJ(a)=[a, 1+cA, a, 1+dA, a].\nIts shifted sum is M A. Because c d M=1, it balances the full-curvature quadratic endpoint product when the seed block has product 1/A; M exceeds the ordinary two-join factor F(1,F(1,1)) (numerically M is about 4.20194, versus about 4.01545). Quadratic/Huber balance alone therefore makes it an attractive but insufficient candidate.\n\nIt is not a universally primitive join. Take the exact primitive one-step block a=[sqrt(2)], write A=1+sqrt(2), and let\nP=2sqrt(2)+1+cA\nbe the sum of all steps before the larger bridge b=1+dA. The tail is the one step sqrt(2). Define the convex 1-smooth function\nf(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1,\nf(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1,\nwith minimizer 0. Its derivative is min(x,1), continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz.\nStart at x_0=P+1=(2+c)A. Every step before b occurs with derivative 1 and the point immediately before b is 1. The bridge sends it to -dA. The final sqrt(2) step is taken in the quadratic region and yields\nx_final=(sqrt(2)-1)dA=d,\nwhich lies in (0,1). At every trajectory point, Q_{i,star}=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2=0: the identity holds in both pieces of this explicit function.\nFor a saturated primitive schedule of shifted sum R=1+s, its potential would require x_0^2>=R^2 x_final^2 whenever the final point is in the quadratic piece and all Q_{i,star}=0. Here R=M A, so it would require\n(2+c)^2 A^2 >= M^2 A^2 d^2.\nBut Md=1/c=4, whereas 2+c=9/4; the inequality is strictly false. Hence the proposed primitive interface is refuted exactly, in dimension one.\nThis hostile test rules out that uniform join rule; it does not exclude a horizon-dependent rule valid only for a different restricted block family. Any such reopening must supply a new certificate and re-test the full smooth-convex class.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_endpoint_cross_term_fusion", "plan_id": "cross_term_fusion_screen", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "screened: concrete repair exists; two proposed routes fail quantitative or feasibility test", "fact_ids": ["0be8b54e0a33381c"], "research_path": "research/max4_cross_term_fusion_screen.md", "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "refuted", "summary": "The direct asymmetric triple join violates the primitive endpoint potential on an exact one-sided Huber trajectory."}}, "glossary": {"P_n": "Shifted exact primitive DP sum S_circle(n)+1.", "B_n": "Exact objective benchmark denominator 2S_bullet(n)+1.", "F": "Homogeneous primitive mass join defined in the proof.", "G": "Homogeneous dominant mass join defined in the proof.", "V_h(C)": "Intrinsic primitive energy defined in the proof.", "Q_ij": "Smooth-convex interpolation expression <=0, defined in the proof.", "J(a)": "Explicit proposed asymmetric three-block join in part 3.", "M": "Proposed shifted-mass factor 3+c+d.", "c": "First bridge scale 1/4 in part 3.", "d": "Second bridge scale (5sqrt(17)-13)/8 in part 3."}}
{"id": "87a12b76b7bec957", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:27:03.462538+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Complex-polynomial transcripts, an explicit legal spectral lens, and a quantitative limit of that lens.\n\nFor each integer n>=1 and each real nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), define Poly_0(z)=1 and Poly_i(z)=product_{t=0}^{i-1}(1-h_t z) for 1<=i<=n. Identify the complex plane with the real Euclidean plane, with inner product <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). For z=alpha+i beta set xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z) for 0<=i<=n, and xi_*=gamma_*=0. Put kappa=alpha-|z|^2 and I_n={*,0,...,n}. For each ordered pair define\nq_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2,\nregarded as the weight on the directed edge j->i. For each directed simple cycle C in I_n define\nArea_C=sum_{j->i in C} Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i),\nEnergy_C=sum_{j->i in C}|xi_i-xi_j|^2.\nDefine D_h to be the set of z satisfying kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C| for every such cycle.\n\n(a) The data (xi_i,gamma_i), including the optimum sample *, are interpolable by a finite everywhere-defined convex differentiable function f:R^2->R with 1-Lipschitz gradient and grad f(0)=0 if and only if z belongs to D_h. For z in D_h let ell_i be the largest q-weight of a directed path i->*, allowing the empty path at *, and put v_i=-ell_i. The maxima exist over simple paths and ell_*=0. There is such an f with f(xi_i)=v_i for every i. These values are simultaneously maximal among all interpolating f satisfying f(0)=0. Its exact GD recursion for h starts at xi_0=1, and its normalized terminal error is\nGap_h(z):=-2ell_n.\nIn particular\n|z|^2|Poly_n(z)|^2 <= Gap_h(z) <= (2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2.\n\n(b) The parameter lens\nLens_n={alpha+i beta: alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))}\nis contained in D_h for every h.\n\n(c) Let T_j be the polynomials defined by T_0(x)=1, T_1(x)=x and T_{j+1}(x)=2xT_j(x)-T_{j-1}(x). For every n>=2 define the positive schedule Chi_n, in any permutation, by the n entries\nsec^2((2k-1)pi/(2(2n+1))), k=1,...,n.\nIts terminal polynomial is (-1)^n T_{2n+1}(sqrt(z))/((2n+1)sqrt(z)), interpreted as a polynomial including z=0, and its sum is s(Chi_n)=2n(n+1)/3. For all z in Lens_n,\nGap_{Chi_n}(z) <= 2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.\n\nFor an arbitrary schedule of length n>=1 define\nV_lens(h)=max{sup_{z in Lens_n} Gap_h(z), 1/(2s(h)+1)}.\nLet W_n(h) be the project worst-case normalized objective error, namely the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite dimension, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and s(h)=sum h_t. Then V_lens(h)<=W_n(h). For the exact benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1) defined by the recursions in predecessor b98bb807d514e80d, and p=log_2 3 from that predecessor, the explicit schedules satisfy\n0<=V_lens(Chi_n)/U_n^ZJ\n <= 2cosh^2(2)(1+4n^p)/n^2 ->0.\nThus the universal-lens witnesses, even augmented by the canonical sum-based Huber witness, cannot prove the required uniform matching lower bound. This is a limitation of that witness family only, not an upper bound on W_n(Chi_n) and not a decision of the unrestricted minimax target.", "evidence": "1. Necessary smooth-convex inequality.\nFor any convex differentiable 1-smooth f and any x,y, put k(u)=f(u)-<grad f(y),u>. Convexity and grad k(y)=0 imply k(y)<=k(u). Integrating the Lipschitz gradient along a segment gives k(u+d)<=k(u)+<grad k(u),d>+||d||^2/2. Take d=-grad k(u) and combine these inequalities to obtain\nf(x)>=f(y)+<grad f(y),x-y>+||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2/2. (1)\nApplied to any proposed interpolant, (1) says its values obey f_i-f_j>=q_ij.\n\n2. Cycle identity and necessity.\nFor complex u,v, <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). Expand\n<z xi_j,xi_i-xi_j>\n=alpha Re(conj(xi_j)(xi_i-xi_j))+beta Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i).\nThe real part is (|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2-|xi_i-xi_j|^2)/2. Also\n||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2=|z|^2|xi_i-xi_j|^2.\nTherefore\nq_ij=(alpha/2)(|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2)+beta Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i)\n      -(kappa/2)|xi_i-xi_j|^2. (2)\nThe norm-difference terms telescope on a cycle, whose weight is beta Area_C-kappa Energy_C/2. Both orientations are available, and their areas have opposite signs but equal energies. Consequently nonpositivity of every cycle is equivalent to the definition of D_h. Summing (1) on cycles proves the necessary implication in (a). The cycle *->0->* also forces kappa>=0.\n\n3. Values from paths.\nAssume z is in D_h. Every closed walk is a sum of simple directed cycles, after splitting at repeated vertices; thus every closed walk has nonpositive weight. Any path to * can have repeated vertices deleted, and deleting the intervening nonpositive cycle cannot reduce the weight. There are finitely many simple paths on I_n, so ell_i is finite and is a maximum over them. At * the empty path has weight zero and every closed walk has weight at most zero, so ell_*=0.\nConcatenate edge j->i with a maximum path i->*, then delete cycles if needed. It follows that ell_j>=q_ij+ell_i. Hence v_i=-ell_i satisfies v_i-v_j>=q_ij. For any other feasible values F_i with F_*=0, summing the inequalities along a path i->* yields -F_i at least the weight of that path. Thus F_i<=-ell_i=v_i for every i.\n\n4. A finite-dimensional global interpolant.\nDefine on the real plane\nH(g)=max_{i in I_n}{<gamma_i,xi_i>-v_i+<xi_i,g-gamma_i>+||g-gamma_i||^2/2}.\nThis is the maximum of finitely many quadratics with Hessian equal to the identity. It is finite, continuous and 1-strongly convex. The inequality v_j-v_i>=<gamma_i,xi_j-xi_i>+||gamma_j-gamma_i||^2/2 is exactly the assertion that at g=gamma_i the j-th quadratic is at most the i-th quadratic. Thus\nH(gamma_i)=<gamma_i,xi_i>-v_i.\nThe i-th quadratic is active and has derivative xi_i at gamma_i. Its supporting affine function is below H, so xi_i belongs to the subdifferential of H at gamma_i.\n\nDefine f(x)=sup_g{<x,g>-H(g)}. For each fixed x, the maximizer g(x) exists and is unique: H(g)-<x,g> is continuous, strongly convex, and coercive because even any one term of the defining maximum has leading term ||g||^2/2. Optimality says x belongs to the subdifferential of H at g(x). Strong convexity implies that for all x,y,\n<x-y,g(x)-g(y)>>=||g(x)-g(y)||^2.\nTo justify this implication, subtract ||g||^2/2 from H, write the two supporting-subgradient inequalities at g(x),g(y), and add them. Cauchy--Schwarz gives ||g(x)-g(y)||<=||x-y||.\nThe definition of f yields, for every displacement d,\n<g(x),d><=f(x+d)-f(x)<=<g(x+d),d>.\nIt follows that the difference between f(x+d)-f(x) and <g(x),d> lies in [0,||d||^2]. Therefore f is differentiable with gradient g(x), and that gradient is 1-Lipschitz. It is finite by existence of the maximizers and convex as a supremum of affine functions. The subgradient condition xi_i in partial H(gamma_i) gives g(xi_i)=gamma_i and f(xi_i)=v_i. At the optimum sample this gives f(0)=0 and grad f(0)=0, so convexity makes 0 a minimizer.\n\nThe prefix-polynomial identity Poly_{i+1}=(1-h_i z)Poly_i gives xi_{i+1}=xi_i-h_i gamma_i. Thus the interpolation is a legal GD trajectory, with ||xi_0||=1. Apply (1) in both directions between n and *:\nv_n>=||gamma_n||^2/2,\nv_n<=<gamma_n,xi_n>-||gamma_n||^2/2.\nSince <gamma_n,xi_n>=alpha|Poly_n(z)|^2, these are the two bounds in (a).\n\n5. A discrete area-energy inequality.\nConsider any r>=2 complex numbers w_0,...,w_{r-1}, with w_r=w_0. Put\nd_k=(1/r)sum_{j=0}^{r-1}w_j exp(-2pi i k j/r), 0<=k<r.\nThe finite geometric-sum identity reconstructs w_j=sum_k d_k exp(2pi i k j/r). The same identity, on expanding the quadratic sums, gives\nsum_j Im(conj(w_j)w_{j+1})=r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin(2pi k/r),\nsum_j |w_{j+1}-w_j|^2=4r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin^2(pi k/r).\nThe k=0 terms vanish. For 1<=k<r, the ratio of the absolute sine coefficient in the first sum to the coefficient in the second is (1/2)|cot(pi k/r)|<=cot(pi/r)/2. The triangle inequality proves\n|sum_j Im(conj(w_j)w_{j+1})| <= (cot(pi/r)/2)sum_j |w_{j+1}-w_j|^2. (3)\nThis also covers r=2, where the left side and cot(pi/2) are zero.\nEvery simple cycle on I_n has length r<=n+2. The function cot(pi/r) increases as the integer r>=2 increases. From (3) and the defining inequality for Lens_n one gets kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C|. This proves (b).\n\n6. Explicit real positive Chebyshev steps and their sum.\nThe recurrence for T_j, the cosine addition formula, and induction give T_j(cos t)=cos(jt). Its parity is the parity of j and its degree is j. Put m=2n+1. The odd polynomial T_m(x) is divisible by x; hence (-1)^n T_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z)) is a real polynomial of degree n. Differentiating T_m(cos t)=cos(mt) at t=pi/2 gives T_m'(0)=(-1)^n m, so its value at z=0 is 1.\nThe positive roots of T_m are cos((2k-1)pi/(2m)), k=1,...,n. Squaring them gives exactly the distinct roots lambda_k=cos^2((2k-1)pi/(2m)) in (0,1) of the degree-n polynomial. It follows from the value at zero that the polynomial equals product_k(1-z/lambda_k). Thus its reciprocal roots are precisely the stated positive schedule, regardless of their order.\n\nTwice differentiating the cosine identity and eliminating the angle gives the polynomial identity\n(1-x^2)T_m''(x)-xT_m'(x)+m^2 T_m(x)=0.\nDifferentiation at x=0 gives T_m'''(0)=-(m^2-1)T_m'(0). The coefficient of z in (-1)^n T_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z)) is consequently -(m^2-1)/6. The coefficient of z in the product of schedule factors is minus their sum. Hence s(Chi_n)=(m^2-1)/6=2n(n+1)/3.\n\n7. The lens lies in an ellipse of small width.\nLet n>=2, N=n+2, theta=pi/N, t=tan(theta)>0, and u=asinh(t/2)>0. For z=alpha+i beta in Lens_n,\n0<=alpha<=1 and |beta|<=t alpha(1-alpha),\nbecause alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=0 and dropping beta^2 only weakens the defining inequality. Put s=alpha(1-alpha), so 0<=s<=1/4. Since sinh(u)=t/2 and cosh(u)>=1,\n(2alpha-1)^2/cosh^2(u)+(2beta)^2/sinh^2(u)\n <=1-4s+16s^2<=1. (4)\n\nFor completeness (4) implies there is a complex w with |Im w|<=u and z=(1+cos w)/2. If beta=0, take a real w with cos w=2alpha-1. If beta!=0, the continuous function\nv -> (2alpha-1)^2/cosh^2(v)+(2beta)^2/sinh^2(v)\ndecreases from infinity as v increases from zero and is at most 1 at v=u. Choose v in (0,u] where its value is 1, and choose a real angle a with\ncos a=(2alpha-1)/cosh(v), sin a=-2beta/sinh(v).\nThen w=a+i v has cos w=2alpha-1+2i beta.\nA square root of z is cos(w/2). The cosine identity for T_m holds for complex arguments as well, because it follows from polynomial recurrence and the complex cosine addition formula. Thus\n|z| |Poly_n(z)|^2\n =|T_m(sqrt(z))|^2/m^2\n =|cos(mw/2)|^2/m^2\n <=cosh^2(m u/2)/m^2. (5)\nThe polynomial interpretation covers z=0 as well; both sides of the equality before the bound are zero there.\n\nThe inequality asinh(t/2)<=t/2 follows by integrating its derivative, which is at most 1 on the nonnegative line. The function tan is convex on [0,pi/4], so it lies below its chord and tan(theta)<=4theta/pi=4/N. Therefore m u/2<=m t/4<=m/N<2. Combining (5) with (a) and alpha<=|z| proves\nGap_{Chi_n}(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2\n <=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2<=2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.\n\n8. Canonical Huber witness and the exact-profile comparison.\nFor any h>=0 let S=s(h) and delta=1/(2S+1). In dimension one define\nf_delta(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=delta, and delta|x|-delta^2/2 otherwise.\nThe matching derivative is x clipped to [-delta,delta], hence is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. The function is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth and minimized at 0. Starting from x_0=1, every partial sum of h lies in [0,S]. Induction gives x_i=1-delta sum_{t<i}h_t>=1-delta S=(S+1)/(2S+1)>=delta. Thus every gradient equals delta and\n2f_delta(x_n)=2delta(1-delta S)-delta^2=1/(2S+1).\nMultiplying either this function or the interpolant from part 4 by L produces the same normalized error for the specified arbitrary L>0. Consequently each term defining V_lens(h) is a legal lower-bound witness for W_n(h), proving V_lens(h)<=W_n(h).\n\nFor Chi_n the Huber term is\n1/(1+4n(n+1)/3)<=3/(4n^2).\nPart 7 bounds its lens term by 2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2. Since 2cosh^2(2)>=2>3/4, both terms are at most 2cosh^2(2)/n^2.\nVerified predecessor b98bb807d514e80d states, for exactly the project phi/psi recursions and the project normalization, that B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1<=1+4n^p with p=log_2 3<2. Multiply the preceding bound on V_lens by B_n to obtain the displayed ratio estimate. It tends to zero because n^{-2}->0 and n^{p-2}->0. This uses no assumed limit for S_bullet(n)/n^rho and no numerical estimate of the profile constant.\n\nOnly the explicitly restricted witness family was upper bounded. None of the calculations gives an upper bound for the unrestricted W_n on the displayed schedules.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_lens_selected_candidate.md", "predecessors": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "mechanism": "exact complex-polynomial interpolation plus universal-lens obstruction", "status": "selected initial-wave package; pending verification", "scope": "specific realization bridge and witness-class limitation; not full target"}, "glossary": {"Poly_i": "Ordered prefix polynomial product_{t<i}(1-h_t z), with Poly_0=1.", "xi_i": "Complex prefix point Poly_i(z), identified with a vector of R^2; xi_*=0.", "gamma_i": "Complex gradient z Poly_i(z), identified with a vector of R^2; gamma_*=0.", "I_n": "Finite interpolation index set {*,0,...,n}.", "q_ij": "Smooth interpolation lower-increment edge weight on directed edge j->i.", "Area_C": "Signed doubled polygon area sum Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i) over a directed cycle.", "Energy_C": "Sum of squared edge lengths of that directed cycle.", "D_h": "Set of complex parameters satisfying every stated smooth-convex interpolation cycle.", "ell_i": "Maximum q-weight over paths i->*, with empty optimum path allowed.", "v_i": "Maximal interpolating function value -ell_i with optimum value zero.", "Gap_h": "The maximal normalized terminal error -2ell_n for the given complex-polynomial transcript.", "Lens_n": "Explicit universal legal lens alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2)).", "Chi_n": "Any ordering of the n positive reciprocal squared Chebyshev roots specified in the statement.", "V_lens": "Maximum of the universal complex-lens witness supremum and the canonical sum-based Huber witness.", "T_j": "Chebyshev polynomials T_0=1, T_1=x, T_{j+1}=2xT_j-T_{j-1}; local notation in this fact.", "H": "The maximum of shifted quadratic functions whose convex conjugate constructs the interpolant."}}
{"id": "eb2eb63228f7d9a4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:28:03.508731+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "P4 screening: exact two-state matrix payoff for random two-curvature quadratics; fixed-one-curvature and fixed finite curvature-support mechanisms fail, while the continuous-profile comparison remains BLOCKED.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED EXACT REALIZATION AND PAYOFF. For a,b in(0,1], let J_{a,b}(x)=a x^2/2 on x>=0 and b x^2/2 on x<=0. Its derivative is a x or b x on the two half-lines, agrees at0, is nondecreasing, and is1-Lipschitz (for x<=0<=y, a y-b x<=y-x). It is convex and differentiable, with minimizer0. Start at x0=+1 or-1 with probability1/2, so the initial squared distance is1 in every sample.\n\nDefine a nonnegative two-by-two matrix\nN_h(a,b)=[[(1-ha)_+^2,(hb-1)_+^2],[(ha-1)_+^2,(1-hb)_+^2]].\nIf a column represents the expected squared magnitude currently on its indicated sign, multiplying by this matrix gives the next signed squared-magnitude masses. This holds because the step is x -> (1-ha)x on the positive half-line and x -> (1-hb)x on the negative half-line. A zero iterate contributes zero mass, so the sign convention at0 is irrelevant.\nThus for every predetermined nonnegative schedule h the exact expected normalized objective under this initial-sign law is\nP_h(a,b)=(1/2)[a,b] N_{h_{n-1}}(a,b)...N_{h_0}(a,b)[1,1]^T.\nFor any Borel probability law nu_h on(0,1]^2, selected after the complete schedule is known, the joint law on (a,b,initial sign) consists only of legal normalized instances and yields\nW_n(h)>=integral P_h(a,b)dnu_h(a,b).\nThe integrand is bounded for fixed finite h, since each normalized error is at most product_i(1+h_i)^2. No infimum is moved through expectation. The matrices retain order and state-dependent curvature; at a=b they reduce to the usual quadratic polynomial model.\n\nDirect attack1 refutes the fixed-a=1 subclass as a universal hard family. For h=(1,H,1), H>=1, the positive initial point is killed at the first step. From x0=-1 one has x1=-(1-b). If Hb>=1, the second step reaches the nonnegative half-line and the final unit step kills it. If Hb<=1, x3=-(1-Hb)(1-b)^2. Therefore\nP_{(1,H,1)}(1,b)=(b/2)(1-Hb)_+^2(1-b)^4<=2/(27H),\nbecause max_{0<=t<=1}t(1-t)^2=4/27. The supremum over either deterministic initial sign is at most4/(27H). Every distribution over b has the same upper cap and it tends to0 with H at horizon3.\n\nDirect attack2: let Lambda be any fixed finite subset of(0,1]. List its reciprocals in nondecreasing order and use them as the steps. Every objective J_{a,b} with a,b in Lambda is solved exactly from either initial sign. Before the first step equal to the reciprocal of the starting-side curvature, all steps are smaller, so the iterate retains its sign. At that reciprocal step it reaches0 and remains there. Appending zero steps gives exact solution for all larger horizons. Thus a fixed finite support of curvatures cannot furnish the sought all-schedule lower bound.\n\nNeither attack settles the genuinely continuous two-curvature class. The unresolved statement would be: for every epsilon>0 there exists N_epsilon independent of h such that for every n>=N_epsilon and h in[0,infinity)^n there is a probability nu_h on(0,1]^2 with integral P_h dnu_h >=(1-epsilon)U_n^ZJ. Equivalently replace the integral by sup_{a,b} P_h(a,b), since point masses are allowed (no minimax interchange). This is a restricted-family theorem of the full desired lower-bound strength and is currently BLOCKED; the explicit matrix formula alone is not a bridge proving it.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max_two_curvature_matrix", "status": "support construction complete; main comparison BLOCKED", "attempt_type": "direct", "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "fixed-curvature subclasses refuted; full continuous class not_refuted"}, "key_stuck_points": ["No inequality comparing sup_{a,b} P_h(a,b) to U_n^ZJ for arbitrary h."], "evidence_status": "unverified; no further first-wave verification requested"}, "glossary": {"J_{a,b}": "Piecewise quadratic with curvatures a on the positive half-line and b on the negative half-line.", "N_h(a,b)": "Displayed nonnegative2x2 transition matrix for squared magnitudes on the two signs.", "P_h(a,b)": "Exact expected normalized objective under equal random initial signs.", "nu_h": "Any schedule-dependent probability law on the curvature pair(a,b).", "Lambda": "A fixed finite curvature set in the annihilation counterexample."}}
{"id": "c9876caabd790d77", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:28:25.542754+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C6 complete legal multi-cut candidate: one-dimensional alternating outward ray extensions admit an exact affine transfer with remote offsets and a retained-area objective; h=(1,5,1,9,1) gives77/4 while K=1/3.", "evidence": "# A legal multi-cut construction with exact remote-history transfer\n\nStatus: complete candidate derivation, unverified. No second initial-wave\nverification submission is made. Worker: max3.\n\nThis is an actual one-dimensional convex 1-smooth function construction,\nnot a free choice of curvature at each iterate and not a PEP reformulation.\nIt keeps remote ray offsets and function-value areas. It gives a lower-bound\nfunctional stronger than the verified single-cut functional K_n, but no\nuniform comparison to U_n^ZJ has been proved.\n\n## Inputs, feasibility, and quantitative output\n\nTake any finite nonnegative schedule and select k checkpoint steps. In\nchronological order write the schedule as\n\n\\[\n(\\ell_0,b_0,\\ell_1,b_1,\\ldots,b_{k-1},\\ell_k),\n\\]\n\nwhere each ell_i is a possibly empty list of nonnegative ordinary steps,\ns_i is its sum, and b_i is a nonnegative checkpoint step. This preserves\nthe individual steps and their order; their sums suffice only because the\nconstructed derivative will be constant during each ordinary block.\n\nFor k>=1 define nonterminal gradient magnitudes v_0,...,v_{k-1} and\nnonnegative offset sums O_0,...,O_k as follows:\n\n\\[\nv_0=\\frac1{1+s_0},\\qquad O_0=0,\n\\]\n\nand, for i=0,...,k-2,\n\n\\[\nv_{i+1}=\\frac{(b_i-1)v_i-O_i}{1+s_{i+1}},\\qquad\nO_{i+1}=O_i+s_i v_i.\n\\tag{1}\n\\]\n\nAlso set O_k=O_{k-1}+s_{k-1}v_{k-1}; its last value is only bookkeeping.\nRequire\n\n\\[\nv_i>0\\quad(0\\leq i<k),\\qquad\nv_i\\geq v_{i-2}\\quad(2\\leq i<k).\n\\tag{2}\n\\]\n\nDefine the terminal distance budget and earlier terminal-ray slope by\n\n\\[\nZ=(b_{k-1}-1)v_{k-1}-O_{k-1},\\qquad\nv_{\\rm old}=\\begin{cases}0,&k=1,\\\\v_{k-2},&k\\geq2.\\end{cases}\n\\]\n\nThe terminal feasibility requirement is\n\n\\[\nZ\\geq(1+s_k)v_{\\rm old}.\n\\tag{3}\n\\]\n\nChoose any final slope magnitude\n\n\\[\nq\\in\\left[v_{\\rm old},\\frac{Z}{1+s_k}\\right].\n\\tag{4}\n\\]\n\nPut E=sum of s_i v_i^2 over 0<=i<k with i congruent to k modulo2.\nThere is a convex differentiable 1-smooth f:R->R with minimizer0 and\nf(0)=0 such that GD from x_0=1 with the exact supplied schedule has\n\n\\[\n2f(x_n)=2E+2qZ-(2s_k+1)q^2.\n\\tag{5}\n\\]\n\nThus this expression is a legal lower bound on W_n(h). Scaling f by L\npreserves the normalized statement at any L>0. The best q within (4) is\n\n\\[\nq_{\\rm opt}=\\max\\left\\{v_{\\rm old},\\frac{Z}{2s_k+1}\\right\\}.\n\\tag{6}\n\\]\n\nIndeed the quadratic in (5) is concave, its unconstrained maximum is at\nZ/(2s_k+1), and this point is no larger than the upper endpoint in (4).\n\nFor k=0 take Z=1, E=0, v_old=0 and s_k=s_0. Equations (4)--(6) then\ngive the usual exact Huber value 1/(1+2s_0). For k=1 and b_0>=1, the\nterminal old slope is0, and (5)--(6) give exactly\n(b_0-1)^2/[(1+s_0)^2(1+2s_1)]. Therefore the maximum over all feasible\nselected chains includes every term of K_n in fact b98bb807d514e80d.\n\n## Explicit construction on the two rays\n\nWrite a signed point as x=(-1)^i r at stage i, where r>=0 is its distance\nfrom0. On each ray construct a radial derivative magnitude Q_+(r) or Q_-(r),\nstarting at Q_+(0)=Q_-(0)=0. Let A_+ and A_- denote the largest distances\npreviously visited on those rays, and let w_+,w_- be their derivative\nmagnitudes. Initially all four numbers are0.\n\nAt a nonterminal stage i the starting distance is a_i. On its active ray\nwrite A_old,w_old for the previous record and slope and d_i=A_old-w_old.\nChoose the magnitude v_i and extend that ray's derivative by a slope-1 ramp\nfrom (A_old,w_old) to\n\n\\[\n(r_i,v_i),\\qquad r_i=A_{\\rm old}+v_i-w_{\\rm old}=d_i+v_i,\n\\]\n\nfollowed by a constant segment of value v_i up to a_i. Conditions (2)\ngive v_i>=w_old; for the first visit to a ray its old slope is0. The\nrecurrence will give\n\n\\[\na_i=d_i+(1+s_i)v_i=r_i+s_i v_i,\n\\tag{7}\n\\]\n\nso a_i>=r_i>=A_old. This extension does not change any previously visited\ndata. During ell_i every iterate remains on this flat segment, since its\npartial step sums lie between0 and s_i. Thus the stage ends at signed\ndistance r_i with gradient sign (-1)^i and magnitude v_i.\n\nAt the checkpoint b_i the next signed point has opposite sign and distance\n\n\\[\na_{i+1}=b_i v_i-r_i=(b_i-1)v_i-d_i.\n\\tag{8}\n\\]\n\nThe previously unvisited amount will be nonnegative by the next-stage\nfeasibility condition. The active ray's new outer record is a_i and its\nslope there is v_i. Hence its offset on its next visit is\n\n\\[\nd_{i+2}=a_i-v_i=d_i+s_i v_i.\n\\tag{9}\n\\]\n\nInitially d_0=d_1=0. Write O_i=d_i+d_{i+1}. Equations (8)--(9) imply\nO_{i+1}=O_i+s_i v_i and\na_{i+1}-d_{i+1}=(b_i-1)v_i-O_i. Equating this last quantity with\n(1+s_{i+1})v_{i+1} gives exactly (1). Also a_0=1 and d_0=0 imply the\nstated v_0, completing the induction that verifies (7) for each nonterminal\nstage. Positive v_i means nonterminal stages do not reach0. The terminal\ncase Z=0 is permitted only with zero old slope and causes no difficulty.\n\nAt terminal stage k, the starting distance satisfies a_k-d_k=Z. Extend\nits ray by a slope-1 ramp from its old record to slope q and then by a flat\nsegment to a_k. Condition (4) makes the ramp endpoint d_k+q no smaller\nthan the old record and no larger than a_k-s_k q. Thus every final-block\niterate stays on its flat segment. The final distance is a_k-s_k q.\n\nFor k=0 the same construction begins directly from the initial origin datum,\nwith a_0=1 and q given by (4).\n\nAfter the last stage extend each radial derivative constantly to infinity.\nDefine the global derivative by f'(x)=Q_+(x) for x>=0 and\nf'(x)=-Q_-(-x) for x<=0, and define f(x)=integral_0^x f'(t)dt.\nEach radial derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, with\nQ_+(0)=Q_-(0)=0. Across0, for x<=0<=y,\n\n\\[\n0\\leq f'(y)-f'(x)=Q_+(y)+Q_-(-x)\\leq y-x.\n\\]\n\nConsequently f' is globally continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz.\nThis proves differentiability, convexity, 1-smoothness, and a minimizer at0.\nThe earlier stage calculations prove the exact original GD recursion.\nIn particular no interpolation or dimensional-realizability gap remains\nwithin the candidate construction.\n\n## Retained function values give the exact objective\n\nOn one ray, a ramp from magnitude w to v adds area (v^2-w^2)/2. Its\nfollowing nonterminal flat segment has length s_i v_i and adds s_i v_i^2.\nTherefore at the outer record after any nonterminal stage i, the radial\nfunction value equals\n\n\\[\nv_i^2/2+\\sum_{j\\leq i,\\ j\\equiv i\\ ({\\rm mod}\\ 2)}s_jv_j^2.\n\\tag{10}\n\\]\n\nThis identity follows by induction on successive visits to that ray: the\nsquared-gradient contributions telescope and the flat-segment areas remain.\nAt the final stage the new ramp endpoint has value E+q^2/2. The distance\nfrom that endpoint to the final point is Z-(s_k+1)q, on which the derivative\nequals q. The final value is consequently\n\n\\[\nE+q^2/2+q[Z-(s_k+1)q]\n=E+qZ-(s_k+1/2)q^2,\n\\]\n\nproving (5). The initial distance is exactly1, so the normalization is exact.\n\n## Exact two-cut illustration\n\nFor h=(1,5,1,9,1) select checkpoint steps5 and9. Then s_0=s_1=s_2=1,\nv_0=1/2, O_0=0, v_1=1, O_1=1/2, Z=15/2, v_old=1/2,\nE=1/4, and q_opt=5/2. All feasibility conditions hold. Formula (5) gives\n\n\\[\nW_5(1,5,1,9,1)\\geq2(1/4)+(15/2)^2/3=77/4.\n\\]\n\nThe actual points are 1,1/2,-2,-1,8,11/2.
