On the positive ray the\nderivative is r on [0,1/2], 1/2 on [1/2,1], r-1/2 on [1,3], and5/2\non [3,infinity). On the negative ray its magnitude is min(r,1).\nDirect integration gives f(11/2)=77/8, consistent with the formula.\nBy contrast the exact K_5 value is max{1/35,4/23,1/3}=1/3.\n\n## Interface still missing for the project target\n\nThe construction works for each chain satisfying (2)--(4), and these\nconditions can be checked from the selected block sums and step values.\nIt does not establish the existence of a sufficiently strong feasible chain\nfor every schedule. The assertion that maximizing (5) over feasible chains\nalways gives (1-o(1))U_n^ZJ would be a new theorem-strength obligation.\nNo such assertion is assumed. The terms -O_i and E are genuine retained\nhistory contributions; discarding them changes the construction and is not\na permitted constant-level approximation without a separate proof.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_alternating_ray_transfer.md", "mechanism": "exact multi-cut outward-ray construction with remote offset and area state", "status": "complete candidate; unverified; initial-wave quota exhausted", "target_gap": "No uniform strong-chain selection/comparison theorem has been established."}, "glossary": {"ell_i": "Chronological block of ordinary nonnegative steps between selected checkpoints.", "s_i": "Sum of ordinary steps in block ell_i.", "b_i": "Checkpoint following block ell_i.", "v_i": "Positive gradient magnitude on nonterminal stage i.", "O_i": "Sum d_i+d_{i+1} of the two ray offsets before stage i.", "d_i": "Previously visited outer distance minus its derivative magnitude on the active ray.", "a_i": "Distance of the starting point of stage i from the minimizer0.", "r_i": "End of the new unit-slope ramp on the active ray.", "Z": "Terminal available distance a_k-d_k.", "v_old": "Derivative magnitude on the terminal ray at its last previous outer record, or0 if unvisited.", "q": "Final-stage gradient magnitude, chosen in [v_old,Z/(1+s_k)].", "E": "Sum of the retained areas s_i v_i^2 on prior visits to the terminal ray, local to this construction.", "Q_+": "Constructed radial derivative magnitude on the nonnegative ray.", "Q_-": "Constructed radial derivative magnitude on the nonpositive ray."}}
{"id": "77c6b8fde5e37d07", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:31:00.740682+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Exact local ConPD minimax identity from verified legal witnesses: for fixed prefix and suffix sums x,y>=0, balancing the global Huber and selected-cut lower bounds yields exactly the Zhang–Jiang psi join.", "evidence": "Use fact b98bb807d514e80d for the two legal bounds. Fix x,y>=0, set A=x+1>=1 and D=2y+1>=1, and vary the joining step b>=0. Define M(b)=max{1/[2(x+b+y)+1],(b-1)_+^2/[A^2 D]}. The first term strictly decreases in b; the second is zero up to1 and strictly increases afterward, so the unique minimizer occurs at their crossing b>1. Write t=b-1>0. The equality is t^2(2A+D+2t)=A^2D, whose left-minus-right factors as (t+A)(2t^2+Dt-AD). Since t+A>0, the unique root is t=[sqrt(D^2+8AD)-D]/4. Consequently b=1+t=psi(x,y), with psi exactly as in predecessor facts. Also A=t(2t+D)/D and 2A+D+2t=(2t+D)^2/D, so the minimum is 1/[2(x+psi(x,y)+y)+1]. This is an exact local minimax identity for every real nonnegative x,y, independent of chronology inside the two fixed-sum blocks. It does NOT imply arbitrary prefixes satisfy the primitive sum caps S_circle(k), or that a recursive decomposition loses no information. That remaining interface has to be established by a new legal state argument; otherwise it is theorem-strength and BLOCKED. This candidate is not additionally submitted under the initial-wave cap.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "mechanism": "exact local balancing of two legal hard functions", "status": "local identity candidate; no universal split theorem"}, "glossary": {"M(b)": "Maximum of the two verified legal lower bounds as a function of the joining step b.", "A": "Shifted prefix sum x+1 in this local identity.", "D": "Shifted suffix mass 2y+1 in this local identity.", "t": "Joining step minus1, t=b-1."}}
{"id": "1ba418a0f41e7ef5", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:34:25.023545+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Exact alternating-ray multi-cut hard functions with retained remote offsets and function-value areas.\n\nFix any integer n>=0, L>0, and any predetermined finite nonnegative schedule h of length n for gradient descent x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)f'(x_t). Write W_n(h) for the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star.\n\nChoose k checkpoint steps, where 0<=k<=n, and write h in its exact chronological order as (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,b_{k-1},block_k). Each block_i is a possibly empty list of nonnegative steps; s_i is its sum. Each b_i>=0 is the intervening checkpoint.\n\nFor k>=1 set m_0=1/(1+s_0) and O_0=0. For i=0,...,k-2 define\nm_{i+1}=((b_i-1)m_i-O_i)/(1+s_{i+1}), O_{i+1}=O_i+s_i m_i.\nRequire m_i>0 for 0<=i<k and m_i>=m_{i-2} for 2<=i<k.\nDefine z_term=(b_{k-1}-1)m_{k-1}-O_{k-1}, and define m_old=0 if k=1 and m_old=m_{k-2} if k>=2. Require z_term>=(1+s_k)m_old.\nDefine e_term=sum_{0<=i<k, i congruent k mod 2}s_i m_i^2.\nChoose any q in the closed interval [m_old,z_term/(1+s_k)].\n\nThen an explicitly constructed one-dimensional convex differentiable function f with L-Lipschitz derivative, f(0)=0=min f, and initial point x_0=1 realizes the full supplied GD schedule and has exactly\n2 f(x_n)/L = 2e_term+2q z_term-(2s_k+1)q^2.\nConsequently this expression is a lower bound for W_n(h). The largest value within the stated q interval is attained at\nq_opt=max{m_old,z_term/(2s_k+1)}.\n\nFor k=0, use z_term=1, e_term=0, m_old=0, s_k=s_0 and q in [0,1/(1+s_0)], without defining any m_i or O_i. The same construction and payoff formula apply and their maximum is 1/(1+2s_0). The empty horizon n=0 is included.\nFor k=1 the feasibility condition is exactly b_0>=1, and the optimized payoff is (b_0-1)^2/[(1+s_0)^2(1+2s_1)].\n\nDefine F_ray_n(h) as the maximum of the optimized displayed payoff over all feasible checkpoint choices, including k=0. This is a finite maximum because h is finite. Then\nK_n(h)<=F_ray_n(h)<=W_n(h),\nwhere K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum_t h_t), max_{0<=j<n}(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_{t<j}h_t)^2(1+2sum_{t>j}h_t)]} is the lower-bound functional established in predecessor b98bb807d514e80d, with its inner maximum omitted at n=0 and (u)_+=max(u,0).\nFor h=(1,5,1,9,1), the checkpoint choice (5,9) has exact optimized payoff77/4, whereas K_5(h)=1/3.\nNo uniform comparison between F_ray_n and the Zhang–Jiang profile is asserted.", "evidence": "## Explicit construction on the two rays\n\nWrite a signed point as x=(-1)^i r at stage i, where r>=0 is its distance\nfrom0. On each ray construct a radial derivative magnitude Q_+(r) or Q_-(r),\nstarting at Q_+(0)=Q_-(0)=0. Let A_+ and A_- denote the largest distances\npreviously visited on those rays, and let w_+,w_- be their derivative\nmagnitudes. Initially all four numbers are0.\n\nAt a nonterminal stage i the starting distance is a_i. On its active ray\nwrite A_old,w_old for the previous record and slope and d_i=A_old-w_old.\nChoose the magnitude m_i and extend that ray's derivative by a slope-1 ramp\nfrom (A_old,w_old) to\n\n\\[\n(r_i,m_i),\\qquad r_i=A_{\\rm old}+m_i-w_{\\rm old}=d_i+m_i,\n\\]\n\nfollowed by a constant segment of value m_i up to a_i. Conditions (2)\ngive m_i>=w_old; for the first visit to a ray its old slope is0. The\nrecurrence will give\n\n\\[\na_i=d_i+(1+s_i)m_i=r_i+s_i m_i,\n\\tag{7}\n\\]\n\nso a_i>=r_i>=A_old. This extension does not change any previously visited\ndata. During block_i every iterate remains on this flat segment, since its\npartial step sums lie between0 and s_i. Thus the stage ends at signed\ndistance r_i with gradient sign (-1)^i and magnitude m_i.\n\nAt the checkpoint b_i the next signed point has opposite sign and distance\n\n\\[\na_{i+1}=b_i m_i-r_i=(b_i-1)m_i-d_i.\n\\tag{8}\n\\]\n\nThe previously unvisited amount will be nonnegative by the next-stage\nfeasibility condition. The active ray's new outer record is a_i and its\nslope there is m_i. Hence its offset on its next visit is\n\n\\[\nd_{i+2}=a_i-m_i=d_i+s_i m_i.\n\\tag{9}\n\\]\n\nInitially d_0=d_1=0. Write O_i=d_i+d_{i+1}. Equations (8)--(9) imply\nO_{i+1}=O_i+s_i m_i and\na_{i+1}-d_{i+1}=(b_i-1)m_i-O_i. Equating this last quantity with\n(1+s_{i+1})m_{i+1} gives exactly (1). Also a_0=1 and d_0=0 imply the\nstated m_0, completing the induction that verifies (7) for each nonterminal\nstage. Positive m_i means nonterminal stages do not reach0. The terminal\ncase z_term=0 is permitted only with zero old slope and causes no difficulty.\n\nAt terminal stage k, the starting distance satisfies a_k-d_k=z_term. Extend\nits ray by a slope-1 ramp from its old record to slope q and then by a flat\nsegment to a_k. Condition (4) makes the ramp endpoint d_k+q no smaller\nthan the old record and no larger than a_k-s_k q. Thus every final-block\niterate stays on its flat segment. The final distance is a_k-s_k q.\n\nFor k=0 the same construction begins directly from the initial origin datum,\nwith a_0=1 and q given by (4).\n\nAfter the last stage extend each radial derivative constantly to infinity.\nDefine the global derivative by f'(x)=Q_+(x) for x>=0 and\nf'(x)=-Q_-(-x) for x<=0, and define f(x)=integral_0^x f'(t)dt.\nEach radial derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, with\nQ_+(0)=Q_-(0)=0. Across0, for x<=0<=y,\n\n\\[\n0\\leq f'(y)-f'(x)=Q_+(y)+Q_-(-x)\\leq y-x.\n\\]\n\nConsequently f' is globally continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz.\nThis proves differentiability, convexity, 1-smoothness, and a minimizer at0.\nThe earlier stage calculations prove the exact original GD recursion.\nIn particular no interpolation or dimensional-realizability gap remains\nwithin the candidate construction.\n\n## Retained function values give the exact objective\n\nOn one ray, a ramp from magnitude w to v adds area (v^2-w^2)/2. Its\nfollowing nonterminal flat segment has length s_i m_i and adds s_i m_i^2.\nTherefore at the outer record after any nonterminal stage i, the radial\nfunction value equals\n\n\\[\nm_i^2/2+\\sum_{j\\leq i,\\ j\\equiv i\\ ({\\rm mod}\\ 2)}s_jv_j^2.\n\\tag{10}\n\\]\n\nThis identity follows by induction on successive visits to that ray: the\nsquared-gradient contributions telescope and the flat-segment areas remain.\nAt the final stage the new ramp endpoint has value e_term+q^2/2. The distance\nfrom that endpoint to the final point is z_term-(s_k+1)q, on which the derivative\nequals q. The final value is consequently\n\n\\[\ne_term+q^2/2+q[z_term-(s_k+1)q]\n=e_term+qZ-(s_k+1/2)q^2,\n\\]\n\nproving (5). The initial distance is exactly1, so the normalization is exact.\n\n## Exact two-cut illustration\n\nFor h=(1,5,1,9,1) select checkpoint steps5 and9. Then s_0=s_1=s_2=1,\nm_0=1/2, O_0=0, m_1=1, O_1=1/2, z_term=15/2, m_old=1/2,\ne_term=1/4, and q_opt=5/2. All feasibility conditions hold. Formula (5) gives\n\n\\[\nW_5(1,5,1,9,1)\\geq2(1/4)+(15/2)^2/3=77/4.\n\\]\n\nThe actual points are 1,1/2,-2,-1,8,11/2. On the positive ray the\nderivative is r on [0,1/2], 1/2 on [1/2,1], r-1/2 on [1,3], and5/2\non [3,infinity). On the negative ray its magnitude is min(r,1).\nDirect integration gives f(11/2)=77/8, consistent with the formula.\nBy contrast the exact K_5 value is max{1/35,4/23,1/3}=1/3.\n\n\n\nOptimization, base cases, and lower-bound conclusion.\nFor any s_k>=0 the function q -> 2e_term+2q z_term-(2s_k+1)q^2 is a strictly concave quadratic. Its unconstrained maximum is z_term/(2s_k+1). Feasibility implies z_term>=0, and 2s_k+1>=s_k+1, so that maximum does not exceed the interval's upper endpoint. Clamping only at its lower endpoint gives the stated q_opt. When k=0, the construction is a ramp from0 to q followed by a plateau to the initial point1. Each ordinary step stays in that plateau because q<=1/(1+s_0). The objective formula is 2q-(2s_0+1)q^2 and its optimum is1/(2s_0+1), including s_0=0 and n=0. When k=1, z_term=(b_0-1)/(1+s_0), m_old=0 and e_term=0. Feasibility is b_0>=1, and optimizing gives z_term^2/(2s_1+1). The case b_0=1, q=0 reaches0 at the checkpoint and remains there; the negative ray can have identically zero derivative, so this is a legal degenerate case. Checkpoints with b_0<1 contribute only a zero term to K_n and need not be selected. Thus k=0 and k=1 recover all terms of K_n as defined in predecessor fact b98bb807d514e80d. There are at most2^n selected checkpoint sets, so the maximum defining F_ray_n is finite and nonempty. Each feasible choice is realized by a legal function and has the exact computed payoff, hence their maximum is at most W_n(h) and is at least K_n(h). This finite counting observation is not a numerical enumeration or a computational ingredient in the proof.\n\nEvery construction above was at L=1. For general L>0 replace the constructed function f_1 by f_L=L f_1. Its derivative is L-Lipschitz and its GD update with step h_t/L is exactly the same as f_1's update with step h_t. Its minimizer0, initial point1, and dimension one stay the same. The normalized objective 2f_L(x_n)/L equals2f_1(x_n), proving the stated normalization for all L. Zero ordinary steps repeat a point and cause no change in the proof. All step and derived parameter values are finite real numbers; every denominator s_i+1 or2s_k+1 is positive. Arbitrarily large isolated or clustered checkpoint steps are allowed whenever the explicitly stated feasibility conditions hold. No claim is made that an arbitrary checkpoint selection satisfies those conditions.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_alternating_ray_transfer.md", "supersedes": "c9876caabd790d77", "status": "full exact snapshot selected for explicitly authorized additional verification", "audit": "Six bounded tiny rational cases matched direct function integration and exact GD; analytic proof independent of those checks."}, "glossary": {"block_i": "The i-th chronological list of ordinary nonnegative steps, between selected checkpoint steps.", "s_i": "Sum of the ordinary steps in block_i.", "b_i": "Selected checkpoint step following block_i.", "m_i": "Positive gradient magnitude on nonterminal alternating-ray stage i.", "O_i": "Sum d_i+d_{i+1} of the two ray offsets before stage i.", "d_i": "Previous outermost distance on the active ray minus its derivative magnitude.", "a_i": "Distance from0 of the starting point of stage i.", "r_i": "Distance from0 of the endpoint of the new slope-1 ramp at nonterminal stage i.", "z_term": "Terminal distance budget a_k-d_k.", "e_term": "Sum of the retained flat-segment areas s_i m_i^2 on earlier visits to the terminal ray.", "m_old": "Previously attained derivative magnitude on the terminal ray, or0 if that ray has not been visited.", "q": "Chosen final derivative magnitude in the displayed feasible interval.", "q_opt": "The maximizing final derivative magnitude max(m_old,z_term/(2s_k+1)).", "F_ray_n": "Maximum of the exact optimized alternating-ray payoff over all feasible checkpoint sets for a length-n schedule.", "Q_+": "Radial derivative magnitude constructed on the nonnegative half-line.", "Q_-": "Radial derivative magnitude constructed on the nonpositive half-line.", "A_+": "Outermost previously visited distance on the nonnegative ray.", "A_-": "Outermost previously visited distance on the nonpositive ray.", "w_+": "Derivative magnitude at the current outermost positive-ray record.", "w_-": "Derivative magnitude at the current outermost negative-ray record.", "A_old": "Outermost distance on the ray about to be extended.", "w_old": "Derivative magnitude at A_old on the ray about to be extended."}}
{"id": "54496c04e3d34eb2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:37:58.401499+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "P5 concrete policy and quantitative payoff: prefix-seeded fair branching between complete-envelope steep and tail-interior gradients is pathwise legal, has an exact finite expectation recursion, and detects factorial-scale error on the linear schedules where all single-cut bounds are O(n^-2).", "evidence": "# P5: a concrete consistent branching policy\nStatus: complete support construction and two quantitative tests; unverified. No new verification allocation requested. The exact-profile comparison remains open.\n\n## 1. Scalar interpolation state\n\nNormalize L=1, choose minimizer0 and initial point1. A history H is a finite set of gradient knots (y,v), always including(0,0), satisfying\n0 <= (v-w)/(y-z) <=1 whenever y>z, and v=w when y=z.\nDefine\nell_H(x)=max_{(y,v) in H} [v-(y-x)_+],\nu_H(x)=min_{(y,v) in H} [v+(x-y)_+].\nEvery monotone1-Lipschitz derivative interpolating H lies between these envelopes. Conversely both envelopes are global monotone1-Lipschitz functions interpolating H: max/min preserve nondecreasingness and the upper bound1 on increments, and the pairwise history conditions force equality at each stored knot. In particular they agree at0 with value0. Thus ell_H<=u_H. Adding a knot(x,g) preserves the history conditions exactly when ell_H(x)<=g<=u_H(x).\n\nFor an endpoint x, the largest normalized objective among all convex1-smooth functions whose derivatives interpolate H and whose value at0 is0 is\nPhi(H,x)=2 integral_0^x u_H(z) dz if x>=0,\nPhi(H,x)=-2 integral_x^0 ell_H(z) dz if x<=0.\nFor the first case take derivative u_H globally, and for the second take derivative ell_H globally. These derivatives are continuous, monotone and1-Lipschitz, vanish at0, and integrate to globally convex1-smooth functions minimized at0. The envelopes bound every possible derivative pointwise, which proves maximality with the appropriate integration orientation. The chosen objective therefore reproduces every stored gradient. It is piecewise quadratic with finitely many pieces and lives in dimension1.\n\nThis is a derivative-oracle construction: no function values were supplied to GD and none need to be preserved except the final chosen value. The whole h is fixed first. An adversary's random choices construct a function afterward whose exact gradient trajectory is that path; they do not change the GD steps adaptively.\n\n## 2. Actual policy, not optimization over all gradient choices\n\nFix h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}) with n>=1 and arbitrary finite nonnegative entries. For each j in{0,...,n-1}, put P_j=sum_{s<j}h_s and c=1/(1+P_j). Policy D_j first forces g_i=c for0<=i<=j. Its points are x_i=1-c sum_{s<i}h_s>=c for i<=j, so these knots are legal with(0,0). After step j,\nx_{j+1}=c(1-h_j).\nFor every t=j+1,...,n-1 let S_t=sum_{s=t}^{n-1}h_s, let H_t be ALL prior knots together with(0,0), and compute\nv_t = u_{H_t}(x_t) if x_t>=0, and ell_{H_t}(x_t) if x_t<0,\nw_t = projection of x_t/(1+2S_t) onto [ell_{H_t}(x_t),u_{H_t}(x_t)].\nWith probability1/2 choose g_t=v_t; otherwise choose g_t=w_t. Coins at successive stages are independent. Add(x_t,g_t), then take the fixed GD step x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t g_t. Repeated points, zero steps and x_t=0 are harmless because their feasible intervals force the stored gradient.\n\nAfter the final step select the global objective attaining Phi(H_n,x_n). This is a finite law on at most2^{n-j-1} legal functions, each with initial squared distance1. The expected normalized error D_j(h) has the exact recurrence\nE_n(H,x)=Phi(H,x),\nE_t(H,x)=(1/2)E_{t+1}(H union{(x,v)},x-h_t v)\n        +(1/2)E_{t+1}(H union{(x,w)},x-h_t w),\nwith v,w defined by the displayed policy at state(H,x,t).\nD_j(h) is E_{j+1} evaluated after its forced prefix. This recurrence is the payoff of two specified actions, not a supremum over all feasible gradients. It is an exact symbolic specification; no exponential enumeration has been run.\n\nEach selected function is legal path by path. Thus W_n(h)>=D_j(h) for every j. Combined with the verified global/single-cut functional K_n from fact b98bb807d514e80d,\nW_n(h)>=B_n^branch(h):=max{K_n(h), max_{0<=j<n}D_j(h)}.\nOne may choose the j with largest expectation after seeing h, or choose a point mass on a sufficiently good leaf. No infimum/expectation interchange is used. This notation B_n^branch is distinct from the benchmark denominator B_n.\n\n## 3. Exact stopping event recovers the selected-cut value\n\nSuppose h_j>1 and j<n-1. Write R=c(h_j-1)>0 and T=S_{j+1}. The first point on the negative half-line is -R, with no negative gradient knots yet. Therefore the feasible interval is[-R,0]. On its w-branch, the chosen gradient is -a, where a=R/(1+2T).\nAll later points remain <=-a, because total remaining displacement is aT and -R+aT=-a(1+T)<=-a.\nThe steep envelope at these points is -a. The later tail proposal is at most -a: if s is the tail sum already used, the ratio of its magnitude to a is\n(1+2T-s)/(1+2T-2s)>=1.\nThus clamping selects -a. Both actions hence coincide thereafter, so this branch exactly follows an asymmetric Huber tail.\nThe terminal envelope on the negative half-line is max(z,-a); hence its normalized objective is\nR^2/(1+2T)=(h_j-1)^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T)].\nThe event has probability1/2, giving D_j(h) at least half of that cut term. At j=n-1 there is no coin; the maximal endpoint value is R^2, exactly the cut term with T=0.\n\nThis confirms normalization and chronology, but the half-cut bound alone is only a check: K_n already retains the full cut maximum. The benefit sought from D_j is in branches that continue through multiple sign changes and record excursions.\n\n## 4. Quantitative multi-excursion test on the linear schedules\n\nTake n>=4 and h_t=t+1. Choose j=2, so P_j=1+2=3 and c=1/4. The forced prefix has\nx_0=1, x_1=3/4, x_2=1/4, x_3=-1/2.\nOn the event that every later coin takes the steep action, the trajectory is exactly GD on the function with derivative\nq(z)=z for z<=1/4,\nq(z)=1/4 for1/4<=z<=1,\nq(z)=z-3/4 forz>=1.\nThis is a single global monotone1-Lipschitz derivative. Its derivative values equal the selected extremal envelope at every new query; adding such a knot does not change the pertinent extremal envelope. Positive knots cannot tighten the negative lower envelope beyond z, and negative knots on q(z)=z cannot tighten the positive upper envelope below min(z,1/4+(z-1)_+). Thus induction proves this entire event, not only its initial portion.\nIn particular x_4=3/2. Thereafter signs alternate and |x_t|>=3/2. For negative x_t the next magnitude is t|x_t|. For positive x_t, t>=4,\nx_{t+1}=-t x_t+(3/4)(t+1)<0,\nand\n|x_{t+1}|>=((t-1)/2)|x_t|>=(t/4)|x_t|,\nusing |x_t|>=3/2. The last inequality and t>=4 preserve the lower bound3/2.\nTherefore\n|x_n| >= (3/2) product_{t=4}^{n-1}(t/4).\nThe chosen final envelope has objective at least that of the displayed global q. Integrating q shows 2f(z)=z^2 for negative z; for positive z>=3/2 it is (z-3/4)^2+3/8>=z^2/4. Hence every terminal point on this event satisfies\n2f(x_n) >= (9/16) [product_{t=4}^{n-1}(t/4)]^2.\nThere are n-3 fair coins after the forced prefix, so\nD_2(h) >= 2^{-(n-3)}(9/16)[product_{t=4}^{n-1}(t/4)]^2. (L)\nThe right side diverges faster than any fixed power of n. For example, from t>=8 each factor in the product is at least2, and the factorial expression gives the full growth:\nproduct_{t=4}^{n-1}(t/4)=(n-1)!/[6*4^{n-4}].\nThis analytic event calculation uses no numerical sweep or path enumeration.\nBy contrast, accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d proves K_n(h)<=16/n^2 for the same linear schedules. Thus this actual branching policy detects objective mass missed by every single-cut term on that family. It is a mechanism advance, not an all-schedule lower bound.\n\n## 5. Guarded-long-step test\n\nFor h=(1,H,1), H>1, choose j=1. Then c=1/2 and the forced points are1,1/2,-(H-1)/2. On the final interior branch, probability1/2, the gradient is x_2/3 and the envelope is a left Huber tail. Its normalized error is (H-1)^2/12. Consequently\nD_1(1,H,1)>=(H-1)^2/24.\nThis avoids the fixed-one-curvature annihilation that affected the previous two-curvature subclass. It does not imply any bound for arbitrary schedules by itself.\n\n## 6. Remaining obligation and next adversarial tests\n\nNo proof presently compares B_n^branch(h) uniformly to U_n^ZJ. The statement\nfor every epsilon>0 there exists N_epsilon independent of h such that\nB_n^branch(h)>=(1-epsilon)U_n^ZJ for all n>=N_epsilon and h>=0\nwould by itself solve the affirmative direction; it cannot be called a support lemma.\nThe concrete next test is whether the fair-coin continuation loses nonvanishing mass on recursive schedules near the ZJ sum threshold, even when B_n^branch includes the exact K_n fallback. If it does, change the continuation probabilities or replace the scalar geometry, not merely append another restricted-family no-go. The successful linear/guarded tests do not establish that the comparison is true.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_branching_policy.md", "plan_id": "max_scalar_branching_oracle", "status": "actual legal policy; quantitative restricted tests; full comparison open", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "evidence_status": "new construction and event calculations unverified; selected verification budget used"}, "glossary": {"H_t": "Complete finite set of prior scalar gradient knots including(0,0).", "ell_H": "Largest monotone1-Lipschitz lower gradient envelope defined in the evidence.", "u_H": "Smallest monotone1-Lipschitz upper gradient envelope defined in the evidence.", "Phi(H,x)": "Exact maximal normalized objective at endpoint x among derivatives interpolating H.", "D_j(h)": "Expected normalized objective of the explicitly specified prefix-seeded two-action policy.", "E_t": "Expected terminal payoff recurrence for this policy.", "v_t": "Steep absolute-gradient action at time t.", "w_t": "Projected tail-scaled interior action at time t.", "B_n^branch": "Maximum of the verified K_n functional and all D_j(h), not the benchmark mass B_n.", "q": "Explicit three-piece derivative used to analyze the all-steep event on linear schedules."}}
{"id": "0fda962c46583d52", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:42:17.418901+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Quantitative full-PEP certificate constraints: exact chronological slack entries, a flow-weighted Huber bound, and a PSD-controlled residual; the exact one-step check distinguishes feasible certificates from scalar pseudo-certificates.", "evidence": "Complete derivation: research/max2_full_certificate_constraints.md. Normalize L=1. Use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. A full objective certificate q has nonnegative lambda_ij, flow out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n}, and actual PSD matrix M_cert=[[q,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]], where mu_i=lambda_i*, nu_i=lambda_*i, Nu=sum nu, sum mu=2+Nu. Set S=sum h, P_i=sum_{t<i}h_t, h_n=0. Exact identities: A_ii=out_i-1_{i=n}-h_i sum_{j>i}lambda_ij; for i<j, 2A_ij=-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji)-h_j sum_{k>j}lambda_ik+h_i(mu_j+sum_{k<=i}lambda_jk). In particular Z=A-mu mu^T/(4q) must remain PSD, not just satisfy row equations. All-gradients-equal evaluation gives 1^T A1=2S+1+sum_i nu_i(P_i+1), hence q>=(2+Nu)^2/[4(2S+1+sum nu_i(P_i+1))]>=1/(2S+1). With tau=1/(2S+1), eta=q-tau-tau sum nu_i[1-tau(P_i+1)]>=0. Define drift_i=sum_j lambda_ij(P_i-P_j), tailnu_i=sum_{j>i}nu_j, and R_i=2h_i+nu_i+drift_i+h_i tailnu_i-2S mu_i. The full row identity is 2A1=2h+mu+nu+drift+h.*tailnu; consequently PSD Cauchy--Schwarz gives R_i^2<=4A_ii eta/tau^2. If S>0, also Nu<=(q-tau)/(tau^2 S). At q=tau this forces nu=0, R=0 and Z1=0 while Z remains PSD. Exact check n=1,h=a: saturated flow/rows reduce to mu_0=t,mu_1=2-t,lambda_01=2(1-t),lambda_10=2-t,0<=t<=1. The Schur diagonal is Z00=2-2a+(2a-1)t-(2a+1)t^2/4, whose maximum for a>=3/2 is (3-2a)/4. Thus PSD excludes every a>3/2 at the Huber level. For a<=3/2,t=1 gives the genuine full certificate Z=(3-2a)/4 [[1,-1],[-1,1]]. For a=2, the same flow/row pseudo-certificate would claim q=1/5 although the quadratic witness gives error1; its missing PSD check is exactly Z00=-1/4. All h may be arbitrary real nonnegative and S=0 is explicitly separated. No stationarity or unproved strong-duality interchange is invoked. Evidence status: complete exploratory derivation, not verified; quota already used. The interaction of residuals and full PSD is active; the scalar Huber bound by itself is not a target comparison.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_full_certificate_constraints.md", "mechanism": "full dual PSD slack and quantitative Huber-face rigidity", "status": "new quantitative constraints with exact feasible/pseudo-certificate tests", "scope": "necessary certificate inequalities; no full minimax comparison", "fact_ids": ["dfde2fa326c908bf"]}, "glossary": {"M_cert": "The full PEP objective certificate slack matrix on (x_0,g_0,...,g_n).", "Z_cert": "Its gradient Schur complement A-mu mu^T/(4q).", "mu_i": "Interpolation multiplier lambda_{i,*} toward the optimum.", "nu_i": "Interpolation multiplier lambda_{*,i} leaving the optimum.", "Nu": "Total multiplier mass leaving the optimum, sum_i nu_i.", "drift_i": "Chronological multiplier moment sum_j lambda_ij(P_i-P_j), with P_*=0.", "tailnu_i": "Sum of nu_j over j>i.", "R_i": "Chronological Huber-face residual 2h_i+nu_i+drift_i+h_i tailnu_i-2S mu_i."}}
{"id": "fa7d5c5f2051df81", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:44:48.055364+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "P5 probability repair: power weighting the same finite legal branch family preserves at least2^(-1/n) of its best-leaf normalized payoff, uniformly in h, and repairs the exact two-step fair-law failure. The best-leaf/profile comparison is still BLOCKED.", "evidence": "# P5 probability repair: finite power weighting\nStatus: unverified derivation. Companion to research/max_branching_policy.md. No new verifier request.\n\nThe fair law need not attain the strongest witness already in its support. At h=(3/2,19/10), seed j=0 has payoffs Q=81/400 (steep quadratic) and C=5/96 (tail Huber), whose fair average is611/4800. Seed j=1 gives81/625. Combined with K_2, the fair value is81/625<U_2^ZJ; see a2e802d2fcd0c0b5. This finite failure does not refute the asymptotic target, but it exposes a concrete probability loss.\n\nRetain the exact legal prefix seeds and two-action tree of54496c04e3d34eb2. For seed j let m=n-j-1 be the number of coins, label the2^m paths by sigma, and let Z_sigma=Phi(H_n^sigma,x_n^sigma)>=0. Let M_j(h)=max_sigma Z_sigma. Put p=n^2 for n>=1. If every Z_sigma=0, set T_j(h)=0 and use any leaf. Otherwise choose the finite law\nPr_p(sigma)=Z_sigma^p / sum_tau Z_tau^p.\nEach sampled leaf still defines a fixed globally legal one-dimensional objective with initial squared distance1. This changes only the probabilities in a specified finite family; it does not choose the schedule adaptively or optimize over arbitrary functions.\n\nThe exact expected normalized objective is\nT_j(h)=sum_sigma Z_sigma^{p+1}/sum_sigma Z_sigma^p.\nDefine A_r=2^{-m}sum_sigma Z_sigma^r. Convexity of t -> t^{(p+1)/p}, applied to the numbers Z_sigma^p, gives\nA_{p+1}>=A_p^{(p+1)/p}.\nTherefore\nT_j=A_{p+1}/A_p>=A_p^{1/p}>=M_j 2^{-m/p}>=M_j 2^{-1/n}.\nAlso T_j<=M_j because it is a convex combination. Thus\n2^{-1/n} M_j(h)<=T_j(h)<=M_j(h)\nuniformly over every finite nonnegative schedule and seed. The relative loss tends to zero with a threshold independent of h. This compares two quantities INSIDE the concrete policy family, not either quantity with the target profile.\n\nAn equivalent conditional sampling rule chooses a child in proportion to the sum of Z_sigma^p over its descendant leaves. Multiplication of conditional probabilities telescopes to the displayed leaf law. This is a finite mathematical specification; no exhaustive computation or enumeration has been run. The allowed adversary can depend on the entire predetermined h; complexity in the task measures GD iterations.\n\nThe repaired lower-bound functional is\nB_n^tilt(h)=max{K_n(h),max_{0<=j<n}T_j(h)}.\nEvery T_j is the expectation of legal normalized instances; hence W_n(h)>=B_n^tilt(h). The K_n term is the already verified witness family from fact b98bb807d514e80d. A maximizing seed exists because there are n choices, and choosing a distribution or deterministic K_n witness after inspecting h incurs no mixture penalty and needs no minimax interchange.\n\nAt n=2 and h=(3/2,19/10), m=1,p=4, and M_0>=81/400. Hence T_0>81/500 because2^{1/4}<5/4. The exact benchmark recurrence gives S_bullet(2)>=3/2+sqrt3>3, so U_2^ZJ<1/7<81/500. Thus the repaired law detects the concrete fair-policy failure. On the linear schedules h_t=t+1 of the companion file, the large all-steep payoff is retained with factor at worst2^{-1/n}; its exponentially small fair probability no longer weakens this comparison.\n\nThis repairs probability weighting only. The unproved statement\nfor every epsilon>0 there exists N_epsilon independent of h such that\nmax{K_n(h),max_j M_j(h)}>=(1-epsilon)U_n^ZJ\nfor all n>=N_epsilon and every h in[0,infinity)^n\nis still a theorem-strength geometric comparison. It is BLOCKED until an actual inequality is found. If it were established, the displayed power-weight inequality would transfer it to a legal distribution with no asymptotic constant loss. No such geometric comparison has been proved here.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_branching_tilt.md", "plan_id": "max_scalar_branching_oracle", "repair_of": "a2e802d2fcd0c0b5", "source_policy": "54496c04e3d34eb2", "status": "probability loss repaired within explicit family; target comparison BLOCKED", "evidence_status": "unverified, no extra submission"}, "glossary": {"Z_sigma": "Nonnegative terminal normalized payoff on leaf sigma of the explicitly specified P5 action tree.", "M_j(h)": "Largest leaf payoff for the fixed prefix seed j.", "T_j(h)": "Expected payoff under leaf weights proportional to Z_sigma^(n^2).", "A_r": "Unweighted mean of the r-th powers of all leaf payoffs for the fixed seed.", "B_n^tilt": "Maximum of the verified K_n functional and all the tilted expectations T_j."}}
{"id": "3c677e24e6b02c0b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:52:07.465154+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C7 legal inner-return extension: a fixed quadratic prefix spliced to one terminal affine cap has an exact record-constrained payoff. On h=(8/5,8/5,13,1) it gives3888/625, strictly exceeding max(F_ray_4,Q_4).", "evidence": "# A fixed quadratic core with an exact terminal affine cap\n\nStatus: complete new candidate derivation, unverified. Worker: max3.\nThis repairs one specific deficiency of the verified outward-ray construction:\nthe prefix may return inside a previously visited region arbitrarily many\ntimes, because one fixed quadratic governs all those returns.\n\n## Legal function and exact trajectory\n\nFix any n>=1 and nonnegative finite schedule h, any lambda in (0,1], a sign\nsigma in {-1,+1}, and an index j in {0,...,n-1}. Define prefix polynomials\n\n\\[\np_0(\\lambda)=1,\\qquad p_t(\\lambda)=\\prod_{r<t}(1-h_r\\lambda).\n\\]\n\nDefine\n\n\\[\nR=\\max\\{0,\\sigma p_t(\\lambda):0\\leq t\\leq j\\},\\qquad\nA=\\sigma p_{j+1}(\\lambda),\\qquad T=\\sum_{r>j}h_r.\n\\]\n\nAssume A>0 and A>=(1+lambda T)R. Choose a cap radius\n\n\\[\nr\\in[R,A/(1+\\lambda T)].\n\\]\n\nThe explicit function is\n\n\\[\nf(x)=\\begin{cases}\n\\lambda x^2/2,&\\sigma x\\leq r,\\\\\n\\lambda r\\sigma x-\\lambda r^2/2,&\\sigma x\\geq r.\n\\end{cases}\n\\tag{1}\n\\]\n\nThe two branches and their derivatives agree at sigma x=r. Its derivative\nis continuous, nondecreasing, and lambda-Lipschitz, hence at most1-Lipschitz;\nit is convex and differentiable and has minimum0 at0. These properties also\nhold when r=0, in which case one side is flat. The entire function is fixed\nbefore the GD run, after observing the predetermined schedule.\n\nStart x_0=1. For every query through j, sigma p_t(lambda)<=r. An induction\ntherefore gives x_t=p_t(lambda) and f'(x_t)=lambda p_t(lambda) through that\nprefix. All sign changes and inner returns in the prefix are legal without\nresetting the function or dropping older values. The update at j gives\nsigma x_{j+1}=A. On the suffix the derivative is the fixed value\nsigma lambda r. Nonnegative steps and A>=(1+lambda T)r imply that every\nsuffix iterate stays in the capped region, since its signed coordinate is at\nleast A-lambda rT>=r. Thus\n\n\\[\n\\sigma x_n=A-\\lambda rT,\\qquad\n2f(x_n)=2\\lambda rA-\\lambda(1+2\\lambda T)r^2.\n\\tag{2}\n\\]\n\nThe initial squared distance to the minimizer is1. Multiplying (1) by L\ngives the same normalized error for the original h_t/L steps at arbitrary\nL>0. The best radius in the displayed interval is\n\n\\[\nr_{\\rm opt}=\\max\\{R,A/(1+2\\lambda T)\\}.\n\\tag{3}\n\\]\n\nThe right endpoint is never exceeded by (3), because T>=0. This gives a\nfully explicit lower-bound functional after maximizing over feasible\n(lambda,sigma,j). The record R is essential: choosing a smaller radius would\nchange the already used prefix function and invalidate the polynomial formula.\n\n## A strict improvement beyond outward rays plus quadratics\n\nTake the exact four-step schedule h=(8/5,8/5,13,1), choose lambda=1,\nsigma=-1 and j=2. The core prefix is\n\n\\[\nx_0=1,\\quad x_1=-3/5,\\quad x_2=9/25,\\quad x_3=-108/25.\n\\]\n\nThus R=3/5, A=108/25, T=1 and r_opt=36/25. Formula (1) is simply the\nunit-curvature quadratic above -36/25, continued affinely below that point.\nThe final step gives x_4=-72/25, and the normalized error is exactly\n\n\\[\n2f(x_4)=\\frac{A^2}{3}=\\frac{3888}{625}.\n\\tag{4}\n\\]\n\nThis strictly exceeds the combination of the previously considered two\nchannels on this schedule, as the following exact comparisons establish.\n\n### Exact outward-ray value\n\nUse the functional F_ray_4 from verified fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a. Any feasible\nchain with two or more checkpoints uses only the first three steps: a final\ncheckpoint equal to1 gives nonpositive distance budget and cannot satisfy the\npositive old-slope requirement. Chains with zero or one checkpoint give\n\n\\[\nK_4(h)=\\max\\{5/177,9/805,9/4901,400/147,0\\}=400/147.\n\\]\n\nFor two checkpoints at indices(0,1), the terminal budget is9/25 whereas its\nrequired lower bound is15, so it is infeasible. For indices(0,2), the verified\nrecurrence gives m_0=1,m_1=3/13,O_1=0,z_term=36/13,s_2=1,m_old=1.\nIts optimizing q is1 and the payoff is33/13. For indices(1,2), it gives\nm_0=5/13,m_1=3/13,O_1=8/13,z_term=28/13,s_2=1,m_old=5/13,\ne_term=40/169; its optimum payoff is1024/507. The three-checkpoint choice\n(0,1,2) has m_2=9/25<m_0=1 and is infeasible. This exhausts all eligible\nsets. Both33/13 and1024/507 are less than400/147. Therefore\n\n\\[\nF_{\\rm ray,4}(h)=400/147.\n\\]\n\n### A sufficient exact quadratic upper bound\n\nFor this h the full quadratic-class error is\n\n\\[\nQ_4(h)=\\max_{0\\leq t\\leq1}\nt(1-8t/5)^4(1-13t)^2(1-t)^2.\n\\]\n\nFor 0<=t<=1, |(1-13t)(1-t)|<=36/13. On [0,1/13] the product is at\nmost1 in absolute value; on [1/13,1] its magnitude is the concave quadratic\n(13t-1)(1-t), whose maximum36/13 is attained at t=7/13.\nIf t<=5/8 then |1-8t/5|<=1 and the displayed objective is at most\n(5/8)(36/13)^2=810/169. If t>=5/8 then |1-8t/5|<=3/5 and the objective\nis at most(3/5)^4(36/13)^2=104976/105625<1. Thus\n\n\\[\n\\max\\{F_{\\rm ray,4}(h),Q_4(h)\\}\\leq810/169\n<3888/625.\n\\]\n\nThe last inequality is exact: their ratio is4056/3125>1. The new witness\ntherefore adds a real legal interaction beyond a maximum of the old channels.\nThis fixed-horizon improvement in a lower-bound family is not a resolution\nof the asymptotic minimax problem.\n\n## Remaining target obligation\n\nNo claim is made that the supremum of (2), even together with F_ray_n and Q_n,\nmatches the exact ZJ profile for every schedule. The specific new mechanism\nis a legal quadratic prefix, including inner returns, followed by a nonlinear\ncontinuation constrained by an exact remote-excursion record. A next extension\ncould allow a fixed two-curvature core or repeated cap exits and returns, but\nmust preserve the old record and values instead of independently resampling\ncurvature. Any uniform profile comparison still needs an actual proof.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_quadratic_core_terminal_cap.md", "fact_ids": ["1fca3e5dd555cb4a"], "status": "full exact candidate; unverified, no extra verification allocation", "new_mechanism": "Inner quadratic returns followed by legal nonlinear activation with retained excursion record", "target_gap": "No uniform exact-profile comparison proved."}, "glossary": {"C7": "Quadratic-core prefix with an exact one-sided terminal affine cap.", "p_t": "Prefix scalar polynomial product_{r<t}(1-h_r lambda).", "R": "Largest signed prefix excursion toward the selected capped side, including0.", "A": "Signed post-checkpoint position sigma p_{j+1}(lambda), assumed positive.", "T": "Sum of the steps after the selected activation index j.", "r": "Cap radius constrained by all prior core queries and by the remaining suffix.", "r_opt": "Optimizing cap radius max(R,A/(1+2lambda T)).", "sigma": "Sign selecting the capped ray, either-1 or+1.", "Q_n": "Exact normalized quadratic-class worst-case functional."}}
{"id": "30fd3be213bc77e2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:52:08.378366+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "P5's signed-steep branch handles the inner returns missed by outward-ray chains: on h_t=(t+1)/2 it realizes an explicit legal function with factorial-square terminal error.", "evidence": "# P5 retains inner returns that the outward-ray family omits\nStatus: new analytic candidate; not verifier-accepted. The only cited correctness bricks are facts 1fca3e5dd555cb4a and b98bb807d514e80d. This document gives a self-contained legal-function calculation, without relying on another unverified construction as a brick.\n\nFor n>=8 let h_t=(t+1)/2 for 0<=t<n. Set c=2/5 and define the global derivative\nq(x)=x for x<=2/5,\nq(x)=2/5 for 2/5<=x<=1,\nq(x)=x-3/5 for x>=1.\nLet f(x)=integral_0^x q(z)dz. The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, with q(0)=0. Thus f is convex differentiable, 1-smooth, minimized at0 with value0. The initial point is1, so the normalized error is2f(x_n).\n\nThe exact first eight steps are\nx_0=1, x_1=4/5, x_2=2/5, x_3=-1/5,\nx_4=1/5, x_5=-3/10, x_6=3/5, x_7=-4/5, x_8=12/5.\nDirect substitution into q and x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t q(x_t) verifies all these values.\nIn particular x_4 and x_6 return inside the previous positive-ray record1. An adversary restricted to successive outward ray records cannot follow this chronology.\n\nFor every t>=8 the signs alternate and |x_t|>=12/5. If x_t<0 then\n|x_{t+1}|=((t-1)/2)|x_t| >= (t/4)|x_t|.\nIf x_t>0 then\nx_{t+1}= -((t-1)/2)x_t+3(t+1)/10.\nUsing x_t>=12/5 gives\n|x_{t+1}| >= [(t-1)/2-(t+1)/8]|x_t|\n =((3t-5)/8)|x_t| >= (t/4)|x_t|.\nThe last bound holds for t>=5; with t>=8 it also preserves the magnitude lower bound and proves the claimed alternating signs. Hence\n|x_n| >= (12/5) product_{t=8}^{n-1}(t/4).\n\nFor x<0, 2f(x)=x^2. For x>=1,\n2f(x)=(x-3/5)^2+12/25.\nThis is at least x^2/4: the difference is\n(3/4)x^2-(6/5)x+21/25\n=(3/4)(x-4/5)^2+9/25 >0.\nTherefore the legal one-dimensional objective satisfies\nW_n(h)>=2f(x_n)>=(36/25)[product_{t=8}^{n-1}(t/4)]^2.\nThe product is (n-1)!/[7! 4^(n-8)] for n>=8. This grows faster than any fixed power. No computation or exhaustive search is part of the proof.\n\n## Membership in the concrete P5 action tree\nThe seed j=2 in P5 forces c=1/(1+h_0+h_1)=2/5, agreeing with the displayed first three queried gradients. Thereafter taking the signed steep envelope at every step reproduces q. Here is the direct envelope check, so this assertion is not a reliance on an unverified earlier finding.\n\nThe stored knots at(0,0),(2/5,2/5),(1,2/5) force the upper gradient envelope on the positive half-line to be exactly q there: the origin bounds it by x, the knot at2/5 bounds it by2/5 for smaller x and by x for larger x, and the knot at1 bounds it by2/5 below1 and by x-3/5 above1. Taking the minimum gives the displayed q. Every other positive queried knot lies on q, so cannot tighten this envelope below q. On the negative half-line the lower envelope supplied by the origin is x. All positive knots have v<=y, so v-(y-x)<=x and cannot raise it. Every negative knot on q(x)=x likewise preserves that envelope. Thus every subsequent signed steep query equals q(x_t), including the interior returns.\n\nConsequently this single leaf is present in P5. Under the original fair law its probability is2^{-(n-3)}, which already leaves a diverging lower bound after multiplication by the factorial-square payoff. Under the separately derived power tilt its expected payoff is within2^{-1/n} of the maximum leaf; that probability repair is an unverified companion candidate, not needed for the present legal-function lower bound.\n\n## Scope\nFinding97c780b4975678c7 gives an unverified analytic argument that the accepted functional F_ray_n from fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a collapses to K_n and is O(n^-2) on this same half-linear family. The present construction does not depend on that argument: it independently establishes a large legal objective and membership in the richer P5 tree. It shows that failing the outward-record restriction is not a reason to abandon scalar envelope branching.\n\nNo all-schedule comparison to U_n^ZJ is claimed. In particular an instability test on this family does not supply the still-missing inequality on schedules with bounded worst-case objective.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_branching_inner_returns.md", "fact_ids": ["1fca3e5dd555cb4a", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "related_findings": ["97c780b4975678c7", "54496c04e3d34eb2", "fa7d5c5f2051df81"], "status": "complete analytic candidate; no extra verification requested"}, "glossary": {"q": "Explicit continuous derivative with slope1, a positive plateau, then slope1.", "c": "Plateau magnitude2/5 in this example.", "P5": "The concrete full-history scalar gradient-envelope branching policy of finding54496c04e3d34eb2."}}
{"id": "810059b7988880a2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:53:06.731096+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Candidate full-certificate stationary energy invariant at genuine global minimizers: boundary, duality and degeneracy arguments supplied; exact one-step primal-dual check passes.", "evidence": "# Global minimizers and a stationary energy identity for full PEP certificates\n\nStatus: exploratory, unverified proof package. No extra verifier submission is authorized. The selected initial submission was used for fact dfde2fa326c908bf. This document does not establish the exact-profile target.\n\n## 1. Claim under investigation\n\nFor each fixed horizon n>=1, the project minimization over h in [0,infinity)^n has a global minimizer, and every global minimizer has strictly positive steps. At any such minimizer there exist a full interpolation upper certificate (q,lambda), a legal primal Gram matrix G and values f_i, attaining q=W_n^*, for which\n\n    <g_t,z_t>=0, 0<=t<n,                                      (S)\n    q=(1/2) sum_{i,j} lambda_ij ||g_i-g_j||^2.                 (E)\n\nHere the Euclidean vectors realize G, x_*=g_*=0, ||x_0||=1, and\n\n    z_t=sum_{i>t}(mu_i+sum_{j<=t}lambda_ij)g_i\n        -sum_{i<=t}(sum_{j>t}lambda_ij)g_i,                    (Z)\n    mu_i=lambda_{i,*}.\n\nEvery unstarred sum ranges over 0,...,n; directed pairs are distinct. The assertion retains the full certificate PSD constraint and the full primal interpolation constraints. It does not claim that these displayed identities alone characterize global minimizers.\n\nThe proof below supplies the compactness, zero-step exclusion, strong-duality and multiplier-regularity arguments rather than assuming interiority or nonsingular multipliers.\n\n## 2. Fixed-horizon compactness and positive optimal steps\n\nNormalize L=1, x_*=0 and f_*=0. For any fixed finite h, the estimates ||g_i||<=||x_i|| and ||x_{i+1}||<= (1+h_i)||x_i|| imply W_n(h)<=prod_i(1+h_i)^2. Every fixed legal function and initial point has an error continuous in h, by continuity of its gradient. A supremum of continuous functions is lower semicontinuous, so W_n is lower semicontinuous.\n\nWrite Poly_n(z)=prod_{t<n}(1-h_t z). For n+1 distinct nodes a_k=k/(n+1), 1<=k<=n+1, the quadratic f(x)=a_k x^2/2 from x_0=1 gives\n\n    a_k Poly_n(a_k)^2 <= W_n(h).\n\nDefine the Lagrange polynomials L_k(z)=prod_{l!=k}(z-a_l)/(a_k-a_l). On the sublevel W_n(h)<=1, interpolation of a polynomial of degree at most n gives\n\n    sum_t h_t = -Poly_n'(0)\n      <= sum_k |L_k'(0)|/sqrt(a_k) =: C_n < infinity.\n\nNonnegativity of the steps therefore bounds each h_t by C_n. The sublevel is closed, bounded, and nonempty because h=0 has W_n=1. Lower semicontinuity on this compact set proves attainment of the global infimum.\n\nFor positivity, first note the explicit Huber lower bound. Put S=sum h_t and delta=1/(2S+1). The function f(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=delta, and delta|x|-delta^2/2 otherwise, is convex and 1-smooth. From x_0=1, every iterate is x_i=1-delta sum_{t<i}h_t>=delta; its terminal error is 2f(x_n)=1/(2S+1)>0.\n\nSuppose a globally minimizing length-n schedule contains a zero. Delete that zero to obtain h' of length n-1 with exactly the same terminal point and worst-case value q>0. Set B=prod_{t<n-1}(1+h'_t), so every normalized legal instance has terminal radius at most B. If its terminal function gap is F, convexity gives F<=B||g||, and a final unit step gives\n\n    F_new <= F-||g||^2/2 <= F-F^2/(2B^2).\n\nWe have 0<=F<=q/2<=B^2/2. The displayed scalar function is increasing on this interval. Thus appending a unit step to h' produces another length-n schedule satisfying\n\n    W_n(h',1) <= q-q^2/(4B^2) < q,\n\ncontradicting global minimality. Therefore every minimizing step is positive. This argument also handles the all-zero proposed minimizer and uses no differentiability of W_n.\n\n## 3. Exact finite-dimensional primal formulation\n\nLet I={*,0,...,n}. For i!=j define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nThe primal variables are the Gram matrix G of (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with G>=0 and G_00<=1, together with the real values f_0,...,f_n. The remaining positions are defined by x_i=x_0-sum_{t<i}h_t g_t, and the optimum sample is zero. Impose Q_ij<=0 for every ordered pair. Maximize 2f_n.\n\nFor completeness, the interpolation sufficiency has the following explicit proof. Given such finite vector/value data, put a_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i and\n\n    H(v)=max_i {a_i+<x_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2}.\n\nAt v=g_i, the i-th expression is a_i and every j-th expression is at most a_i, because that comparison is exactly -Q_ij>=0. Hence H(g_i)=a_i and x_i belongs to the convex subdifferential of H at g_i. The function H is finite and 1-strongly convex, with quadratic growth. Its conjugate f=H^* is finite everywhere, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, and satisfies f(x_i)=f_i and grad f(x_i)=g_i. Indeed, strong convexity makes the maximizing vector in the conjugate unique; the strong monotonicity of the subgradient relation bounds the distance between two such maximizing vectors by the distance between their arguments. This gives the claimed gradient Lipschitz constant. The zero sample makes zero a minimizer. Thus the primal optimum is exactly W_n(h), with dimension at most n+2.\n\nThe feasible primal set is compact: the radius/gradient estimates above bound all Gram entries; the two optimum interpolation inequalities imply 0<=f_i<=||x_i||^2/2. It is closed and nonempty, so its optimum is attained.\n\nFor positive h, the primal admits strict interpolation inequalities and G positive definite. Take n+2 distinct numbers theta_k in (0,1), the quadratic with diagonal Hessian diag(theta_k), and an initial vector having every coordinate nonzero and norm less than one. The columns x_0,g_0,...,g_n are evaluations of 1,zPoly_0(z),...,zPoly_n(z) at these nodes, times nonzero coordinate factors. Their distinct degrees and nonzero leading coefficients make this matrix invertible. No two sampled positions agree, because their different prefix polynomials cannot agree at n+2 distinct nodes. None equals zero for the same degree reason. Direct substitution gives\n\n    Q_ij=-(1/2)sum_k theta_k(1-theta_k)(x_{i,k}-x_{j,k})^2 <0\n\nfor every distinct pair, including *. The norm inequality is also strict.\n\nSource context: Taylor, Hendrickx and Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem 4 and Theorem 6 treat smooth-convex interpolation and strong duality for fixed-step methods using the latest gradient with nonzero coefficient. Their proof uses a full-rank quadratic transcript. The preceding Vandermonde construction and the separation argument below provide the needed hypotheses and conclusion directly here; no general-step extension is presumed.\n\n## 4. Full dual attainment by finite-dimensional separation\n\nLet T_ij(G) denote the gradient/position part of Q_ij, so Q_ij=f_i-f_j+T_ij(G). Consider the open convex set of triples (a,b,c) for which some G>=0 and some f satisfy\n\n    a_ij>Q_ij(f,G), b>G_00-1, c<2f_n.\n\nThe point (0,0,q), where q is the finite primal optimum, is outside this set. A separating hyperplane, using the independent upward variations of a,b and downward variation of c, has nonnegative coefficients lambda_ij, q_0, alpha, not all zero, and yields\n\n    sum lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G)+q_0(G_00-1)-alpha*2f_n >= -alpha*q\n\nfor all G>=0 and all f. The strict primal point constructed above rules out alpha=0: substituting it would make the left side strictly negative whenever lambda or q_0 is nonzero. Thus normalize alpha=1.\n\nThe arbitrary f coordinates force\n\n    out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n}.\n\nThe arbitrary PSD matrix G then forces\n\n    M(q_0,lambda,h)=q_0 E_00+sum lambda_ij T_ij >=0.\n\nTaking G=f=0 gives q_0<=q. Conversely the flow and PSD constraints give an upper certificate W_n(h)<=q_0. Therefore q_0=q and the full dual is attained.\n\nThis argument establishes the direction needed here: at a minimizing positive schedule there is a certificate with q exactly equal to its true worst-case value. A feasible upper certificate by itself is never interpreted as a lower bound.\n\n## 5. A strictly feasible certificate at every finite h\n\nA regularity direction is needed because the optimal certificate can have many zero multipliers and singular PSD slack. We now construct a certificate with all directed multipliers positive and M positive definite at the same fixed h.\n\nFor each finite i, adding equal weight to lambda_{i,*} and lambda_{*,i} preserves the flow. Their quadratic sum is\n\n    Q_{i,*}+Q_{*,i}=||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>.\n\nLet H_max=max_t h_t, r=1/(2H_max+3), and w_i=r^(2i)>0. The gradient block A_w of the weighted sum of these cycles has diagonal w_i and, for i>t, off-diagonal w_i h_t/2. With D=diag(w_i), the matrix D^(-1/2) A_w D^(-1/2) has diagonal one and off-diagonal h_t r^(i-t)/2. Each row's absolute off-diagonal sum is at most H_max*r/(1-r)<1/2. Hence A_w is positive definite, as follows also by bounding each cross term with the two corresponding squared coordinates.\n\nStart with lambda_{n,*}=2, and add weight one to every directed finite-to-finite pair. Those paired edges are flow-balanced. Next add K w_i to each of lambda_{i,*} and lambda_{*,i}. All directed multipliers are now positive and the required flow holds. For sufficiently large finite K, their gradient block A is positive definite, because K A_w dominates the fixed remaining symmetric matrix. The full matrix has form\n\n    M=[[q,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]],  mu_i=lambda_{i,*}.\n\nChoosing q>mu^T A^(-1)mu/4 makes M positive definite. This constructs strict feasibility with no assumptions on which multipliers of the optimum vanish.\n\n## 6. KKT without assumed nondegeneracy\n\nTake a global minimizing positive schedule h and an attained certificate (q,lambda). It is also a global minimizer of q over the joint variables (q,lambda,h), subject to flow, lambda>=0 and M>=0: every such certificate upper-bounds a true W_n(h)>=W_n^*.\n\nHere is the finite-dimensional separation argument giving the needed multipliers. Restrict directions d to the linear flow-null space. Let J be the set of zero lambda entries, and let K_0=ker M. If a direction satisfies\n\n    dq<0, d lambda_e>0 for e in J,\n    (DM[d]) restricted to K_0 is positive definite,\n\nthen a sufficiently small positive step along it is feasible and improves q. To justify the matrix assertion, decompose into K_0 and its orthogonal complement. The complementary block of M is positive definite, the kernel block has a positive first-order term, and cross blocks are first order. The Schur complement subtracts only a second-order term. The dependence on h and lambda is polynomial, so its Taylor remainder is also second order. Positivity of h persists for sufficiently small steps. Such a descent direction is therefore impossible.\n\nSeparating the image of the direction space from the open cone defined by these three strict inequalities gives an objective multiplier alpha>=0, numbers r_e>=0 supported on J, and a PSD matrix G supported on K_0, not all zero, annihilating every flow-null direction:\n\n    alpha*dq - sum r_e*d lambda_e - <G,DM[d]> =0.\n\nMove from the optimal certificate toward the strictly feasible certificate from section 5 while holding h fixed. That direction has every d lambda_e>0 on J and has positive compression of DM to K_0. Substituting it rules out alpha=0, unless r=G=0, which would contradict nontrivial separation. Normalize alpha=1.\n\nExtending the annihilator by flow multipliers written as -f_i gives the coefficient identities\n\n    G_00=1,\n    f_i-f_j+T_ij(G)=-r_ij<=0,\n    lambda_ij r_ij=0,\n    M G=0,\n    <G,partial M/partial h_t>=0 for every t.\n\nThe PSD matrix G is consequently a legal normalized primal Gram matrix by section 3. The interpolation inequalities and lambda complementarity imply sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0. Taking the trace of M G then gives q=2f_n. Thus G realizes an actual worst-case transcript. Degenerate lambda and singular M were allowed throughout.\n\nIf either J or K_0 is empty, omit its corresponding strict-cone factor and multiplier. The same argument applies in these zero-dimensional cases.\n\n## 7. The resulting quantitative identities\n\nLet x_0,g_0,...,g_n be Euclidean vectors with Gram matrix G. Expanding the derivative with respect to h_t gives exactly\n\n    partial/partial h_t [sum lambda_ij T_ij]\n      = <g_t,\n          sum_{i>t}(mu_i+sum_{j<=t}lambda_ij)g_i\n          -sum_{i<=t}(sum_{j>t}lambda_ij)g_i >.\n\nThe stationary equations are therefore (S) with z_t defined in (Z), including every edge that crosses the chronological cut t.\n\nThe x_0 row of M G=0 and G_00=1 give\n\n    sum_i mu_i <x_0,g_i>=2q.                                  (X)\n\nThe full quadratic trace can be expanded as\n\n    0=<M,G>\n     =q-sum_i mu_i<x_0,g_i>\n       +sum_{t<n}h_t<g_t,z_t>\n       +(1/2)sum_{i,j}lambda_ij||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nUsing (S) and (X) yields the announced energy identity (E). The energy is nonnegative, and all chronological cut terms have been justified individually rather than discarded.\n\nThese equations constrain both a full feasible dual certificate and a full feasible primal transcript at an actual global minimizer. They do not supply a uniform numerical comparison with the Zhang--Jiang recurrence yet.\n\n## 8. Exact one-step primal-dual check\n\nFor n=1, h_0=3/2, q=1/4, take\n\n    lambda_{0,*}=lambda_{1,*}=lambda_{1,0}=1,\n\nwith all other multipliers zero. Direct expansion yields\n\n    M = v v^T, v=(1/2,-1,-1).\n\nA matching stationary primal Gram matrix is\n\n    G=[[1,1/3,1/6],\n       [1/3,1/6,0],\n       [1/6,0,1/12]],\n    (f_0,f_1)=(1/4,1/8).\n\nIt is the convex combination with weights 8/9 and 1/9 of two legal one-dimensional transcripts, each starting at x_0=1:\n\n* Huber threshold 1/4: (g_0,g_1)=(1/4,1/4), (f_0,f_1)=(7/32,1/8).\n* Quadratic f(x)=x^2/2: (g_0,g_1)=(1,-1/2), (f_0,f_1)=(1/2,1/8).\n\nThe direct sum after coordinate scaling by the square roots of the weights is itself one legal two-dimensional instance, so the combination is not merely a formal averaging device. In detail, for weights a,b and scalar functions H,Q, use F(u,v)=a H(u/sqrt(a))+b Q(v/sqrt(b)) and initial point (sqrt(a),sqrt(b)); this preserves smoothness one and gives exactly the displayed Gram matrix and values.\n\nThe full matrix equality M G=0 holds. The cut vector is z_0=2g_1 and <g_0,g_1>=0, verifying stationarity. The directed energy is\n\n    (1/2)(||g_0||^2+||g_1||^2+||g_1-g_0||^2)\n      =1/6+1/12=1/4=q.\n\nGlobal one-step optimality follows directly: the Huber lower bound is at least 1/4 for h_0<=3/2, and the quadratic lower bound (h_0-1)^2 is at least 1/4 for h_0>=3/2. The displayed certificate attains 1/4.\n\nA hostile check is useful: the pure Huber transcript is worst-case for this very same optimal schedule and dual certificate, but has <g_0,g_1>=1/16 and fails stationarity. Thus it is invalid to assume that an arbitrary active worst-case function supplies the stationary primal matrix. The multiplier-separation construction selects a suitable primal matrix, and an admissible direct sum may be essential.\n\n## 9. Exact remaining obstruction\n\nThe current identities do not force q to equal the sum-based Huber level, do not show lambda_{*,i}=0 at a global minimizer, and do not imply that all temporal gradients are orthogonal. Each such additional simplification would require its own proof or counterexample test.\n\nA route to the target would still need a quantitative estimate combining the full PSD slack, all interpolation inequalities, the cut equations (S), and the energy (E), uniformly in horizon, whose constant matches the exact recurrence. Merely defining that missing estimate is not a result. The package above establishes a candidate rigorous interface at genuine global minimizers, with exact feasible-data checks, and records the current obstruction.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "c267d75cd115105d", "branch_id": "max2_global_stationary_energy", "artifact": "research/max2_stationary_energy.md", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "partial", "solved_candidate_subgoals": ["global minimizer attainment", "zero optimal steps excluded", "primal and dual strict feasibility", "full KKT with degenerate multipliers", "cut orthogonality and energy identity", "exact one-step data check"], "key_stuck_points": ["No uniform quantitative comparison of the full stationary system with the exact Zhang-Jiang recurrence."], "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "inconclusive", "summary": "Pure Huber transcript at the exact one-step optimum fails stationarity; an 8/9 Huber plus 1/9 quadratic direct sum satisfies it. No justification for further scalar or gradient-orthogonality reduction."}, "evidence_status": "unverified; no additional verifier allowance"}, "glossary": {"Poly_i": "The ordered prefix polynomial product_{t<i}(1-h_t z).", "M": "The full dual PSD certificate matrix on (x_0,g_0,...,g_n).", "G": "The full normalized legal primal Gram matrix selected by conic stationarity.", "lambda_ij": "Nonnegative directed smooth-convex interpolation multipliers with out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n}.", "mu_i": "The optimum-directed multiplier lambda_{i,*}.", "z_t": "The complete chronological-cut adjoint vector defined in equation (Z) of the proof.", "Q_ij": "Signed smooth-convex interpolation expression f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, constrained nonpositive.", "r_ij": "Nonnegative KKT multipliers for lambda_ij>=0, equal to -Q_ij on the selected primal data.", "C_n": "Finite fixed-horizon compactness bound from Lagrange interpolation at n+1 positive quadratic eigenvalues."}}
{"id": "29ee3816ba260cf9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:54:58.868489+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Reset-doubling family closed as a direct negative construction by an exact DP comparison, including every small seed depth: W_n(q^(k,j))>U_n^ZJ for all k,j>=0. A feasible asymmetric split in the original dominant DP has denominator strictly larger than the candidate's universal Huber denominator. This unverified package answers the route-screening need without claiming that the quadratic spectral interval was controlled.", "evidence": "# The exact DP already dominates every reset-doubling candidate\n\nStatus: self-contained unverified route rejection. Uses only the universal Huber lower bound in accepted fact 0be8b54e0a33381c and elementary algebra of the original DP. No new verification allocation is requested.\n\nFor every k,j>=0, the reset-doubling schedule q^(k,j) from research/max4_reset_seed_boundary_certificate.md satisfies\nW_(len q^(k,j))(q^(k,j)) > U_(len q^(k,j))^ZJ.\nThus the entire stated family fails as a direct negative construction, including the smaller seed depths left open by the asymmetric-Huber test. No assertion about other richer joins or a modified family is made.\n\nNotation: lambda=1+sqrt(2), A=lambda^k, B=beta_k-1, R=A+B+2, A=2B^2+B. The length n and shifted sum of the candidate are\nn+1=2^j(2^k+2), 1+sum q^(k,j)=lambda^j R.\nWrite P_n=S_circle(n)+1 and D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 (D_n is the benchmark B_n, renamed here only to avoid conflict with the seed parameter B). The shifted joins are\nF(X,Y)=(X+Y+sqrt(X^2+6XY+Y^2))/2,\nG(X,D)=2X+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8XD)/2.\nBoth are increasing in their arguments. F(X,X)=lambda X.\n\nFirst, D_n>=2P_n-1 for all n. To check the needed comparison, take X,Y>=1 and C=F(X,Y), so C^2-(X+Y)C-XY=0. The polynomial whose larger root is G(X,2Y-1) is\nq(z)=z^2-(4X+2Y-1)z+4X^2.\nDirect substitution gives\nq(2C-1)=2[X-(2X+1)(C-X-Y)].\nHere C-X-Y=XY/C. Moreover C<X+2Y<=Y(2X+1), since 2XY-X-Y>=0 for X,Y>=1. Hence q(2C-1)<0. Therefore G(X,2Y-1)>2F(X,Y)-1. Induct on n along a maximizing primitive split and use the increasing dominant join. The case n=0 is equality. This proves the claim without assuming that the dominant DP literally lists every primitive schedule.\n\nBalanced primitive splits also give P_(2^r-1)>=lambda^r for each r>=0. For our n, use the legal dominant split\nm=2^(k+j)-1, ell=2^(j+1)-1, n=m+ell+1.\nIt follows that\nD_n >= G(lambda^(k+j),2lambda^(j+1)-1)\n     = lambda^j G(A,2lambda-u), where u=lambda^(-j) in (0,1].\n\nIt remains to compare this with the candidate's Huber denominator 2lambda^j R-1. Set d=2lambda-u and c=8-2lambda-u. We have d>1, c>0, d-c=4lambda-8>0, and d+c=8-2u>0. Using A=2B^2+B gives the exact identity\n\nd^2+8Ad-(4B+c)^2\n=16(d-1)B^2+8(d-c)B+(d-c)(d+c)>0.\n\nAll quantities under comparison are positive. Taking square roots and substituting into G gives\nG(A,2lambda-u)>2A+2B+4-u=2R-u.\nConsequently D_n>2lambda^j R-1.\n\nFinally the Huber lower bound from fact_id 0be8b54e0a33381c yields\nW_n(q^(k,j)) >=1/(2lambda^j R-1)>1/D_n=U_n^ZJ.\nThis is a pointwise rejection for every member of the concrete family, so it is stronger than merely ruling out a fixed asymptotic improvement. The same sum argument applies to a permutation of any such schedule, but does not establish how to modify its steps successfully.\n\nRelation to the requested spectral screening in master_guidance ac69664acf0dee30:\nFor a quadratic f_mu(x)=mu x^2/2, 0<mu<=1, the candidate has the exact scalar recursion\np_(j+1)(mu)=p_j(mu)^2 [1-(1+lambda^(-1)R_j)mu], R_j=lambda^j R,\np_0(mu)=p_pi^(k)(mu)(1-beta_k mu)(1-mu).\nEquivalently,\np_j(mu)=p_0(mu)^(2^j) product_(r=0)^(j-1)[1-(1+lambda^(r-1)R)mu]^(2^(j-1-r)).\nThe vanishing at mu=1 does not control the rest of this interval. No full-interval stability theorem is claimed. Since the exact profile comparison above already rules out every member as a direct negative schedule, neither a numerical spectrum sweep nor a large boundary-certificate search is needed to make this route decision.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_reset_boundary", "research_path": "research/max4_reset_exact_profile_rejection.md", "fact_ids": ["0be8b54e0a33381c"], "finding_ids": ["47e76f047d585d7a", "c204acf5cc773129"], "guidance_id": "ac69664acf0dee30", "status": "closed as a direct negative family; new joins remain open"}, "glossary": {"lambda": "1+sqrt(2).", "A": "lambda^k, shifted mass of the seed's silver prefix.", "B": "beta_k-1, positive solution A=2B^2+B; local seed symbol distinct from benchmark B_n.", "R": "A+B+2, shifted sum of the reset seed.", "P_n": "S_circle(n)+1.", "D_n": "The exact benchmark denominator B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, renamed locally to distinguish it from seed parameter B.", "F": "Shifted primitive join defined in the evidence.", "G": "Dominant denominator join defined in the evidence.", "u": "lambda^(-j).", "p_j": "GD endpoint multiplier on scalar quadratic f_mu for schedule q^(k,j)."}}
{"id": "3dd8ba794379225a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:00:44.620618+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "History-aware P5 extension: exact constant-tail optimizer and finite checkpoint-lookahead actions. The new tree contains the accepted ray functional and all old P5 leaves, with a strict exact improvement9/2 ->55/12 on(1,5,3,1).", "evidence": "# P5 extension: exact history-aware plateau actions\nStatus: new complete construction and quantitative tests; unverified. No new verifier allocation requested. The uniform exact-profile comparison is still BLOCKED.\n\n## 1. History and orientation\nWork at L=1, minimizer0, initial point1. A finite history H contains(0,0) and gradient knots(y,v) satisfying 0<=v-w<=y-z whenever y>=z. Define\nell_H(r)=max_(y,v in H)[v-(y-r)_+],\nu_H(r)=min_(y,v in H)[v+(r-y)_+].\nThe extrema of nondecreasing1-Lipschitz functions are again nondecreasing1-Lipschitz. The history inequalities show that both envelopes interpolate every knot. Hence a new gradient at r is legal exactly in[ell_H(r),u_H(r)]. Integrating either envelope gives a global convex1-smooth function with minimizer0. For a completed path ending at x, its largest possible normalized value is\nPhi(H,x)=2 integral_0^x u_H(r)dr for x>=0,\nPhi(H,x)=-2 integral_x^0 ell_H(r)dr for x<=0.\nThis follows by the pointwise envelope inequalities and is attained by integrating the indicated entire envelope. Every previous queried gradient is preserved.\n\nFor a nonzero current point, reflect if necessary so that it becomes a positive radius x: replace every knot(y,v) by(sigma y,sigma v), where sigma is the sign of the current point. All formulas below concern this reflected positive coordinate. Multiply selected magnitudes by sigma to obtain the original gradient. At x=0 choose gradient0.\n\n## 2. Exact feasibility of a constant-gradient future block\nFix a nonnegative proposed block mass S. A magnitude q>=0 can be imposed on the entire interval[x-Sq,x] consistently with the old history exactly when\nq belongs to I(H,x,S):=[ell_H(x), C(H,x,S)],\nwhere\nC(H,x,S)=min_(y,v in H) max{v,(x-y+v)/(1+S)}.                 (1)\nThe interval may be empty.\n\nTo prove this, add endpoint knots(x,q) and(z,q), where z=x-Sq. Monotonicity of the envelopes makes their joint feasibility equivalent to\nq>=ell_H(x), q<=u_H(z).\nThe origin knot implies that the latter inequality with q>0 forces z>=q>0. For q=0, z=x>0. Thus the imposed plateau stays on the selected ray. For one old knot the upper inequality is\nq<=v+(x-y-Sq)_+.\nThe continuous function on the right minus q is strictly decreasing, with unique zero\nmax{v,(x-y+v)/(1+S)}.\nIndeed if x-y<=Sv the zero is v; otherwise it is(x-y+v)/(1+S). Intersecting these inequalities proves(1). The origin knot also gives ell_H(x)>=0 and C(H,x,S)<=x/(1+S).\n\nOnce both endpoint knots are feasible, the derivative has to be constant q between them; adding any intermediate knot with value q preserves legality. Hence every nonnegative step block of total mass S follows this plateau, in its actual chronological order. No individual stepsize bound is needed.\n\n## 3. Exact optimal completion by a constant-gradient tail\nFor a tail of total mass S, any q in I(H,x,S) has exact maximal normalized terminal value\nA(H,x,S;q)=2 integral_0^(x-Sq) min{u_H(r),q}dr.             (2)\nAdding the two endpoint knots makes the new upper envelope on[0,x-Sq] exactly min{u_H(r),q}. Integrate that global new envelope. The resulting function attains(2), realizes the entire constant-gradient tail and every old gradient, and has initial squared distance1.\n\nThis optimum is a concrete finite calculation from the old knots. The function u_H is continuous piecewise affine with slopes0 or1 and finitely many pieces. On a rising piece write u_H(r)=r-d. For q whose level lies in that piece, put\nE=integral_0^(q+d)u_H(r)dr-q^2/2;\nE is constant as q varies through that rising piece. Formula(2) becomes\nA=2E+2q(x-d)-(1+2S)q^2.                                 (3)\nIts only interior stationary point is\nq=(x-d)/(1+2S).                                          (4)\nAn optimizing q can therefore be selected by checking:\n- the endpoints of the nonempty interval I(H,x,S);\n- the finitely many plateau heights of u_H that belong to I;\n- the values(4) whose level lies in the associated rising piece and in I.\nThe formula also covers zero levels by including the interval endpoints. Comparing these finitely many exact values selects q_stop(H,x,S). No numerical enumeration of trajectories or optimization over all functions is being assumed.\n\nThe offsets d and retained areas E in(3) are compulsory history data. The former proposal x/(1+2S) silently replaced d by0 and need not be optimal even when it is feasible.\n\n## 4. A specified finite enlarged action tree\nAt time t, with current point x_t and full gradient history H_t, retain the old P5 actions:\n(a) the signed steep envelope;\n(b) the projection of x_t/(1+2S_t) onto the complete feasible interval, where S_t=sum_(r=t)^(n-1)h_r.\n\nAdd the following actions:\n(c) the signed value q_stop for S=S_t, whenever I is nonempty;\n(d) for each future checkpoint j in{t,...,n-1}, the signed value C(H,|x_t|,S_(t,j)), whenever I is nonempty, where\nS_(t,j)=sum_(r=t)^(j-1)h_r.\nHistory is reflected as in Section1 for(c),(d). Choose any listed action, add its knot, and apply the given h_t. At the end use Phi.\n\nThis is an explicit finite tree, with at most n+3 listed actions at each time, at most(n+3)^n leaves, and no free continuum of gradient choices. It starts from H_0={(0,0)}, x_0=1, rather than requiring a preselected seed. Every action is pathwise legal by the formulas above. Thus every completed leaf is a single globally defined legal one-dimensional function, and its displayed payoff is at most W_n(h).\n\nLet M_new(h) be the maximum leaf payoff. A genuine probability law whose expectation loses a uniformly vanishing factor is obtained by giving a leaf of value Z>=0 mass proportional to Z^(n^2), for n>=1. If all values vanish, use any law. With N leaves and p=n^2, Jensen's inequality for a->a^(1+1/p) gives\n[sum Z^(p+1)]/[sum Z^p] >= [N^(-1)sum Z^p]^(1/p)\n >= N^(-1/p) max Z.\nConsequently the expected normalized payoff T_new satisfies\n(n+3)^(-1/n) M_new(h) <= T_new(h) <= M_new(h) <= W_n(h).   (5)\nThe factor tends to1 independently of h. This distribution can be sampled as conditional branch probabilities using sums of descendant pth powers; their products telescope to the leaf law. This is a symbolic specification, not a computation performed here. The schedule is fixed before the law or any function is chosen.\n\nFor n=0 take f(x)=x^2/2, with normalized error1.\n\n## 5. Inclusion of the old policy and the accepted ray construction\nThe tree contains all original forced-prefix P5 leaves. Indeed choosing checkpoint j initially gives C=1/(1+P_j), where P_j=sum_(r<j)h_r. If q=C was chosen for a block of mass S and a first step of mass a<=S has been performed, q is still feasible for the residual block from x-aq. The new knot(x,q) gives the residual upper bound C_new<=q, while feasibility gives C_new>=q. Thus C_new=q. Repeating the same lookahead checkpoint keeps exactly the old constant-gradient prefix. At the checkpoint itself S=0, so C=u_H at the query, again equal to q. Thereafter the two original actions are retained.\n\nIt also contains the exact alternating-ray leaves in accepted fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a. At a new outward stage let(A_old,w_old) be the previous record on that ray, d=A_old-w_old, and let the current radius satisfy x=d+(1+S)m with m>=w_old. Every earlier same-ray knot(y,v) satisfies y-v<=d and v<=w_old. The opposite-ray knots satisfy y-v<=0<=d. Formula(1) therefore gives C>=m, while the record knot gives C<=max(w_old,(x-d)/(1+S))=m. Thus the future-checkpoint action is exactly m. The residual-block argument then follows every plateau in that fact. At the terminal stage the stored knots force the same old upper derivative envelope as its slope1 ramps and flat segments. Formula(3) has its exact retained area e_term and offset d; q_stop is precisely its q_opt. Therefore\nM_new(h)>=F_ray_n(h)>=K_n(h)\nusing the accepted fact, without discarding any ray feasibility condition.\n\nThis inclusion is a comparison of constructions, not a second independent claim that the ray family solves the target. The added actions are also available at non-record states, and the old P5 inner-return branches are preserved.\n\n## 6. Exact strict improvement over the best old P5 leaf\nTake h=(1,5,3,1). In the old P5 tree, seed j=0 reaches0 at its first step; seed j=3 also reaches0 at the last step. Seed j=2 has c=1/7 and its best last-step leaf gives4/147. For seed j=1, c=1/2 and the points through its forced prefix are1,1/2,-2.\n\nAt -2 the old interior choice is -2/9, because the remaining mass is4. It stays on this plateau and yields4/9. The old steep choice is -2 and sends the point to4. At4, the old positive upper envelope is\nu(r)=r on[0,1/2], 1/2 on[1/2,1], r-1/2 forr>=1.\nThe last old steep action takes gradient7/2 and ends at1/2, giving1/4. The last old interior action takes gradient4/3 and ends at8/3. Its maximal normalized value is\n1/2+2(4/3)(7/2)-3(4/3)^2=9/2.\nThese cases exhaust the old tree, so its maximum leaf payoff is exactly9/2; its K_4 fallback is at most4/9.\n\nAt the same history, the new optimal tail uses d=1/2, E=1/4, S=1. Formula(4) gives q=7/6, which lies in I=[1/2,7/4]. The new terminal value is\n1/2+(7/2)^2/3=55/12 >9/2.\nA complete global realizing derivative is\nq_global(x)=x for x<=1/2;\n1/2 for1/2<=x<=1;\nx-1/2 for1<=x<=5/3;\n7/6 forx>=5/3.\nIt is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. Its trajectory is\n1,1/2,-2,4,17/6,\nand direct integration gives normalized error55/12. The terminal gain over9/2 is exactly1/12. This verifies a real geometric improvement, not a change in averaging weights.\n\n## 7. Checkpoint lookahead test\nFor h=(1,5,1,9,1), choose the checkpoint5 from the initial state, giving magnitude1/2 through its prefix. At the resulting point-2 choose future checkpoint9; the ordinary intervening mass is1, so formula(1) gives magnitude1. The next two points are-1 and8. The exact tail optimizer at8 has d=1/2,E=1/4,S=1 and q=5/2, giving\n2E+(8-d)^2/3=77/4.\nThis reproduces the accepted fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a example.
