The old two-action rule at-2 offered only magnitudes2 and2/23; neither was the needed1. The new action is derived from complete-history feasibility, not inserted to fit this example.\n\n## Target status\nNo uniform comparison of M_new(h) with U_n^ZJ has been proved. Equation(5) removes probability-weight loss; the new formulas repair two concrete omissions of the geometry. A theorem that M_new(h)>=(1-o(1))U_n^ZJ uniformly for every finite nonnegative h would still be a target-strength theorem and remains BLOCKED. The finite examples above do not establish such a statement or an asymptotic negative answer.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_history_aware_branching.md", "fact_ids": ["1fca3e5dd555cb4a"], "prior_findings": ["54496c04e3d34eb2", "fa7d5c5f2051df81", "30fd3be213bc77e2"], "status": "full unverified derivation and exact tests; no additional verification requested", "target_gap": "Uniform geometry/profile comparison remains theorem-strength and BLOCKED"}, "glossary": {"I(H,x,S)": "Exact feasible interval for a constant-gradient block from positive radiusx with massS and reflected complete historyH.", "C(H,x,S)": "Upper endpoint min_(y,v in H) max(v,(x-y+v)/(1+S)) of that interval.", "A(H,x,S;q)": "Exact maximal normalized terminal value of the specified constant-gradient completion.", "q_stop": "A maximizing tail magnitude selected from the explicitly given finite list of breakpoints and quadratic stationary points.", "M_new": "Maximum leaf payoff in the enlarged finite P5 action tree.", "T_new": "Expected payoff when leaves are weighted by their payoff to the powern^2.", "d": "Horizontal offset of a rising piece u_H(r)=r-d, local to the current tail formula.", "E": "Integral of the old upper envelope through a levelq minusq^2/2, constant on each rising piece."}}
{"id": "2599c72fdf8542a0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:03:34.094908+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Exact screen for genuine two-bridge fusion: no primitive join [a,1+cA,a,1+dA,a] with c,d>0 and a quadratic-balanced seed can beat the existing three-copy binary capacity F(1,1+sqrt(2)). Explicit primitive cut tests Mc<=2+d and Md<=2+c, together with Mcd<=1 from the quadratic, imply M^4-4M^3-1<=0. The tempting c=2/3 candidate is refuted by f'=clip(x,-1/4,1), with V=-(25/144)(1+sqrt(2))^2. Unverified package, not a target result.", "evidence": "# Exact optimality screen for a three-copy, two-bridge primitive join\n\nStatus: complete unverified construction-class obstruction, not a statement about unrestricted minimax optimality. No fact submission under the current allocation.\n\nA concrete candidate was\nc=2/3, d=(5sqrt(7)-11)/6, M=3+c+d=(11+5sqrt(7))/6,\nJ(a)=[a,1+cA,a,1+dA,a], A=1+sum a.\nIt has cdM=1 and M>F(1,1+sqrt(2)), where F is the exact shifted primitive join. Thus it passes the full-curvature product balance and suggests a larger primitive capacity than two ordinary binary joins. It is nevertheless invalid. The general argument below screens all positive c,d in this motif and proves that none can exceed the existing binary factor when the seed is quadratic-balanced.\n\nDefinitions. At L=1 write x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). For a nonnegative schedule h with sum s=R-1 define its saturated intrinsic primitive energy\nV_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sR||g_n||^2.\nThe primitive property means V_h>=0 on every smooth convex trajectory. The following necessary tests are explicit legal one-dimensional functions, so no conjectural composition theorem is used.\n\n1. An exact cut constraint for every primitive schedule.\nChoose any step b>=1, let P be the sum of preceding steps, and T the sum of following steps. Set a_minus=(b-1)/(T+1). Use f'(x)=clip(x,-a_minus,1), f(0)=0, and x_0=P+1. Its prefix stays on the positive affine ray and arrives at1. The b-step arrives at -(b-1). All tail iterates stay on the negative affine ray, and the final point is -a_minus. This follows from nonnegative partial sums and (T+1)a_minus=b-1. The degenerate b=1 case is harmless.\nAt each sample, Q_(i,star)=f_i-g_i x_i+g_i^2/2=0, since the function is quadratic in its core and affine with the matching offset on each tail. A direct expansion using the GD recursion yields\nV_h=x_0^2-x_n^2-2s f_n-sR g_n^2+2 sum_i h_i Q_(i,star).\nThus this trajectory gives V_h=(P+1)^2-R^2 a_minus^2. If h is primitive it follows that\n(b-1)R <= (P+1)(T+1).                                      (CUT)\nThis is a necessary primitive-energy inequality, not merely the objective single-cut lower bound.\n\n2. Full-curvature condition.\nFor f(x)=x^2/2 and x_0=1, all Q_(i,star)=0, and V_h=1-R^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2. Hence a primitive schedule must obey\nR |product_i(1-h_i)| <=1.                                  (QUAD)\n\n3. The three-copy motif cannot exceed the ordinary two-join factor.\nLet a be any nonnegative block with shifted sum A>0 and |product_i(1-a_i)|=1/A. This includes each standard quadratic-balanced basic primitive block. Form J(a) with c,d>0 and put M=3+c+d, so its shifted sum is R=MA.\nThe two bridge instances of (CUT) give\nMc<=2+d, Md<=2+c.\nCondition (QUAD) gives Mcd<=1, because the endpoint quadratic multiplier has magnitude cd/A.\nWriting t=c+d=M-3 and u=(M-1)/(M+1), the cut conditions are exactly c,d<=u. If M<=4 there is already no gain over F(1,1+sqrt(2))>4. Suppose M>4. Both c,d lie in [t-u,u]. At a fixed sum t, the product c(t-c) is concave, so\ncd >= u(t-u)=(M-1)(M^2-3M-2)/(M+1)^2.\nCombining this with Mcd<=1 gives\nM^4-4M^3-1<=0.\nThis polynomial is strictly increasing for M>3, and has one root M_*>4. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2) and C=F(1,lambda). Its defining equation C^2-(1+lambda)C-lambda=0 gives lambda=C(C-1)/(C+1). Substitution into lambda^2-2lambda-1=0 gives C^4-4C^3-1=0. Therefore M_*=F(1,lambda), and necessarily\nM<=F(1,1+sqrt(2)).\nThe existing two-step binary parenthesizations of three copies attain this scalar factor. This argument rules out a larger primitive factor for the displayed three-copy motif, regardless of a proposed richer certificate.\n\n4. Exact hostile instance for the displayed new constants.\nTake a=[sqrt(2)], A=lambda, c=2/3 and d=(5sqrt(7)-11)/6. One has 0<d<2/3. Use f'(x)=clip(x,-1/4,1), f(0)=0, and x_0=A. The first sqrt(2)-step reaches1; the first bridge reaches -cA. Every remaining step stays on the negative affine ray because their total is T=(2+d)A-1 and\n-cA+T/4 <= -1/4 iff d<=2/3.\nThe endpoint is x_5=-cA+[(2+d)A-1]/4. On the negative affine ray, the primitive terminal expression x_5^2+2s f_5+sR g_5^2 equals (x_5-s/4)^2. Since s=MA-1,\nx_5-s/4=-A[c+(1+c)/4]=-(13/12)A.\nThus V_J=A^2-(13A/12)^2=-(25/144)A^2<0. This is a direct failure of the proposed primitive certificate in dimension one. It does not rely on reversing schedules or on a general H-duality assertion.\n\nSearch record and scope.\nMatlas returned unrelated statements for the requested primitive/H-duality composition search. The downloaded GSW paper arXiv:2410.16249 was checked: its Section 3.1 states H-duality for basic schedules, so no unsupported universal reversal closure was assumed. A tiny SDP check was considered, but cvxpy, numpy, scipy, clarabel, and scs are absent; no numerical solver, exhaustive search, or installation was run. The exact cut construction made one unnecessary.\n\nThe obstruction uses the seed's exact quadratic balance and the three-copy/two-bridge form. It does not close non-balanced seeds, independently chosen blocks, four-block joins, or a direct objective certificate. Those would require their own feasible certificate and exact-profile comparison. Any proposed reopening must identify which hypothesis changes and retain the cut test as a mandatory hostile example.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_direct_ternary_join", "research_path": "research/max4_ternary_cut_capacity.md", "status": "refuted as a capacity-improving primitive join", "used_prior_findings": ["b1615bf75df9b476"], "verification_status": "unverified; no new submission"}, "glossary": {"V_h": "Saturated intrinsic primitive energy defined in the evidence.", "R": "One plus the sum of the tested schedule.", "s": "R-1, sum of the tested schedule.", "Q_istar": "f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2 with minimizer0 and f_star0.", "P": "Sum of steps before the selected cut.", "T": "Sum of steps after the selected cut.", "a_minus": "Negative affine-ray gradient magnitude (b-1)/(T+1).", "A": "One plus the sum of the block a.", "J": "Three-copy two-bridge schedule defined in the evidence.", "M": "3+c+d, the shifted-sum multiplier of J.", "M_star": "Unique root above4 of M^4-4M^3-1; equal to F(1,1+sqrt(2))."}}
{"id": "a62e3118ed129c35", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:09:47.126992+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Independent C6 check of the already rejected reset-doubling lane: the first doubling with seed depthk>=7 has W>= (sqrt2-1)^2/96, uniformly in k.", "evidence": "This is an independent, unverified algebraic specialization of accepted fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a, not a new verifier allocation. It is redundant for route closure after max4's29ee3816ba260cf9, so no further effort on this family is proposed.\n\nSet lambda=1+sqrt2, sigma=1/lambda, A=lambda^k, B=(sqrt(1+8A)-1)/4 and R=A+B+2. Then A=2B^2+B. The reset seed is q0=[pi^(k),B+1,1], where pi^(k) has length2^k-1 and sumA-1. These length and mass formulas follow inductively from pi^(0)=empty and pi^(r+1)=[pi^(r),1+lambda^(r-1),pi^(r)], using lambda^2=2lambda+1. The first doubled schedule is q1=[q0,1+sigma R,q0], of length N=2^(k+1)+3.\n\nIn fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a select two checkpoints, the first occurrence of B+1 and the central1+sigma R. Its exact block sums are s0=A-1,s1=1,s2=R-1. Thus\nm0=1/A, m1=B/(2A), O1=(A-1)/A,\nz_term=[sigma R B/2-A+1]/A, e_term=(A-1)/A^2, m_old=1/A.\nIf B>=6lambda, then the numerator of z_term is at least3R-A+1>2R-1. Consequently q_opt=z_term/(2R-1)>1/A, and all the terminal and nonterminal feasibility conditions in the accepted fact hold. Its exact legal error is\nV_k=2(A-1)/A^2 + [sigma R B/2-A+1]^2/[A^2(2R-1)].\n\nSince R>A and sigma B>=6, the numerator is at least R(sigma B/2-1)>=sigma R B/4. It follows that\nV_k>=sigma^2 R B^2/(32A^2)>=sigma^2 B^2/(32A)>=sigma^2/96,\nusing A=2B^2+B<=3B^2 for B>=1.\nFor every k>=7, B>6lambda: lambda^7=169lambda+70>150lambda+72=2(6lambda)^2+6lambda, and the map B->2B^2+B is increasing. Therefore the first-doubling subsequence has a fixed positive error lower bound for everyk>=7.\n\nAccepted fact0be8b54e0a33381c gives 1/U_N^ZJ>=2N+1. Hence W_N(q1)/U_N^ZJ >=(sigma^2/96)(2N+1), which diverges as k grows. This strengthens the size of this specific hostile error but is not needed after the separate exact-DP rejection of the entire family. It is not a lower bound for arbitrary h and not a solution of(P).", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1fca3e5dd555cb4a", "0be8b54e0a33381c"], "related_findings": ["47e76f047d585d7a", "c204acf5cc773129", "29ee3816ba260cf9"], "status": "independent unverified check only; stronger route closure already posted by max4; no further work allocated here"}, "glossary": {"lambda": "Silver constant1+sqrt2, following the reset-family notation.", "sigma": "1/lambda.", "A": "lambda^k for the seed depth in this finding.", "B": "Positive solution of2B^2+B=A, equal to the reset seed's bridge minus1.", "R": "Shifted reset-seed step sumA+B+2.", "q0": "The reset seed[pi^(k),B+1,1].", "q1": "Its first balanced doubling[q0,1+sigma R,q0].", "V_k": "Exact two-checkpoint normalized error supplied by the accepted alternating-ray fact."}}
{"id": "1dc29ea19aa3894a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:10:48.665962+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "New exact joint-perturbation inequality: at a full stationary primal-dual tuple, any certificate descent must pay inactive-interpolation costs through a bilinear schedule-flow term. One-step q>tau data give actual full descent and show star-outgoing flow cannot simply be removed.", "evidence": "# Exact cost of a joint certificate perturbation above the Huber level\n\nStatus: unverified quantitative derivation, following the updated q>tau assignment. The historical GS interface is not re-proved. The initial verification allowance is already used.\n\n## 1. Definitions and the exact finite-perturbation inequality\n\nNormalize L=1, x_*=g_*=f_*=0. For a fixed positive length-n schedule h, let (q,lambda,G,f) be a full stationary primal-dual tuple. Explicitly:\n\n* lambda_ij>=0 for distinct i,j in I={*,0,...,n}, with out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n};\n* M(q,lambda,h)>=0 is the matrix of q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij after GD substitution;\n* G>=0 is the Gram matrix of (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), G_00=1, and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0;\n* M G=0 and lambda_ij Q_ij=0;\n* <g_t,z_t(lambda)>=0 for t<n, where\n  z_t(b)=sum_{i,j} b_ij(1_{t<i}-1_{t<j})g_i.\n  The indicator is zero for the star, and g_*=0.\n\nAll objects in these conditions refer to the SAME full legal transcript. No subset of these conditions is used as a replacement for the full system.\n\nSet sigma_ij=-Q_ij>=0. Consider ANY second feasible upper certificate (q',lambda',h'), where h'>=0 and lambda' has the same flow. The new certificate need not be optimal. Put a=h'-h and b=lambda'-lambda. Define the explicit bilinear form\n\n    B_G(a,b)=sum_{t<n} a_t <g_t,z_t(b)>.\n\nBecause M is affine separately in h and lambda, its exact finite expansion, paired with the original G, is\n\n    <M(q',lambda',h'),G>\n      = (q'-q)-sum_{i,j}lambda'_ij sigma_ij+B_G(a,b).           (1)\n\nTo verify the formula, the first-order h term vanishes by stationarity. The lambda term is sum b_ij T_ij(G), with T_ij=Q_ij-(f_i-f_j). Flow equality cancels the f differences, while sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0 changes sum b_ij Q_ij to -sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij. The only remaining mixed term is B_G(a,b). The q coefficient is G_00=1. There are no higher-order terms.\n\nBoth matrices in the left side are PSD. Therefore EVERY new feasible certificate satisfies\n\n    q'-q >= sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij - B_G(h'-h,lambda'-lambda). (2)\n\nThis is a concrete quantitative obstruction to a proposed joint descent. A descent by Delta=q-q'>0 must obey\n\n    B_G(h'-h,lambda'-lambda)\n       >= Delta+sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij.                       (3)\n\nIn particular:\n\n* Holding h fixed cannot lower q, as expected from matching primal feasibility.\n* Holding lambda fixed cannot lower q for ANY new h, not just for a small change: then both terms on the right of (2) vanish.\n* The gain from simultaneous motion has to overcome the exact cost of placing multiplier mass on interpolation inequalities that are strict for the old stationary primal.\n\nLet R_g=max_i ||g_i||. Direct Cauchy--Schwarz bounds give\n\n    |B_G(a,b)|<=R_g^2 ||a||_1 ||b||_1,\n    Delta+sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij\n       <=R_g^2 ||h'-h||_1 ||lambda'-lambda||_1                 (4)\n\nfor every feasible descent. Thus joint movement, not a single-block update, is essential. This is a necessary inequality, not a proof that a descent exists when q exceeds tau.\n\nFor a differentiable path through a stationary tuple, any first-order newly positive multiplier on an edge with sigma_ij>0 costs first order, while the useful bilinear term starts at second order. Hence a path with q'(0)=0 must have zero first derivative on every such inactive edge. This conclusion also follows directly from (2) by dividing by the path parameter.\n\n## 2. One-step q>tau certificates with unavoidable star-outgoing flow\n\nThe full one-step system provides a useful hostile test of attempts to remove lambda_{*,i} without moving the schedule.\n\nTake h_0=1+a with a>=1. The true worst-case value is q=a^2, certified by\n\n    lambda_{0,*}=2a^2,\n    lambda_{*,0}=2a^2-2,\n    lambda_{1,0}=2,\n\nand all other multipliers zero. The flow equations hold. Its entire PSD identity is\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2f_1+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n      =a^2||x_0-g_0||^2+||a g_0+g_1||^2.                    (5)\n\nThe quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 from x_0=1 gives g_0=1, g_1=-a, f_1=a^2/2 and makes the right side zero. Thus primal and dual match and W_1(1+a)=a^2. This value is strictly above tau=1/(2a+3) for a>=1.\n\nFor a>1, every tight certificate at this very same schedule needs nonzero star-outgoing flow. To prove this, suppose nu_i=lambda_{*,i}=0. Then mu_0+mu_1=2, where mu_i=lambda_{i,*}. Pairing the optimal PSD slack with the matching quadratic vector (1,1,-a) gives M(1,1,-a)^T=0. Its x_0 row yields\n\n    mu_0-a mu_1=2a^2.\n\nCombining with mu_0+mu_1=2 gives\n\n    mu_1=2(1-a)<0,\n\na contradiction. Thus a general-q proof cannot simply remove all star-outgoing multipliers while holding h and q fixed.\n\nThere IS an actual full feasible descent in this example. For any 0<epsilon<a-1, replace a by a-epsilon in all displayed variables. The nonnegative multipliers, both squares in (5), the chronological GD equation and the matching quadratic primal all remain valid. The objective drops by\n\n    a^2-(a-epsilon)^2=2a epsilon-epsilon^2>0.\n\nThis verifies a schedule-and-flow motion with the full cone and the same legal function, rather than just a formal linearized descent. These tuples are not stationary, so the example does not decide the hypothetical global-minimizer question.\n\nFor completeness, the path continues below a=1. For 1/2<=a<=1 take\n\n    lambda_{0,*}=2a, lambda_{1,*}=2(1-a), lambda_{1,0}=2a,\n\nall other multipliers zero, and q=a^2. The gradient block is [[2a,1],[1,1]], while mu=(2a,2(1-a)). Its Schur complement is\n\n    Z=(2a-1) [[1,1/a],[1/a,1/a^2]] >=0.\n\nThe quadratic again annihilates the full slack and attains q. Decreasing a keeps all constraints feasible until a=1/2, at which point q=tau=1/4. Thus the full one-step non-Huber region has an explicit piecewise-algebraic descent to the Huber-tight optimum. The change of formula at a=1 is necessary to preserve nonnegative multipliers.\n\n## 3. Why the general-q residual alone has not supplied the descent\n\nFor an arbitrary full feasible certificate, the earlier exact Huber evaluation is\n\n    eta=(1,tau e)^T M(1,tau e)\n       =q-tau-tau sum_i nu_i[1-tau(P_i+1)]>=0,\n    tau=1/(2S+1), S=sum h_t, P_i=sum_{t<i}h_t.\n\nIts residual estimates control M applied to this one explicit Huber vector. A feasible descent from a stationary certificate also has to control the ENTIRE nullspace of M and the cost of activating strict primal interpolation inequalities. Formula (2) makes the missing interaction concrete: a positive value of q-tau by itself neither gives an allowed first-order multiplier support nor a positive value of B_G large enough to pay the required costs.\n\nTwo attempted shortcuts therefore fail for explicit reasons:\n\n1. Freeze the optimal multipliers and use the Huber slack to move only h: equation (2) proves that q cannot decrease for any h'.\n2. Remove star-outgoing flow at fixed h and q: the exact a>1 example proves such a tight certificate need not exist.\n\nThese are restrictions on the indicated operations. They do not refute a general simultaneous feasible descent theorem at global minimizers. The old full stationary tuple must remain attached to (2); using source/cut/energy identities with a nonlegal Gram would invalidate the inequality.\n\nThe current open task is to build a simultaneous variation with a favorable explicit B_G and PSD feasibility from the specific GD structure, or obtain a different quantitative consequence from the complete matrix. No Huber-tightness or asymptotic profile comparison has been proved here.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_joint_descent_cost.md", "branch_id": "max2_huber_gap_descent", "status": "quantitative obstruction plus exact feasible descent calibration; general global-minimizer descent unresolved", "evidence_status": "unverified, no verifier call", "attempted_shortcuts": ["step-only change with fixed multipliers is rigorously blocked", "star-outgoing removal at fixed h,q is refuted for exact one-step h>2"]}, "glossary": {"sigma_ij": "Nonnegative slack -Q_ij of the original matching legal stationary primal.", "B_G": "The explicit bilinear variation sum_t a_t<g_t,z_t(b)> for a step change a and a flow change b, using the original legal Gram G.", "R_g": "Maximum gradient norm in that original Gram representation.", "mu_i": "Multiplier lambda_{i,*} toward the optimum.", "nu_i": "Multiplier lambda_{*,i} leaving the optimum.", "tau": "The canonical sum-based Huber value 1/(2 sum h_t+1)."}}
{"id": "d1d7cfb25e10bfc5", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:20:43.025475+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "The new recursive screening family is catastrophically bad on a coupled-coordinate witness: with fixed parameters epsilon=1/100, gamma=2099/20000 in the checked Ye–Liu construction, selecting every step above a fixed level gives W_(2^m-1)(H_m)>=(98/81)^(2^(m-r-3))/[2(1+s_r)] for all m>=r+4. This identifies a concrete separation from ray, quadratic, one-cap, and homogeneous two-curvature channels.", "evidence": "# Coupled-coordinate instability on the recursive screening family\n\nStatus: unverified source-based derivation, not a new accepted fact.\nWorker: max3. This is a companion to max3_recursive_channel_obstruction.md;\nit is not a negative answer to the minimax target.\n\n## Statement\n\nDefine c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_r=a c^r and\nH_0=(), H_m=(H_(m-1),b_(m-1),H_(m-1)). Its length is N_m=2^m-1 and\nits sum is s_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).\n\nThere is a fixed integer r, independent of m, for which the explicit\nYe--Liu coupled-coordinate construction yields\n\n    W_(N_m)(H_m) >= (98/81)^(2^(m-r-3)) / [2(1+s_r)]\n\nfor all m>=r+4. In particular this genuine worst-case error tends to infinity,\neven though the restricted ray, quadratic, one-cap, and two-curvature functionals\nin the companion candidate tend to zero. Both claims remain unverified here.\n\n## External input, definitions, and normalization\n\nThe source is Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for\nGradient Descent, arXiv:2609.09152v2, Section3.1, Lemmas3.1--3.2 and the\npre-weakening construction for Lemma2.2; its proof is AppendixA.1--A.3.\nThe PDF and extracted text are in this worker's downloads directory. The\ndefinitions, the two-coordinate proof, the global assembly, and the terminal\nobjective calculation have been read. A Matlas search for the local transfer\nstatement returned unrelated results, none used. The direct source is\nhttps://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09152 .\n\nThe exact input used is as follows. For epsilon,gamma>0 put\nchi=sqrt(1+2gamma+epsilon^2). There exist finite constants A_YL and B_YL,\ndepending only on those two parameters, such that for every finite nonnegative\nschedule and every chosen chronological checkpoint set with all checkpoint\nsizes z_i>=B_YL, a finite-dimensional convex 1-smooth function with a minimizer\nat zero and initial point e_1 has normalized objective at least\n\n    [1/(2(1+S_tail))] product_i\n           [z_i/(chi(A_YL+S_i)+epsilon z_i)]^2.\n\nHere S_i is the sum of ordinary steps after the previous checkpoint and before\nz_i, and S_tail is the sum after the last checkpoint. The normalization is\nW=2(f(x_N)-min f)/||x_0||^2. Section3.1 computes unnormalized final gap\nD^2/[4(1+S_tail)] at initial squared distance1; multiplying by2 gives the\ndisplayed coefficient. The paper then deliberately weakens its statement by\ndropping that factor2. No normalization inference from the label R_n is used.\n\nApplicability details: each local function is a translated/scaled unit Moreau\nenvelope of the support function of a finite convex hull of gradient vectors\nand zero. Its gradient is Euclidean projection onto that convex hull, hence\nit is convex and 1-smooth. The prescribed two-coordinate trajectories have\nnonnegative first-coordinate descent and second-coordinate ascent. Two exact\nzero regions ensure that old components and future components are inactive\nthroughout the full GD trajectory. The global objective is one half the sum of\nthese adjacent-coordinate functions and a final one-sided Huber term. Each\ncoordinate appears in at most two terms, giving global smoothness constant1;\nthe local steps are therefore h_t/2. All component offsets are nonnegative,\nso the origin is a common minimizer. These checks preserve arbitrary finite\nnonnegative steps and the entire checkpoint sequence. Dimension is the number\nof checkpoints plus one, finite at every fixed horizon.\n\nThe paper permits B_YL=2(1+epsilon^(-2)) and sufficiently large finite A_YL.\nOnly finiteness and these hypotheses are used below; neither parameter is sent\nto zero with m, and no constant is hidden in an asymptotic notation.\n\n## Exact checkpoint calculation\n\nChoose epsilon=1/100 and gamma=2099/20000, so chi=11/10. Fix any permitted\nfinite A_YL. Choose r large enough that\n\n    b_r>=B_YL=20002,   chi A_YL/b_r<=1/100.\n\nSuch a fixed r exists since b_r=a c^r tends to infinity. In H_m choose every\nstep whose level is at least r. All ordinary blocks between consecutive selected\nsteps, as well as the first and last ordinary blocks, are exact copies of H_r.\nThus every S_i and S_tail equals s_r. Set M=m-r.\n\nThe selected step sizes are b_r c^ell, with level ell occurring\n2^(M-ell-1) times for ell=0,...,M-1. Since\n\n    s_r/b_r=(1-(2/c)^r)/(c-2)<=5/3,\n\nthe fixed normalized denominator satisfies\n\n    d=chi(A_YL+s_r)/b_r<=1/100+11/6=553/300<37/20.\n\nEvery transfer is at least\n\n    G_ell=c^ell/[37/20+c^ell/100].\n\nThis expression increases with ell. Direct rational comparisons give\n\n    G_0>=1/2, G_1>=4/3, G_2>=7/2, G_3>=8,\n\nand G_ell>=1 for every ell>=4. For M>=4, the total transfer product is\ntherefore at least\n\n    [(1/2)^8 (4/3)^4 (7/2)^2 8]^(2^(M-4))\n       =(98/81)^(2^(M-4)).\n\nSquaring and multiplying the exact terminal coefficient from the source gives\n\n    W_(N_m)(H_m)>=(98/81)^(2^(M-3))/[2(1+s_r)],\n\nwhich is the claimed bound. All constants r,A_YL,s_r are fixed independently\nof m. The exponent equals (N_m+1)/2^(r+3), so the lower bound grows\nexponentially in the horizon, despite its very small fixed growth constant.\n\n## What this identifies and what it does not\n\nThis is a strict separation of mechanisms on a concrete schedule family. The\ncoupled-coordinate witness can undergo many shrinking transfers and then grow\nthrough larger checkpoints, without having to revisit the previously defined\none-dimensional ray. In the binary level counts the first four transfer types\nalready have product greater than one. The verified ray construction cannot\nexecute even its first outward return on the same family. A two-curvature\nhomogeneous core becomes a scalar contraction after its two signs collapse or\ninterchange; that is also insufficient on these schedules.\n\nThis calculation is not a constant/profile lower bound for arbitrary schedules\nand is not intended to reproduce the known silver-exponent theorem. Its use is\nto locate a real geometric difference that a stronger exact construction must\nretain. The local additive price A_YL and checkpoint threshold B_YL remain\nfixed costs of the cited mechanism; nothing here removes them or shows that\nthey are negligible at the desired leading-constant scale.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_recursive_family_nonlinear_separation.md", "companion_candidates": ["79f6ce08b41616b5", "4d3911499920adbe"], "status": "unverified source-based application; no verifier request", "scope": "explicit hostile test only, not all-schedule profile theorem", "external_refs": [{"key": "YL26", "authors": ["Yuhan Ye", "Kaizhao Liu"], "title": "Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent", "arxiv": "2609.09152", "year": 2026, "cited_for": "Section3.1 pre-weakening final-gap computation for Lemma2.2; Lemmas3.1--3.2 and AppendixA.1--A.3 read"}], "source_grounding": "Direct downloaded arXiv v2 text; Matlas local-transfer query returned unrelated matches."}, "glossary": {"H_m": "Ordered recursive schedule with growth c=13/5 and scale a=c^-17.", "r": "Fixed cutoff level chosen to exceed the published activation threshold and dominate the fixed additive transfer cost.", "A_YL": "Finite additive transfer constant in the cited Ye–Liu construction, not the step scale a.", "B_YL": "Published checkpoint threshold2(1+epsilon^-2)=20002 for epsilon1/100.", "chi": "11/10, equal to sqrt(1+2gamma+epsilon^2) for the fixed chosen parameters.", "G_ell": "Rational lower bound c^ell/(37/20+c^ell/100) on a level-ell selected transfer.", "M": "Number of levels m-r above the fixed cutoff.", "s_r": "Total step sum in one ordinary block H_r.", "W": "Project normalization twice objective gap at L=1 and initial squared distance1."}}
{"id": "11bf723a8eab9940", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:26:14.115130+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Concrete growing-dimensional nonseparable Moreau adversary with nonzero intercepts and negative temporal correlations: every schedule(1,b_0,...,1,b_(m-1)),5<=b_i<=11/2, has an explicit legal trajectory of normalized error1/4, although all quadratic-class errors tend uniformly to0.", "evidence": "# A nonseparable max-affine Moreau relay with affine offsets\nStatus: complete new candidate, unverified. No additional verifier allocation requested.\nWorker max; responds to TASK and guidance66447c4a378fcc32.\n\n## 1. Explicit persistent-error family\nLet m>=1 and choose arbitrary real b_0,...,b_(m-1) in[5,11/2].\nUse the predetermined length2m schedule\nh=(1,b_0,1,b_1,...,1,b_(m-1)).\nIn R^(m+1) take the standard orthonormal basis e_0,...,e_m, set\nrho_i=1/(1+b_i) for0<=i<m,\nw_i=rho_i(e_i-e_(i+1)) for0<=i<m,\nw_m=e_m/6,\nand set eta=1/36. Define one fixed function\nJ(z)=max{0, max_(0<=i<=m)(<w_i,z>-eta)},\nf(x)=min_z {J(z)+(1/2)||x-z||^2}.                           (1)\nStart at x_0=e_0 and choose minimizer0. All quantities in(1) are fixed once h is known. This is not a sequence of independently changed objectives or Hessians.\n\nThe function J is finite nonnegative convex and J(0)=0. Its Moreau envelope f is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with f>=0,f(0)=0. To spell out the local certification used below, if w belongs to the subdifferential of J at z and x=z+w, then z is the unique minimizer in(1), grad f(x)=w, and f(x)=J(z)+||w||^2/2. The minimum is unique by the quadratic's strong convexity. Monotonicity of subgradients at two such pairs gives\n||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>,\nwhich proves the required Lipschitz bound. The differentiability follows directly by comparing the unique minimizing values at x and x+v; the linear term is <x-z,v> and the remainder is bounded by a constant times||v||^2. Convexity follows from joint convexity in(x,z) and partial minimization.\n\n## 2. Every supporting plane, not just adjacent interpolation\nWe claim that at block i's two queried points the gradient is w_i. At the block start it is e_i; after its first unit step it is e_i-w_i. These claims imply\ne_i-(1+b_i)w_i=e_(i+1),                                  (2)\nso induction gives the exact entire GD trajectory.\n\nFor either query write x=e_i-u w_i with u=0 or1 and z=x-w_i=e_i-(u+1)w_i. Let c=u+1, so1<=c<=2.\nThe i-th affine plane at z has value\n<w_i,z>-eta=rho_i-2c rho_i^2-eta.\nHere 2/13<=rho_i<=1/6. The function rho-4rho^2 is decreasing over this interval and its minimum is1/18. Thus the current plane's value is at least1/36>0.\n\nThe next plane, when i<m-1, has value\nc rho_i rho_(i+1)-eta.\nThe current plane minus it is\nrho_i[1-c(2rho_i+rho_(i+1))]>=0,\nbecause c<=2 and every rho is at most1/6.\nWhen i=m-1 the same formula uses1/6 in place of rho_(i+1), because the terminal plane is w_m=e_m/6, and the same bound holds.\n\nThe previous plane, when i>=1, has value\n-rho_(i-1)[1-c rho_i]-eta<0.\nEvery other non-current plane has value -eta, since its two-coordinate support is disjoint from z. The zero plane has value0. Thus the current affine plane dominates EVERY stored plane. It follows that w_i belongs to the subdifferential of J at z. This proves the claimed gradients at both queries and hence(2), including all chronological interactions with old and future planes.\n\nAt x_(2m)=e_m set z=(5/6)e_m. The terminal plane has value\n<w_m,z>-eta=5/36-1/36=1/9.\nThe only other possibly nonzero inner product is with w_(m-1), and it is negative. All remaining planes have value-eta, while the zero plane has value0. Therefore grad f(e_m)=w_m and\nf(e_m)=1/9+(1/2)(1/36)=1/8.\nThe initial squared distance is1, so\nW_(2m)(h)>=2f(e_m)=1/4.                                  (3)\n\nFor general smoothness L>0 use f_L=L f. The updates h_t/L then have exactly the same points, and the normalized objective remains1/4.\n\nThe intermediate objective values are also explicit:\nf(e_i-u w_i)=rho_i-(2u+1)rho_i^2-eta, u=0,1.\nThus the construction supplies positions, gradients, values and a global function; it does not stop at a PSD matrix or local inequalities.\n\n## 3. Actual growing dimension and nonseparability\nThe visited block-start points are the m+1 vectors e_0,...,e_m. The vectors w_0,...,w_m are linearly independent: their coordinate matrix is triangular with nonzero diagonal. Their Gram matrix is explicitly real PSD because they are the displayed Euclidean vectors. Its only off-diagonal entries at distinct stages are\n<w_i,w_(i+1)>=-rho_i rho_(i+1) for i<m-1,\n<w_(m-1),w_m>=-rho_(m-1)/6.\nEach w_i is queried twice, so the temporal Gram matrix also has positive repeated-gradient correlations. Energies are2rho_i^2 on the relay stages and1/36 at the terminal point. They vary with the chosen b_i. In particular the accepted orthogonal-moment obstruction0e7e78056dc6258c does not apply.\n\nThe function cannot be written as a sum of univariate functions in any fixed orthonormal coordinates. Indeed linear independence of the w_i lets us choose, for any i, a point z with <w_i,z>=eta and <w_j,z><eta for every j!=i. In a neighborhood of z, J is exactly max{0,<w_i,z>-eta}. At x=z+theta w_i with0<theta<1 and sufficiently small surrounding perturbations, its envelope has Hessian\nP_i=w_i w_i^T/||w_i||^2.\nFor two neighboring, nonparallel w_i,w_(i+1), their inner product is nonzero, so the rank-one projectors P_i and P_(i+1) do not commute. All Hessians of a function separable in a fixed orthonormal basis would be diagonal in that basis and would commute wherever they exist. This contradiction proves genuine nonseparability, not merely a rotated scalar witness or an orthogonal direct sum.\n\n## 4. Why this schedule test is not just a large quadratic product\nFor any scalar quadratic f_lambda(x)=lambda x^2/2 with0<=lambda<=1, one block's multiplier is\np_b(lambda)=(1-lambda)(1-b lambda).\nFor0<=lambda<=1/6 and5<=b<=11/2, it is nonnegative and at most1-lambda<=exp(-lambda).\nFor1/6<=lambda<=1, its largest positive value is at most5/36, and its negative minimum has magnitude\n(b-1)^2/(4b)<=81/88.\nThe latter expression increases with b>1, and its endpoint value at b=11/2 is81/88. These statements also follow by checking the vertex and endpoints of the quadratic p_b on the given interval.\n\nConsequently the worst normalized objective over every scalar quadratic satisfies\nmax_(0<=lambda<=1) lambda product_i p_(b_i)(lambda)^2\n <=max{1/(2 e m),(81/88)^(2m)} ->0.                       (4)\nThe first term comes from maximizing lambda exp(-2m lambda). A general finite-dimensional convex quadratic diagonalizes orthogonally, and its normalized error is a convex combination of these scalar errors; zero-curvature directions contribute no objective. Thus(4) controls the full quadratic class uniformly in the varying b_i, whereas(3) stays1/4 on the explicitly nonseparable nonlinear function.\n\nThis does not assert that scalar nonlinear witnesses fail on these schedules. The point is to give a concrete globally consistent variable-energy high-dimensional trajectory surviving mixed short/long steps even when every quadratic channel converges.\n\n## 5. General block and offset extension\nHere is the same candidate family for an arbitrary chronological partition\n(block_0,b_0,...,block_(m-1),b_(m-1),block_m),\nwhere s_i is the ordinary block sum, as in the project glossary. Put\nM_i=s_i+b_i>0, rho_i=1/M_i for i<m, and choose rho_m>0.\nKeep w_i=rho_i(e_i-e_(i+1)), w_m=rho_m e_m, initial point e_0.\nReplace the common offset eta in(1) by arbitrary nonnegative offsets eta_i.\n\nWrite c_i=s_i+1. The following EXPLICIT sufficient conditions certify the entire trajectory:\n0<=eta_i<=rho_i-2c_i rho_i^2                         for i<m, (5)\neta_i-eta_(i+1)<=rho_i-c_i(2rho_i^2+rho_i rho_(i+1)) for i<m, (6)\n0<=eta_m<=rho_m-(s_m+1)rho_m^2.                               (7)\nThe rho_(i+1) in the last instance of(6) is the terminal scalar rho_m.\n\nDuring ordinary block i let u be the partial step sum already performed. Then0<=u<=s_i and the intended point is e_i-u w_i. The current plane at its proximal point is\nrho_i-2(u+1)rho_i^2-eta_i>=0\nby(5). Its difference from the next plane is nonnegative by(6), because the difference decreases with u. Every old adjacent plane has value\n-rho_(i-1)[1-(u+1)rho_i]-eta_(i-1)<=0;\nindeed(5) forces2c_i rho_i<=1 and hence the bracket is nonnegative. Farther old and future planes have values -eta_j<=0. The checkpoint query is u=s_i, and the full block plus checkpoint transfers exactly from e_i to e_(i+1). During the terminal block the points are (1-u rho_m)e_m; condition(7) makes the terminal plane nonnegative at its proximal points, and all old planes are nonpositive. These checks again retain every supporting plane.\n\nThe exact terminal normalized value is\n2rho_m-(2s_m+1)rho_m^2-2eta_m.                              (8)\nThus(5)--(7) are a concrete sufficient-condition interface whose payoff is calculated, not an unproved minimax statement.\n\nFor fixed rho's one can test the least offsets with the finite recurrence\neta_0=0,\neta_(i+1)=max{0, eta_i+c_i(2rho_i^2+rho_i rho_(i+1))-rho_i}.  (9)\nAny feasible nonnegative offset sequence dominates this one coordinatewise, by induction. Hence if(9) violates any upper capacity in(5) or(7), there is no offset sequence satisfying these sufficient conditions for the chosen partition and rho's. If it respects all capacities, it gives the largest payoff(8) among them. The weights rho_i=1/M_i are fixed by the exact coordinate transfers; this is not an arbitrary covariance optimization.\n\n## 6. Exact hostile test and what must change next\nFor blocks consisting of one unit step followed by4, take the natural relay weights rho_i=1/5 for every nonterminal stage. At any pair of such consecutive stages, the increment in(9) is\n2(2/25+1/25)-1/5=1/25,\nwhereas the capacity in(5) is\n1/5-4/25=1/25.\nThus with three or more such nonterminal blocks, eta_2>=2/25 exceeds its capacity1/25. No nonnegative intercept choice repairs THIS equal-distance, constant-gradient relay on the whole repeated(1,4) schedule. Allowing arbitrary terminal weight does not repair the first three nonterminal blocks. This is a failure of the displayed model, not of all high-dimensional correlated constructions or of the target.\n\nThe structural cost is visible in(5): a phase with a fixed direction must support both its incoming and outgoing coordinate components throughout the entire ordinary block. A new mutation must change that within-block geometry (vary direction or energy, or retain nonzero past coordinate residuals), while retaining every old plane. Merely declaring(5)--(7) feasible for arbitrary schedules at the target payoff would be false in this example.\n\nNo comparison of this relay family to U_n^ZJ for arbitrary schedules is proved. Its first deliverable is the explicit nonseparable function, exact growing-dimensional trajectory, quantitative mixed-step test, and exact extension/failure interface.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_correlated_moreau_relay.md", "guidance": "66447c4a378fcc32", "fact_ids": ["0e7e78056dc6258c"], "status": "complete new candidate, no additional fact submission; uniform profile comparison open", "construction": "one explicit nonseparable convex max-affine Moreau envelope in dimensionm+1", "extension": "exact nonnegative offset recurrence(9) with capacities(5)--(7)", "hostile_test": "equal-distance relay fails repeated(1,4) after three nonterminal blocks"}, "glossary": {"J": "The explicit maximum of zero and affine planes used before Moreau smoothing.", "w_i": "Euclidean affine-plane normals rho_i(e_i-e_(i+1)), with terminal normal rho_m e_m.", "rho_i": "Relay gradient scale, equal to the reciprocal ordinary-plus-checkpoint block mass at nonterminal stages.", "eta_i": "Nonnegative affine-plane offset; eta=1/36 in the explicit mixed-step test.", "M_i": "Ordinary-plus-checkpoint mass s_i+b_i in the coordinate relay.", "P_i": "The rank-one orthogonal projector onto span(w_i), used only in the nonseparability proof.", "p_b": "Scalar quadratic multiplier(1-lambda)(1-b lambda) for one tested two-step block."}}
{"id": "471bfbf7f226fc04", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:26:14.441114+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Concrete uniform descent proved for a positive penultimate star-outgoing multiplier, with an explicit PSD certificate retaining all signs: b_new=(2b+nu)/(nu+2), q_new=2q/(nu+2). This excludes lambda_{*,n-1}>0 in every tight certificate at a global minimizer.", "evidence": "# A concrete final-step descent from a penultimate star-outgoing multiplier\n\nStatus: new self-contained candidate, unverified. No new verification allocation is assumed. This addresses one real component of the general-q descent problem; it does not prove that every q>tau tuple has such a component.\n\n## 1. Input and exact schedule change\n\nNormalize L=1 and f_*=0. Let h be any nonnegative schedule of length n>=1, with final step b=h_{n-1}. Use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0, and the standard full upper certificate\n\n    q||x_0-x_*||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij = M >=0,\n\nwhere M is a PSD quadratic form after the exact GD substitutions, lambda_ij>=0, and the function-value coefficients cancel. The index * has x_*=g_*=0 and f_*=0. The result applies to every full feasible certificate, regardless of stationarity or Huber tightness.\n\nSuppose\n\n    nu=lambda_{*,n-1}>0.\n\nFor any 0<epsilon<=nu/(nu+2), put c=1-epsilon and replace ONLY the last step by\n\n    b_new=c b+epsilon.\n\nThen the new predetermined GD schedule has the rigorous uniform bound\n\n    W_n(h_0,...,h_{n-2},b_new) <= c q < q.                    (D)\n\nAt the largest allowed epsilon this reads\n\n    b_new=(2b+nu)/(nu+2),       q_new=2q/(nu+2).\n\nThe strict inequality is meaningful whenever q>0; every valid finite-horizon objective upper bound has q>0 by the canonical Huber witness.\n\n## 2. Direct proof for all smooth convex functions\n\nFix any legal function and initial point, using the old and new schedules with the same prefix. Write\n\n    x=x_{n-1}, g=grad f(x),\n    z=x-b g                    (old endpoint),\n    y=x-g                      (unit-step point),\n    z_new=c z+epsilon y         (new endpoint),\n    delta=f(x)-||g||^2/2 >=0.\n\nBecause Q_{*,n-1}=-delta, isolating this term in the original full PSD certificate yields\n\n    2f(z)+nu delta <= q||x_0-x_*||^2.                        (A)\n\nThe remaining terms have the correct signs: the PSD quadratic form is nonnegative and every other -lambda_ij Q_ij is nonnegative.\n\nThe unit-step descent inequality gives f(y)<=delta. Convexity then yields\n\n    2f(z_new) <=2c f(z)+2epsilon f(y)\n                <=2c f(z)+2epsilon delta\n                <=c[2f(z)+nu delta]\n                <=c q||x_0-x_*||^2,\n\nwhere 2epsilon<=c nu was the only restriction on epsilon. This proves (D) without selecting any hard function, differentiating W, or assuming a stationary worst-case primal.\n\nThe unit-step inequality can itself be derived from a single smooth-convex interpolation constraint:\n\n    Q_{y,x}=f(y)-f(x)+<grad f(y),g>\n              +||grad f(y)-g||^2/2\n           =f(y)-delta+||grad f(y)||^2/2 <=0.\n\nThus the proof uses precisely the full smooth-convex class and is dimension-independent.\n\n## 3. Explicit nonnegative-multiplier, PSD certificate\n\nThe construction is stronger than a sign argument: it gives an explicit enlarged interpolation certificate for the changed schedule. The two auxiliary points are the old endpoint z and the unit-step point y. The new algorithm still uses n steps and no auxiliary oracle calls; auxiliary points appear only in the proof and are defined from the same penultimate point and gradient.\n\nLet g_z,g_y,g_new denote their actual gradients, and put\n\n    d=c nu-2epsilon >=0.\n\nKeep c times every old multiplier except lambda_{*,n-1}; assign d to Q_{*,n-1}. Add the new nonnegative multipliers\n\n    2c       on Q_{z_new,z},\n    2epsilon on Q_{z_new,y},\n    2epsilon on Q_{y,x}.\n\nAll function-value terms cancel against the new objective -2f(z_new). After substituting z_new=c z+epsilon y and y=x-g, the full polynomial identity is\n\n    c q||x_0-x_*||^2-2f(z_new)\n      +c sum_{(i,j)!=(star,n-1)}lambda_ij Q_ij\n      +d Q_{*,n-1}\n      +2c Q_{z_new,z}+2epsilon Q_{z_new,y}\n      +2epsilon Q_{y,x}\n\n    = c M\n      +c||g_new-g_z||^2\n      +epsilon||g_new-g_y||^2\n      +epsilon||g_y||^2.                                    (PSD)\n\nThe right side is an explicit PSD quadratic form, including the original full matrix M, not only its row sums or energy trace. The left side uses nonnegative multipliers throughout. All interpolation data concern the same globally legal function; no incompatible local transcript is glued in.\n\nTo check the identity, combine the first two new inequalities with weights c,epsilon. Their gradient-position terms cancel because z_new=c z+epsilon y. Then combine Q_{y,x}=f(y)-delta+||g_y||^2/2. The coefficient d is exactly what balances the old nu delta term.\n\nThe displayed certificate uses auxiliary analysis points. It is not asserted to be an explicit parameter-by-parameter map into the minimal n+2-point dual. Its full PSD identity proves the actual uniform upper bound directly, so it is already sufficient for a global-minimizer contradiction. When the new steps are positive, exact finite interpolation duality also gives a minimal-format certificate at its true worst-case value, but that existential statement is not needed for (D).\n\n## 4. Consequence at global minimizers\n\nSuppose h is a globally minimizing length-n schedule and its full certificate is tight: q=W_n(h)=W_n^*. If lambda_{*,n-1}>0, formula (D) constructs a length-n nonnegative predetermined schedule with strictly smaller true worst-case value. This contradicts global minimality.\n\nConsequently EVERY tight full certificate at a global minimizer must satisfy\n\n    lambda_{*,n-1}=0.                                      (V)\n\nThe contradiction uses an actual globally valid changed algorithm, so it is not merely a stationarity-implies-tightness shortcut. It does not need q=tau or a bound on the total step mass. It also handles a final step already equal to one: then the schedule does not change and (D) would improve the allegedly tight q itself.\n\nThis conclusion leaves lambda_{*,i} for i<n-1, lambda_{*,n}, and the PSD Schur-complement defect completely open. In particular it does not establish Huber tightness of a global minimizer.\n\n## 5. Exact checks and the remaining q>tau case\n\nFor the one-step certificate at h_0=1+a, a>1, take\n\n    q=a^2, lambda_{0,*}=2a^2,\n    lambda_{*,0}=2a^2-2, lambda_{1,0}=2,\n\nall other multipliers zero. Its exact slack is\n\n    a^2||x_0-g_0||^2+||a g_0+g_1||^2,\n\nand f(x)=x^2/2 attains q. The new formula gives c=1/a^2, h_new=1+1/a and the certified bound q_new=1. This is strictly smaller than a^2. The formula preserves nonnegativity and PSD through the explicit identity above; smaller epsilon gives a continuous descent.\n\nThe hypothesis nu>0 cannot be silently replaced by q>tau. For 1/2<a<1, the tight one-step certificate at h_0=1+a has q=a^2>tau=1/(2a+3), but no star-outgoing multipliers:\n\n    lambda_{0,*}=2a, lambda_{1,*}=2(1-a), lambda_{1,0}=2a.\n\nIts gradient block is [[2a,1],[1,1]], its star-directed vector is mu=(2a,2(1-a)), and its full Schur complement is\n\n    Z=(2a-1)[[1,1/a],[1/a,1/a^2]] >=0.\n\nThe quadratic matches q. Thus the remaining non-Huber defect can be carried entirely by nonzero PSD slack even when all star-outgoing weights vanish. This example is not globally optimal; it is a hostile test of extending (V) into an unsupported general tightness claim.\n\n## 6. Related terminal extension\n\nIf a certificate instead has lambda_{*,n}=nu>0, the same argument applied to an appended step gives, for 0<=epsilon<=nu/(nu+2),\n\n    W_{n+1}(h,epsilon) <= (1-epsilon)q.\n\nHere use the convex combination of x_n and x_n-grad f(x_n), and isolate the terminal residual delta_n=f_n-||g_n||^2/2 in the old certificate. This is a length-(n+1) improvement and must not be counted as a length-n contradiction. It may be useful for upper-bound composition, but no asymptotic comparison with the exact Zhang--Jiang profile follows automatically.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_penultimate_flow_descent.md", "branch_id": "max2_huber_gap_descent", "prior_finding": "1dc29ea19aa3894a", "status": "new concrete sufficient descent mechanism; full q>tau reduction still unresolved", "evidence_status": "unverified, no new verifier call", "scope": "all full feasible certificates with positive penultimate star-outgoing flow; genuine fixed-length descent"}, "glossary": {"nu": "The strictly positive multiplier lambda_{*,n-1} from the optimum to the penultimate sample.", "delta": "The nonnegative unit-step residual f(x_{n-1})-||g_{n-1}||^2/2 after normalization L=1 and f_*=0.", "b": "The original final stepsize h_{n-1}.", "b_new": "The changed final step (1-epsilon)b+epsilon.", "z": "The old endpoint x_{n-1}-b g_{n-1}, an auxiliary analysis point after the schedule changes.", "y": "The unit-step point x_{n-1}-g_{n-1}.", "z_new": "The actual endpoint for the changed final step, equal to (1-epsilon)z+epsilon y.", "d": "The remaining penultimate star-outgoing multiplier (1-epsilon)nu-2epsilon>=0."}}
{"id": "c60f5c7eb36efc1b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:30:33.207945+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "New quantitative descent from the actual PSD Schur complement: if its range contains any nonzero vector supported on the final two gradient coordinates, a tight certificate cannot be globally minimizing. Explicit step change and uniform strict bound supplied, including nu=0, q>tau certificates.", "evidence": "# Quantitative final-step descent from a terminal pair in the PSD Schur range\n\nStatus: unverified candidate. It extends the concrete descent search to certificates with no star-outgoing multipliers. It is not a general q>tau descent theorem.\n\n## 1. Full-certificate hypothesis\n\nNormalize L=1, x_*=f_*=0, and ||x_0||=1. Let a length-n schedule h, n>=1, have final step b=h_{n-1}>0 and a full feasible upper certificate (q,lambda), using all smooth-convex inequalities Q_ij<=0 and a PSD slack matrix\n\n    M=[[q,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]],   mu_i=lambda_{i,*}, q>0.\n\nLet Z=A-mu mu^T/(4q)>=0 be its complete gradient Schur complement. This is the actual matrix from the full certificate, not a surrogate formed from a few minors.\n\nSuppose there exist kappa>0, a>0 and s in {+1,-1} such that\n\n    Z >= kappa v v^T,\n    v = a e_{n-1}+s e_n.                                   (H)\n\nThe indices e_i refer to gradient coordinates. The sign s=+1 will give a decrease of the final step; s=-1 will give an increase.\n\nDefine the finite radius bounds\n\n    B_prev=prod_{t<n-1}(1+h_t),    B=(1+b)B_prev,\n    C=q/(2B^2)+1/kappa.\n\nChoose any d>0 satisfying\n\n    d<=a/C,\n    d<=q^2/(8a B^2 B_prev^2),                               (d)\n    d<b                  if s=+1.\n\nThe changed schedule, with all preceding steps fixed, is\n\n    b_new=b-s d.\n\nThen the actual worst-case value obeys the explicit strict improvement\n\n    W_n(h_0,...,h_{n-2},b_new)\n       <= q-d q^2/(8a B^2) < q.                            (D)\n\nNo Huber-tightness, vanishing star-outgoing flow, stationarity, or rank assumption on the old primal is needed. The sufficient condition is the concrete full-matrix domination (H).\n\n## 2. Uniform proof, including nearly worst-case instances\n\nFix any legal function. Put E=2f(x_n), p=g_{n-1}, g=g_n, and r=a p+s g. The complete certificate identity and every interpolation sign give\n\n    q-E >= M evaluated on the actual vectors\n          >= g_all^T Z g_all\n          >= kappa ||r||^2.                               (1)\n\nHere the second inequality follows by completing the square in x_0 in the full matrix. Thus ||r||^2<=(q-E)/kappa; the formula remains valid for every instance, not only for a maximizing one.\n\nThe elementary bounds ||p||<=B_prev, ||x_n||<=B, and convexity yield\n\n    ||g||^2 >= E^2/(4B^2).                                 (2)\n\nThe identity ||r||^2=a^2||p||^2+||g||^2+2s a<p,g> implies\n\n    -2s <p,g> >= (||g||^2-||r||^2)/a.                     (3)\n\nThe new endpoint is x_n+s d p. Smoothness gives\n\n    E_new <= E+2s d<p,g>+d^2||p||^2\n           <= E-(d/a)[E^2/(4B^2)-(q-E)/kappa]\n                    +d^2 B_prev^2.                        (4)\n\nFor every E, the scalar inequality E^2>=q^2-2q(q-E) is simply (E-q)^2>=0. Inserting it into (4) gives\n\n    E_new <= q-d q^2/(4a B^2)\n               -[1-(d/a)C](q-E)+d^2 B_prev^2.\n\nSince E<=q and d<=a/C, the middle term is nonpositive. The other restriction in (d) then gives (D). Taking the supremum over all legal functions and dimensions proves the uniform bound.\n\nThus this is an actual stepsize perturbation with an explicit certified new value. It is not a first-order assertion evaluated only on a guessed active function. The elementary smoothness upper inequality in (4) also follows from Q_{new,old}<=0 by completing the square\n||g_new-g-s d p||^2/2, so the argument is consistent with the full interpolation cone.\n\nThe proof provides a uniform analytic certificate for the new schedule. It does not claim an explicit closed formula for its minimal-format interpolation multipliers. Such a formula is unnecessary to conclude that a purported globally optimal schedule has been strictly improved.\n\n## 3. A range exclusion at global minimizers\n\nLet T=span{e_{n-1},e_n}. For a PSD matrix Z and a nonzero v in range(Z), one has\n\n    Z >= kappa v v^T,   kappa=1/(v^T Z^dagger v)>0.\n\nIndeed write v=Z^(1/2)w with w=Z^(dagger/2)v, and use (w^T Z^(1/2)u)^2<=||w||^2 u^T Z u. Thus any vector in range(Z) supported on the last two coordinates, with both entries nonzero, can be rescaled to condition (H). Either sign of their ratio is allowed.\n\nA vector with only the last coordinate nonzero is incompatible with a tight positive certificate: its square would force g_n=0 on a matching worst-case instance, hence f_n=0. A vector with only coordinate n-1 nonzero likewise forces g_{n-1}=0, making x_n=x_{n-1} a minimizer. Matching worst cases exist in the finite full interpolation formulation; alternatively, the quantitative argument below rules these cases out without assuming attainment.\n\nFor the last coordinate alone, q-E>=kappa||g_n||^2>=kappa E^2/(4B^2). This uniform inequality implies E<=r<q, where r is the positive root of r+kappa r^2/(4B^2)=q. It improves the allegedly tight upper bound. For the penultimate coordinate alone, use ||g_n||<=(1+b)||g_{n-1}|| to get the same conclusion with kappa replaced by kappa/(1+b)^2.\n\nConsequently every tight full certificate at a globally minimizing positive schedule satisfies\n\n    range(Z) intersect T = {0}.                             (R)\n\nIn particular\n\n    rank(Z)<=n-1.\n\nFor n=1 this forces Z=0. For larger n, it does not force the whole Schur complement to vanish: its range may involve earlier gradients in every nonzero vector.\n\nAn equivalent exact matrix formulation uses the earlier-coordinate set J={0,...,n-2}. The generalized Schur complement of Z with respect to its J block must vanish:\n\n    Z_TT-Z_TJ Z_JJ^dagger Z_JT=0.                           (SC)\n\nFor a PSD block matrix, completing squares gives the factorization into a block supported on the J rows and a residual supported only on T. The latter is PSD and its nonzero range lies in range(Z) intersect T. Conversely, if that residual is zero, the factorized range has no nonzero vector with zero J coordinates. This proves the equivalence, including singular Z_JJ. For n=1, J is empty and (SC) means Z=0.\n\nThis range restriction retains all history-dependent entries of Z; it does not replace Z by a two-by-two principal minor.\n\n## 4. Exact one-step checks, including nu=0 above the Huber level\n\nFor b=1+a with 1/2<a<1, there is a tight certificate with\n\n    q=a^2,\n    lambda_{0,*}=2a, lambda_{1,*}=2(1-a), lambda_{1,0}=2a,\n\nand all other multipliers zero. Its Schur complement is\n\n    Z=(2a-1)[[1,1/a],[1/a,1/a^2]]\n      =[(2a-1)/a^2] (a,1)^T(a,1).\n\nThus (H) holds with s=+1, kappa=(2a-1)/a^2, even though every star-outgoing multiplier vanishes. Formula (D) gives an explicit strict descent by decreasing b. The matching legal quadratic has g_1=-a g_0, exactly the relation exposed by the PSD square.\n\nFor 0<b<3/2, the tight Huber certificate with lambda_{0,*}=lambda_{1,*}=lambda_{1,0}=1 has\n\n    q=1/(2b+1), Z=(3-2b)[[1,-1],[-1,1]]/4.\n\nHere (H) holds with a=1, s=-1. The final step increases and the uniform bound strictly improves. This is consistent with the exact one-step optimum b=3/2, where Z=0 and this descent disappears.\n\nThese checks also show why q>tau alone is neither the hypothesis nor the conclusion of this result: the range mechanism can detect a suboptimal Huber-tight schedule, and it detects a non-Huber certificate with nu=0.\n\n## 5. Remaining obstruction\n\nTogether with the penultimate-flow descent in research/max2_penultimate_flow_descent.md, the new constraints rule out two concrete ways for a globally minimizing tight certificate to carry slack: lambda_{*,n-1}>0 and any Schur-range vector supported on the final two gradients.\n\nThey leave earlier star-outgoing multipliers, the final star-outgoing multiplier, and a Schur range intertwined with earlier gradients. There is no proved implication from (R) to q=tau, no proved capacity bound by S_bullet(n), and no exact-profile conclusion. The next mechanism would have to control that earlier-coordinate dependence without treating the final two-coordinate Schur complement as the full matrix.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_terminal_schur_descent.md", "branch_id": "max2_huber_gap_descent", "status": "new explicit sufficient descent and full-matrix range obstruction; general q>tau unresolved", "evidence_status": "unverified; no additional submission", "prior_findings": ["471bfbf7f226fc04", "1dc29ea19aa3894a"]}, "glossary": {"Z": "The full gradient Schur complement A-mu mu^T/(4q) of the actual feasible interpolation certificate.", "T": "The coordinate subspace spanned by the penultimate and final gradient basis vectors.", "J": "The earlier gradient coordinates 0,...,n-2.", "kappa": "A positive coefficient for which Z>=kappa v v^T with v supported on the last two gradients.", "s": "A sign in {+1,-1}: +1 decreases the final step, -1 increases it.", "B_prev": "The finite radius upper bound product_{t<n-1}(1+h_t) for normalized initial distance one.", "B": "The terminal radius upper bound (1+h_{n-1})B_prev.", "Z^dagger": "The Moore-Penrose inverse of the PSD Schur complement."}}
{"id": "ab7207a7c030bafb", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:34:38.243780+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Nonstationary correlated Moreau relay repairs the(1,4) obstruction: explicit nonseparable dimensionm+2 functions give W_(2m)>=51/125 for every(1,b_i) sequence with4<=b_i<=5. Optimizing the stationary AR correlation yields the exact internal threshold M/(s+1)=1+sqrt2.", "evidence": "# Correlated-frame mutation of the Moreau relay\nStatus: complete new analytic candidate; unverified. No verifier allocation requested.\nThis mutates11bf723a8eab9940 after its equal-distance orthogonal-state relay failed repeated(1,4). It retains an actual single max-affine Moreau envelope, with nonzero offsets, variable gradient energies and every supporting plane.\n\n## 1. A globally specified nonstationary correlated family\nFix m>=1 and arbitrary b_i in[4,5],0<=i<m. Use the schedule\nh=(1,b_0,1,b_1,...,1,b_(m-1)).\nIn R^(m+2) take an orthonormal basis e_0,...,e_(m+1). Fix r=-2/5 and set\nu_0=e_0,\nu_(i+1)=r u_i+sqrt(1-r^2)e_(i+1),0<=i<=m.                (1)\nThen ||u_i||=1 and <u_i,u_j>=r^|i-j|, directly by induction. These are the block-start points, not independent sampled curvature matrices.\n\nPut M_i=1+b_i for i<m, M_m=5, and\nw_i=(u_i-u_(i+1))/M_i,0<=i<=m.\nChoose eta=1/50 and define\nJ(z)=max{0, max_(0<=i<=m)(<w_i,z>-eta)},\nf(x)=min_z {J(z)+||x-z||^2/2}.                           (2)\nAll planes and points are fixed by h. The Moreau envelope f is convex differentiable1-smooth, minimized at0 with value0, because J is finite nonnegative convex and J(0)=0. If plane i is active at z=x-w_i, then w_i belongs to the subdifferential of J at z, so this z is the unique minimizer in(2), grad f(x)=w_i and f(x)=<w_i,x>-||w_i||^2/2-eta. This follows directly from the subgradient optimality condition for the strongly convex minimization; monotonicity of the two subgradients gives the global1-Lipschitz gradient bound.\n\nStart from x_0=u_0, so the initial squared distance from minimizer0 is1.\nThe intended two queries in block i are u_i and u_i-w_i, both with gradient w_i. Their two steps then end exactly at\nu_i-(1+b_i)w_i=u_(i+1).                                 (3)\n\n## 2. Closed-form comparisons with ALL old and future planes\nLet x=u_i-(c-1)w_i, where1<=c<=2 for the two queries of a real block. The terminal query has i=m,c=1. Its candidate proximal point is z=u_i-c w_i.\nWrite t=c/M_i. Direct inner-product calculation from(1) gives\nA_i(c):=<w_i,z>=(1-r)/M_i [1-2t],                         (4)\nand for k>=1,\n<w_(i+k),z>=(1-r)r^(k-1)/M_(i+k) [r+t(1-r)],              (5)\n<w_(i-k),z>=(1-r)r^(k-1)/M_(i-k) [-1+t(1-r)].             (6)\nEquations(4)--(6) check every plane; no nonadjacent interpolation condition is dropped.\n\nAll M_j lie in[5,6]. Equation(4) is at least\n(7/5)(1/5-4/25)=7/125,\nbecause the minimum over c occurs at c=2 and (M-4)/M^2 increases for5<=M<=6.\nThus the current affine plane is positive after subtracting eta=1/50.\n\nFor the other planes, divide their values and(4) by the positive factor(1-r)/M_i. Put a=2/5, so r=-a. For any real A,\nmax_(k>=1) r^(k-1) A=max{A,-a A}.\nTherefore every future-plane value from(5), after this normalization, is at most the larger of\n-2M_i/25+7c/25,\n4M_i/125-14c/125.                                       (7)\nHere we used M_i/M_j<=M_i/5 and the nonnegative maximal value; the maximum of the two displayed bounds has that sign.\n\nSince -1+(7/5)t<0, the largest value in(6) occurs at lag2 if such a past plane exists. Every past-plane value is bounded above by\n2M_i/25-14c/125.                                        (8)\nA nonexistent lag2 only makes this upper estimate conservative.\n\nThe normalized current value is1-2c/M_i. Its differences from the three bounds in(7)--(8) are, respectively,\n1+2M_i/25-c(2/M_i+7/25),\n1-4M_i/125-c(2/M_i-14/125),\n1-2M_i/25-c(2/M_i-14/125).\nAll three decrease with c over1<=c<=2. At c=2 each increases with M_i on[5,6], by direct differentiation. Their minimum values at M_i=5,c=2 are\n1/25, 33/125, 3/125,\nall strictly positive. This proves strict domination of every other nonzero plane. Equal offsets eta cancel in these comparisons.\n\nThus plane i is active at every indicated proximal point. The induction(3) is exact, and the terminal point is u_m with gradient w_m.\n\n## 3. Exact nonvanishing objective\nAt the terminal query M_m=5, so\n<w_m,u_m>=(1-r)/5=7/25,\n||w_m||^2=2(1-r)/25=14/125.\nHence\nf(u_m)=7/25-7/125-1/50=51/250,\nand the legal normalized error is\nW_(2m)(h)>=2f(u_m)=51/125.                               (9)\nThis holds uniformly over every m and every varying b_i in[4,5].\n\nThe two intermediate values in real block i are also explicit:\nf(u_i-u w_i)=(1-r)/M_i-(2u+1)(1-r)/M_i^2-eta, u=0,1.\nTogether with(1)--(3), these give all actual points, values and gradients. Their complete interpolation legality follows from the displayed global function and was established by checking every supporting plane.\n\nFor L>0 use Lf; the prescribed h_t/L updates and normalized error are unchanged.\n\n## 4. Growing dimension, temporal correlations and nonseparability\nThe stage gradient energies are2(1-r)/M_i^2, so they vary with the b_i. For j=i+k>i,\n<w_i,w_j>=-(1-r)^2 r^(k-1)/(M_i M_j).                    (10)\nThey are nonzero at every separation and alternate in sign. The two equal gradients inside each block add positive temporal correlations. The Gram matrix is explicitly PSD because(1) constructs real vectors, not because of a guessed covariance condition.\n\nThe normals w_0,...,w_m are linearly independent: the coefficient of the new basis vector e_(i+1) in w_i is nonzero, while all earlier normals lack that coordinate. The number of independent sampled gradient directions therefore grows with m.\n\nThe Moreau envelope is genuinely nonseparable in any fixed orthonormal coordinates. For each i, linear independence of the normals allows a point z satisfying <w_i,z>=eta and <w_j,z><eta for j!=i. Around x=z+theta w_i,0<theta<1, the local envelope is that of the single hinge max{0,<w_i,z>-eta}, and its Hessian is the projector w_i w_i^T/||w_i||^2. Neighboring normals are neither orthogonal nor parallel by(1),(10), so these two projectors do not commute. A function separable in one orthonormal basis has commuting Hessians at all points where they exist. This proves the claim.\n\nThis family is thus outside both the scalar constructions and the exact orthogonality hypotheses of fact0e7e78056dc6258c.\n\n## 5. The same schedules converge on every quadratic\nFor b in[4,5], the scalar quadratic block multiplier is\np_b(lambda)=(1-lambda)(1-b lambda),0<=lambda<=1.\nFor0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and at most exp(-lambda). For1/5<=lambda<=1 its positive maximum is at most4/25, and its negative minimum has magnitude at most\nmax_(4<=b<=5)(b-1)^2/(4b)=4/5.\nConsequently the entire quadratic-class normalized objective is at most\nmax{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0,                           (11)\nuniformly in all the varying b_i. Orthogonal diagonalization extends this bound to every finite-dimensional convex quadratic. Equation(9) is therefore a nonlinear, nonseparable persistent-error construction on mixed short/long schedules whose full quadratic channel converges. It is not a claim that scalar nonlinear witnesses are small on this particular class.\n\n## 6. Exact silver threshold within the stationary correlated relay\nNow take a common block mass M>0 and common ordinary-block sum s>=0, so the checkpoint is b=M-s. Use M_i=M also for the terminal virtual normal, a common offset eta, and r=-a with0<=a<1. At the final ordinary query put t=(s+1)/M. Formula(4) has normalized current value C=1-2t.\n\nThe largest past-plane comparison is the lag2 value\na[1-t(1+a)].\nIt is dominated by C exactly when\nt<=1/(2+a),                                             (12)\nprovided a<1. The largest future comparison is max{A,-a A}, where A=-a+t(1+a). Its lag1 requirement C>=A is exactly\nt<=(1+a)/(3+a).                                         (13)\nThe other requirement C>=-a A is t<=(1+a)/(2+a), which is weaker than(12).\nThus every plane comparison, including arbitrary past and future lags, is satisfied precisely at\nt<=min{1/(2+a),(1+a)/(3+a)}                              (14)\nfor a chain containing the relevant lag2 and future1 comparisons; in any shorter chain it is a sufficient condition. The zero plane additionally requires eta<= (1+a)(1-2t)/M, so a strictly positive common offset is available throughout(14).\n\nThe first term in(14) decreases with a, the second increases. Their crossing solves a^2+2a-1=0, namely a=sqrt2-1. The largest allowed t is therefore sqrt2-1, and the least admissible common mass ratio is\nM/(s+1)=1+sqrt2.                                        (15)\nThis is an exact optimization INSIDE the stated stationary frame construction. It is not a profile comparison and not an optimality theorem for general GD. The special case a=0 needs M>=3(s+1), recovering the independent-state relay's restriction. The correlated choice repairs repeated(1,4), since M=5,s+1=2 and5/2>1+sqrt2.\n\nFor a varying block partition, formulas(4)--(6) remain explicit with the actual M_i and actual endpoint query values c=1,s_i+1. With nonnegative plane offsets eta_i, full legality is the finite system\nA_i(c)-eta_i>=0,\nA_i(c)-eta_i>=<w_j,u_i-cw_i>-eta_j for every j,\nat those two c endpoints. The comparisons are affine in c, so they cover all intermediate nonnegative partial sums. This is a precise extension mechanism with all remote planes retained. The robust interval construction above is an actual solved nonstationary case of that system.\n\n## 7. Target-scale limitation still visible\nThe threshold(15) alone does not imply that arbitrary schedules admit a strong relay decomposition. In particular it requires a selected checkpoint b to satisfy\nb >= sqrt2 s+(1+sqrt2)\nin the stationary case, so it does not capture arbitrary smaller checkpoints after large ordinary blocks. The intended lower-bound profile is not obtained by asserting that these decompositions always exist.\n\nThe next mutation must use variable block-start distances, nonstationary correlations, or within-block changing gradients while keeping the full comparisons(4)--(6) or their exact replacement. The concrete achievement here is a globally legal correlated family that repairs the previous relay's hostile(1,4) test, survives arbitrary parameter changes b_i in[4,5], and exposes an exact correlation-versus-history tradeoff. The all-schedule (1-o(1))U_n^ZJ inequality remains open.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_markov_relay_mutation.md", "guidance": "66447c4a378fcc32", "prior_finding": "11bf723a8eab9940", "fact_ids": ["0e7e78056dc6258c"], "status": "complete candidate; no verification allocation requested", "new_mechanism": "nonzero all-lag temporal correlations, variable energies, common positive affine offsets, exact all-plane formulas", "target_gap": "No uniform profile comparison or strong arbitrary-schedule relay decomposition proved"}, "glossary": {"u_i": "Unit block-start vectors generated by the explicitly stated AR covariance recursion, not queried gradients.", "r": "Common negative lag-one correlation of the unit block-start vectors, local to this relay model.", "w_i": "Affine-plane normal(u_i-u_(i+1))/M_i and the gradient on its designated block.", "M_i": "Full ordinary-plus-checkpoint mass of a relay block; M_m is an artificial final normal scale.", "J": "The one fixed maximum of zero and affine planes used in the Moreau envelope.", "eta_i": "Nonnegative affine-plane offset; common eta=1/50 in the solved varying-block case.", "A_i(c)": "Self-plane inner product at the candidate proximal pointu_i-cw_i.", "p_b": "Scalar quadratic multiplier(1-lambda)(1-b lambda) of a tested block."}}
{"id": "695cc7b3cda3b59e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:37:16.376898+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Earlier star-outgoing weights admit a concrete reset criterion nu_i>2 prod_{t>i}max(1,(h_t-1)^2), while exact endpoint averaging fails through even one later unit step.", "evidence": "# Earlier reset weights: a quantitative bound and an exact obstruction to naive propagation\n\nStatus: unverified supplement to the full-certificate descent search. All claims below are proved directly; no earlier unverified finding is used as an assumed theorem.\n\n## 1. A reset criterion for any earlier star-outgoing multiplier\n\nNormalize L=1, f_*=0. Consider a full feasible certificate at level q for a length-n schedule h. Let nu_i=lambda_{*,i}>0 for some i<n. Define\n\n    delta_i=f(x_i)-||g_i||^2/2 >=0,\n    A_i=prod_{t=i+1}^{n-1} max{1,(h_t-1)^2}.\n\nThe full PSD certificate, with the term nu_i Q_{*,i}=-nu_i delta_i isolated, gives for every legal function\n\n    2f(x_n)+nu_i delta_i <= q||x_0-x_*||^2.                 (1)\n\nReplace h_i by one, leaving every other step in place. The changed trajectory agrees with the old one through x_i and reaches y=x_i-g_i at the next iteration. Unit-step descent gives f(y)<=delta_i.\n\nFor any nonnegative step a and any current point z, smoothness gives\n\n    f(z-a grad f(z)) <= f(z)+(a^2/2-a)||grad f(z)||^2.\n\nWhen 0<=a<=2, this is at most f(z). When a>2, use ||grad f(z)||^2<=2f(z) to obtain an upper bound (a-1)^2 f(z). Thus the unchanged suffix multiplies the objective by at most A_i, and\n\n    W_n(h with h_i replaced by 1) <= (2A_i/nu_i) q.          (2)\n\nIn particular nu_i>2A_i gives a genuine fixed-length strict improvement. Every tight certificate at a globally minimizing schedule must therefore obey\n\n    nu_i<=2A_i, 0<=i<n.                                    (3)\n\nAt i=n-1, direct endpoint convexity gives the much stronger conclusion nu_{n-1}=0 by the explicit last-step descent already recorded separately. Bound (3) is useful for earlier indices but does NOT assert that their multipliers vanish.\n\nThe product A_i is a proved worst-case growth bound, not a guess about conditional history. It can be large and does not itself yield the exact Zhang--Jiang profile.\n\n## 2. Exact failure of carrying final-step convexity through even one later unit step\n\nA tempting extension is to move an earlier step toward one and then use convexity to average the two resulting terminal objective values after the unchanged suffix. This fails for a single unchanged unit step.\n\nDefine an everywhere finite convex 1-smooth function on the real line by\n\n    f(x)=x^2/2,                       x<=1;\n         x-1/2,                       1<=x<=2;\n         (x-1)^2/2+1,                 2<=x<=3;\n         2x-3,                        x>=3.\n\nIts derivative is, respectively, x, 1, x-1, and 2. It is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. The unique minimum is f(0)=0.\n\nWrite T(x)=x-f'(x), the unit GD update, and F(x)=f(T(x)). The following exact values hold:\n\n    F(3/2)=1/8,\n    F(5/2)=1/2,\n    F(2)=1/2 > (F(3/2)+F(5/2))/2 =5/16.                   (4)\n\nThus f composed with a unit GD step is not convex, even though f itself is convex and 1-smooth.\n\nThe points in (4) arise from exactly the proposed schedule interpolation. Start at x_0=9/2, where g_0=2. The first step b=3/2 gives x_1=3/2. Replacing it by the unit step gives x_1=5/2. Taking their midpoint step b=5/4 gives x_1=2. Follow all three by the same final unit step. Their final objective values are respectively 1/8, 1/2, and 1/2. Hence the middle schedule's value exceeds the convex average of the endpoint schedules by 3/16, with a common function and common initial point.\n\nThis refutes the exact propagation step needed to extend the penultimate convex-blend proof unchanged to earlier times. It does not refute a different simultaneous schedule/certificate perturbation, and the example is not claimed to be a matching worst-case stationary tuple.\n\n## 3. Consequence for the active program\n\nThe final-step descent uses literal convexity of f along the final update line. Earlier reset changes enter a nonlinear suffix, so that proof cannot simply be repeated backward. The quantitative fallback (2) survives, but requires sufficiently large multiplier weight relative to the proved suffix-growth factor.\n\nLikewise the terminal-pair Schur-range descent controls a perturbation at the final point directly; an earlier gradient relation cannot be treated as if the rest of the GD trajectory were affine. Any propagation mechanism must retain or bound the actual suffix response. This is the concrete obstruction to the attempted backward extension, not a conclusion that all full-certificate approaches fail.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_earlier_reset_obstruction.md", "branch_id": "max2_huber_gap_descent", "prior_findings": ["471bfbf7f226fc04", "c60f5c7eb36efc1b"], "status": "quantitative earlier-weight constraint plus refuted naive backward convexity propagation", "evidence_status": "unverified; no additional submission"}, "glossary": {"nu_i": "The optimum-to-sample multiplier lambda_{*,i} in the full certificate.", "delta_i": "The nonnegative residual f(x_i)-||g_i||^2/2 after normalization.", "A_i": "The proved suffix objective-growth factor product_{t=i+1}^{n-1}max{1,(h_t-1)^2}.", "T": "The unit-gradient update T(x)=x-f'(x) in the explicit scalar counterexample.", "F": "The composed objective F(x)=f(T(x)) in that counterexample."}}
{"id": "27cbc4f5a2e9be3c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:41:05.753339+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Explicit full-certificate transport of star-outgoing weight: an adjacent algebraic identity preserves flow and adds a PSD square, with a precise forward-edge capacity at h_i>1. Reaching the penultimate point yields actual strict descent.", "evidence": "# Explicit forward transfer of optimum-outgoing multipliers\n\nStatus: unverified new certificate operation. It preserves the standard full matrix and nonnegative interpolation multipliers under the stated capacity condition.\n\n## 1. One adjacent transfer\n\nUse the signed smooth-convex quantities\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,\n\nwith the optimum sample * equal to zero. Fix a positive step h_i=a>0, so x_{i+1}=x_i-a g_i. A direct expansion gives the polynomial identity\n\n    Q_{*,i+1}+(1/a)Q_{i+1,i}+((1-a)/a)Q_{i,i+1}\n       -Q_{*,i}\n       =(1/a)||g_{i+1}-(1-a)g_i||^2.                       (I)\n\nFor clarity, write delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2. Then\nQ_{i+1,i}=delta_{i+1}-delta_i+||g_{i+1}||^2+(a-1)<g_i,g_{i+1}>,\nand\nQ_{i,i+1}+Q_{i+1,i}=(1-a)||g_i||^2+||g_{i+1}||^2+(a-2)<g_i,g_{i+1}>.\nSubstitution yields (I), including all coefficients and signs.\n\nTake any full feasible upper certificate with multipliers lambda and PSD slack M. Choose t>0 satisfying\n\n    t<=lambda_{*,i},\n    t<=a lambda_{i,i+1}/(a-1)       when a>1.                (C)\n\nWhen 0<a<=1 the second condition is absent. Change only these four multipliers:\n\n    lambda'_{*,i}=lambda_{*,i}-t,\n    lambda'_{*,i+1}=lambda_{*,i+1}+t,\n    lambda'_{i+1,i}=lambda_{i+1,i}+t/a,\n    lambda'_{i,i+1}=lambda_{i,i+1}+t(1-a)/a.                (T)\n\nAll multipliers remain nonnegative by (C), and each node's flow divergence is unchanged. The schedule h and certificate level q are unchanged. The FULL slack changes by\n\n    M'=M+(t/a)||g_{i+1}-(1-a)g_i||^2 >=0.                  (P)\n\nThus (T) is an explicit feasible motion in the original certificate variables, not merely a reduced row-sum operation. It neither assumes stationarity nor removes any PSD constraint.\n\nFor a<=1, the transfer adds or preserves the forward-edge multiplier.
