For a>1 it consumes exactly t(a-1)/a of the existing forward edge lambda_{i,i+1}. At a=1 the added square is t||g_{i+1}||^2 and no forward capacity is consumed.\n\n## 2. Transport across a whole interval\n\nSuppose lambda_{*,i}>0 for some i<n-1. Assume that for every j=i,...,n-2, either\n\n    0<h_j<=1,\n\nor\n\n    h_j>1 and lambda_{j,j+1}>0.                             (A)\n\nChoose t>0 at most lambda_{*,i} and at most every finite capacity\n\n    h_j lambda_{j,j+1}/(h_j-1),  j in [i,n-2] with h_j>1.\n\nThere are finitely many strictly positive capacities, so such a t exists. Apply (T) successively at j=i,...,n-2, always transferring the same amount t. Intermediate optimum-outgoing weights receive t and then pass it on, while the consumed adjacent forward edges are distinct. The resulting standard-format certificate has\n\n    lambda'_{*,i}=lambda_{*,i}-t,\n    lambda'_{*,n-1}=lambda_{*,n-1}+t>0,\n\nand unchanged other optimum-outgoing weights, schedule, objective level and flow divergence. Its full matrix is\n\n    M'=M+sum_{j=i}^{n-2}(t/h_j)\n             ||g_{j+1}-(1-h_j)g_j||^2 >=0.                 (B)\n\nNo arbitrary chronological crossing edge has been discarded; the operation modifies only the stated adjacent multipliers and retains the rest of the original matrix exactly.\n\n## 3. A resulting actual descent of the algorithm\n\nWrite nu=lambda'_{*,n-1}>0 and b=h_{n-1}. Isolating Q_{*,n-1}=-(f_{n-1}-||g_{n-1}||^2/2) in the transferred certificate gives\n\n    2f_n+nu delta_{n-1}<=q||x_0-x_*||^2.\n\nAt x=x_{n-1}, the unit-step point y=x-g_{n-1} satisfies f(y)<=delta_{n-1}. The old endpoint is z=x-b g_{n-1}. For epsilon=nu/(nu+2), c=1-epsilon, the new final step b_new=c b+epsilon gives z_new=c z+epsilon y. Convexity yields\n\n    2f(z_new)<=2c f(z)+2epsilon delta_{n-1}\n                =c[2f(z)+nu delta_{n-1}]\n                <=c q||x_0-x_*||^2.\n\nThus the concrete predetermined schedule with final step\n\n    b_new=(2b+nu)/(2+nu)\n\nhas the strict uniform bound\n\n    W_n(h_0,...,h_{n-2},b_new)<=2q/(2+nu)<q.\n\nThis is a genuine fixed-length improvement. The transfer phase stayed within the minimal full interpolation certificate, and the final convexity argument is valid for the same function in every instance. If desired, its explicit enlarged PSD certificate is obtained by adding the old endpoint and unit-step point as auxiliary analysis samples; its identity is given in research/max2_penultimate_flow_descent.md.\n\n## 4. A forced blocking pattern at global minimizers\n\nFor a globally minimizing positive schedule and ANY tight full certificate, lambda_{*,n-1}=0. Moreover, if lambda_{*,i}>0 with i<n-1, then there must exist at least one j in [i,n-2] such that\n\n    h_j>1 and lambda_{j,j+1}=0.                             (Block)\n\nOtherwise (A) would transport positive weight to the penultimate point and the preceding explicit schedule change would strictly improve the global value.\n\nThis is a necessary pattern in the actual full certificate. It does not say that every star-outgoing weight vanishes: an overrelaxed step with a zero forward edge can block this particular transfer. The final weight lambda_{*,n} is also outside the fixed-length transport argument.\n\n## 5. Tightness and matching primal data\n\nIf the original q is tight at the fixed h, every matching legal primal remains matching after any feasible transfer: h and q have not changed and the new certificate is globally valid. Because M' is the sum of M and the nonnegative squares in (B), each transferred square must vanish on that matching primal:\n\n    g_{j+1}=(1-h_j)g_j\n\nat every transfer edge. The new positive star weights also require delta_j=0 wherever they are placed. These are consequences of the full certificate and primal complementarity, not assumptions used to discard other inequalities.\n\nThis explains the restrictive nature of the transport. In particular, the earlier nonlinear-suffix counterexample does not invalidate (I): its relevant tight-certificate capacity conditions are not supplied. Conversely, it remains invalid to bypass a zero forward edge merely by claiming that the composed objective is convex.\n\n## 6. Scope of the new mechanism\n\nThe operation supplies a concrete way to exploit some earlier optimum-outgoing weights while preserving PSD and every multiplier sign. It isolates exact barriers h_j>1, lambda_{j,j+1}=0 rather than an unspecified missing separator.\n\nIt still does not show that a q>tau global minimizer must have a transferable weight, or that the full Schur defect can always remove a blocking edge. Those are distinct open structural questions. No implication to the exact profile U_n^ZJ is asserted.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_star_flow_transfer.md", "branch_id": "max2_huber_gap_descent", "status": "new full-feasible multiplier operation and exact global-minimizer blocking pattern", "evidence_status": "unverified; no new verifier call", "related_findings": ["471bfbf7f226fc04", "c60f5c7eb36efc1b", "9949b0d613e96cde"]}, "glossary": {"t": "The positive amount of optimum-outgoing interpolation multiplier transported across one or more chronological edges.", "delta_j": "The nonnegative residual f_j-||g_j||^2/2 after L=1, f_*=0 normalization.", "lambda'": "The explicitly modified nonnegative multipliers in formula (T).", "M'": "The original full PSD matrix plus the displayed nonnegative gradient squares.", "Block": "An index j with h_j>1 and lambda_{j,j+1}=0, which prevents this particular adjacent transfer."}}
{"id": "2a5fc1bcdb2df807", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:43:08.757599+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "New analytic upper-certificate interface after the numerical cap: retain E_h(u)>=nu||sum u_i g_i-(x0-xn)-kappa g_n||^2 with independent weight sum D. An exact3-by3 PSD transport rule is derived, and actual one- and two-step certificates test it. This is not a claimed improved recursion: the adjacent update forces nu'<=nu, and no growth law beating B_n is established.", "evidence": "# Active analytic interface: retain a squared gradient-aggregate residual\n\nStatus: self-contained unverified certificate algebra and two exact tests. This is an analytic mechanism after the600-evaluation discovery cap, not a claimed improved schedule family. No numerical scan extension or new verifier submission.\n\nWork at L=1. For a nonnegative schedule h of length n, use x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). Let u=(u_0,...,u_n) be nonnegative proof weights with D=sum u_i>0. These are certificate coefficients, not algorithmic state or additional GD updates. Define\nU=sum_i u_i g_i, d=x_0-x_n, e=U-d,\nE_h(u)=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_i u_i||g_i||^2+2sum_i u_i<g_i,x_0-x_i>-||U||^2.\n\nFor any minimizer x_star, direct expansion gives\n||x_0-x_star||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D(f_n-f_star)\n=||x_n-x_star-e||^2+E_h(u),\nwhere Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. Hence any bound\nE_h(u)>=nu||e-kappa g_n||^2, nu>=0,                 (A)\nimplies W_n(h)<=1/D. Unlike a saturated primitive certificate, D is independent of the physical sum and can be strictly below2sum(h)+1. The square preserves vector information about the difference between the certificate gradient aggregate and actual GD displacement.\n\nFor completeness Q_ij<=0 follows by applying the smooth descent inequality to F(z)=f(z)-<g_i,z>: x_i minimizes F, and F(x_j-grad F(x_j))<=F(x_j)-||grad F(x_j)||^2/2. This gives the displayed interpolation inequality; the smooth descent inequality follows by integrating the Lipschitz gradient along a segment.\n\n1. Exact tested one-step family.\nLet t=1+r with1/2<=r<=1 and take h=[t], u_0=1/r, u_1=(1-r)/r^2. Then D=1/r^2 and direct coefficient comparison gives\nE_h(u)=(2/r)(-Q_10)+((2r-1)/r^4)||r g_0+g_1||^2.\nBoth coefficients are nonnegative. Moreover\ne-g_1=((1-r-r^2)/r^2)(r g_0+g_1).\nIf1-r-r^2!=0, (A) therefore holds with\nkappa=1, nu=(2r-1)/(1-r-r^2)^2.\nIf1-r-r^2=0, e-g_1 vanishes identically, so any finite nu>=0 is allowed in (A). The objective bound is W_1(t)<=r^2, attained by f(x)=x^2/2. Thus this is a real unsaturated objective certificate rather than a proposed potential with no examples.\n\nA convenient rational seed is t=5/3, u=(3/2,3/4), D=9/4, kappa=1, nu=27. Its exact identity is\nE_[5/3](u)=3(-Q_10)+(27/16)||(2/3)g_0+g_1||^2\n          =3(-Q_10)+27||e-g_1||^2.\n\n2. Exact one-step transport as a3-by3 matrix condition.\nSuppose (A) is available for a block. Append a predetermined step t>=0. Put g=g_n, q=g_(n+1). Keep the old weights except increase u_n by alpha, and append the new weight beta. Require\nu_n+alpha>=0, beta>=0, D'=D+alpha+beta>0,\nwhere nu_n in this line means the old terminal weight u_n, not the coefficient nu. Equivalently, the first condition is simply u_n+alpha>=0. Define\nu'>=0, kappa' real, e'=e+(alpha-t)g+beta q.\nChoose mu>=0 with 2(D+alpha)+mu>=0.\n\nLet M be the symmetric3-by3 coefficient matrix in the vector variables(e,g,q) with entries\nM_ee=nu-nu',\nM_eg=-nu kappa-alpha-nu'(alpha-t),\nM_eq=-beta-nu'(beta-kappa'),\nM_gg=nu kappa^2+D+alpha+alpha(1-alpha)-nu'(alpha-t)^2-mu(t-1),\nM_qq=D+alpha+beta(1-beta)-nu'(beta-kappa')^2+mu,\nM_gq=(D+alpha)(t-1)+beta(t-alpha)-nu'(alpha-t)(beta-kappa')-(mu/2)(2-t).\n\nIf M is positive semidefinite, the extended schedule satisfies\nE_(h,t)(u')>=nu'||e'-kappa' q||^2.\nThis is an explicit sufficient matrix test with fixed dimension. Its identity is\nE_(h,t)(u')-nu'||e'-kappa'q||^2\n=E_h(u)-nu||e-kappa g||^2\n +[2(D+alpha)+mu](-Q_(n+1,n))+mu(-Q_(n,n+1))\n +(e,g,q)^T M(e,g,q).\nThe matrix quadratic form means the same coefficients paired with Euclidean inner products, so PSD suffices in every dimension.\n\nTo check the identity before imposing positivity, the exact energy increment is\nE_(h,t)(u')-E_h(u)\n=2(D+alpha)(f_n-f_(n+1))+alpha(1-alpha)||g||^2+beta(1-beta)||q||^2\n +2beta(t-alpha)<g,q>-2<e,alpha g+beta q>.\nSubstituting the two interpolation expressions gives the displayed matrix. No unknown interpolation theorem or numerical feasibility claim is hidden in this transport rule.\n\n3. A nontrivial exact transport test.\nFor the rational seed in part1, append t=1 and choose alpha=0,beta=1,nu'=27,kappa'=28/27,mu=0. Then\nM=diag(0,9/4,239/108)>=0.\nThus h=(5/3,1) with proof weights(3/2,3/4,1), D'=13/4, has the exact bound\nE_h(u')>=27||e'-(28/27)g_2||^2,\nso W_2(5/3,1)<=4/13. This bound is deliberately reported at its actual value; it is weaker than the project benchmark and supplies no negative answer to(P). Its role is to test that the new residual state transports with a nonzero coefficient and nonnegative interpolation multipliers.\n\n4. Necessary tests and the unresolved propagation issue.\nFor an all-affine Huber trajectory with constant sampled gradient and s=sum h,\nE_h(u)=D(2s+1-D)||g||^2, e=(D-s)g.\nThus (A) must obey\nnu(D-s-kappa)^2<=D(2s+1-D).\nFor f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, define p_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j), p=p_n and w=sum_i u_i p_i. Then it must also obey\n1-Dp^2-(1-w)^2>=nu[w-1+(1-kappa)p]^2.\nThese give immediate exact hostile tests for any proposed coefficient recursion.\n\nThe one-step matrix has M_ee=nu-nu', so this particular adjacent-interpolation update cannot increase nu. A proposed long-block rule must address this restriction, rather than claim that repeated application already gives the desired rate. No growth law for D has been proved. In particular no D_n>B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 has been obtained.\n\nA concrete extension to investigate is discounting old proof weights and retaining the full displacement as a vector coordinate. For0<=eta<=1,\nE_h(eta u)=eta E_h(u)+eta(1-eta)||U||^2.\nIf new weights are u'=(eta u+alpha at the old terminal sample,beta at the new sample), then\nE_(h,t)(u')=eta E_h(u)+eta(1-eta)||U||^2\n +2(eta D+alpha)(f_n-f_(n+1))+alpha(1-alpha)||g||^2+beta(1-beta)||q||^2\n +2beta(t-alpha)<g,q>+2<d-eta U,alpha g+beta q>.\nThe retained coordinates(d,U,g) transform linearly to(d+tg,eta U+alpha g+beta q,q). This gives a concrete next matrix interface; it is not a proved accelerated closure. Nonlocal interpolation links within concatenated blocks may be necessary to regain sufficient residual control. A complete candidate still needs a feasible infinite recursion, an actual objective upper bound, and an exact comparison with the true B_n profile.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_gradient_residual_transport", "research_path": "research/max4_gradient_residual_transport.md", "status": "exact unverified local interface; accelerated block closure unresolved", "prior_search_report": "650089feeda25479", "verification_status": "no submission; current allocation unchanged"}, "glossary": {"u": "Nonnegative proof weights on sampled gradients; not GD algorithm state.", "D": "Sum of proof weights, yielding W<=1/D if the intrinsic energy is nonnegative.", "U": "Weighted gradient aggregate sum_i u_i g_i.", "d": "Actual GD displacement x_0-x_n.", "e": "U-d, the difference between certificate aggregate and displacement.", "E_h": "Intrinsic dominance energy explicitly defined in the evidence.", "M": "The exact3-by3 transport matrix acting on(e,g_n,g_(n+1)).", "nu": "Nonnegative retained residual-square coefficient.", "kappa": "Scalar last-gradient alignment parameter.", "alpha": "Change to the old terminal proof weight when appending one step.", "beta": "New terminal proof weight.", "mu": "Nonnegative extra paired-interpolation multiplier.", "eta": "Optional discount factor on all old proof weights."}}
{"id": "ee5ddfe0b5f7f1f5", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:46:04.264980+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Variable-energy nonstationary Markov-gradient construction with positive affine offsets: an exact all-history signed-sum criterion yields W_(2m)>=301/400 for every mixed schedule(1,b_i) with4<=b_i<=17/4. Full block transfer/payoff formulas are explicit.", "evidence": "# Variable-energy Markov gradients with positive affine offsets\nStatus: complete new construction and exact quantitative test; unverified.\nNo new verifier allocation requested. This is a mutation of the fixed-radius relay, not a claim that its sufficient conditions hold for arbitrary h.\n\n## 1. A finite max-affine construction with a closed history criterion\nPartition a predetermined nonnegative schedule chronologically as\n(block_0,b_0,...,block_(m-1),b_(m-1),block_m),\nwhere block_i has sum s_i, following the project notation.\nChoose positive amplitudes alpha_i and positive virtual budgets t_i with\nt_i>=s_i+1,0<=i<=m.\nPut\nB_i^M=(s_i+b_i-t_i)alpha_i for0<=i<m,\nr_i^M=-t_(i+1)alpha_(i+1)/B_i^M,\nand require B_i^M>0 and -1<r_i^M<0.\nChoose unit vectors recursively in R^(m+1):\nzeta_0=e_0,\nzeta_(i+1)=r_i^M zeta_i+sqrt(1-(r_i^M)^2)e_(i+1).\nDefine omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i. Their correlations are explicitly\nC_ij=<zeta_i,zeta_j>=product_(l=min(i,j))^(max(i,j)-1)r_l^M\nfor i!=j, and C_ii=1.\nThis gives a real PSD Gram matrix and linearly independent gradients.\n\nLet nonnegative offsets eta_i be chosen and define the ONE fixed function\nJ(z)=max{0,max_(0<=i<=m)(<omega_i,z>-eta_i)},\nf(x)=min_z {J(z)+||x-z||^2/2}.                            (1)\nStart at X_0=t_0 alpha_0 zeta_0. One may impose t_0 alpha_0=1 for exact initial normalization; otherwise divide the final payoff by(t_0 alpha_0)^2.\nThe function f is convex differentiable1-smooth, minimized at0 with value0. These facts follow directly from the Moreau minimization of the finite nonnegative convex J. In particular, if plane i is active at z=x-omega_i, then the subgradient optimality condition gives grad f(x)=omega_i and\nf(x)=<omega_i,x>-||omega_i||^2/2-eta_i.                   (2)\nThis directly realizes all values; no interpolation theorem is being invoked as an unverified black box.\n\nDefine the intended block-start points by\nX_(i+1)=X_i-(s_i+b_i)omega_i.\nSet Z_i=X_i-t_i omega_i.\nThe following three EXPLICIT conditions are sufficient for every intended gradient and hence the exact supplied GD trajectory:\n(a) D_i^M:=B_i^M(1-(r_i^M)^2) satisfies\n    D_i^M>=|r_i^M|D_(i+1)^M,0<=i<m-1;                  (3)\n(b) C_ij alpha_j<=alpha_i for every distinct i,j;      (4)\n(c) with delta_i=t_i-s_i-1>=0,\n    0<=eta_i<=delta_i alpha_i^2,\n    eta_i-eta_j<=delta_i alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)\n    for every distinct i,j.                           (5)\n\nThese are conditions on a specified frame and specified affine offsets. Their feasibility at the target scale for arbitrary schedules is NOT assumed.\n\n## 2. Why the entire history is retained\nThe defining r_i^M gives the exact recurrence\nZ_0=0,\nZ_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i^M(zeta_i-r_i^M zeta_(i+1)).              (6)\nA direct induction using the Markov products gives\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,\nand, for j<i,\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=-sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l^M C_(j,l).             (7)\nTo verify(7), note that for j<=i,\n<zeta_i-r_i^M zeta_(i+1),zeta_j>\n=(1-(r_i^M)^2)C_(j,i),\nwhereas it is zero for j>=i+1.\n\nThe sum on the right of(7) is nonnegative. Pair its terms at l=j,j+1, then j+2,j+3, and so on. Each even-position product C_(j,l) is positive, and the paired contribution equals\nC_(j,l)[D_l^M-|r_l^M|D_(l+1)^M]>=0\nby(3). A possible final unpaired term is positive. Thus\n<Z_i,omega_j><=0 for j<i,\n<Z_i,omega_j>=0 for j>=i.                               (8)\nThe second line means equality to0, not positivity; equivalently, it is =0.\nNo old plane has been discarded.\n\nAt a query in block i let u be the partial ordinary step sum already used, so0<=u<=s_i. The intended point is x=X_i-u omega_i, and\nz=x-omega_i=Z_i+d omega_i,\nd=t_i-u-1>=delta_i.\nThe current plane has value d alpha_i^2-eta_i>=0.\nBy(8), any other plane has value at most\nd alpha_i C_ij alpha_j-eta_j.\nIts difference from the current plane is therefore at least\nd alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)-eta_i+eta_j.\nCondition(4) allows replacing d by its lower bound delta_i; then(5) proves nonnegativity. Consequently plane i dominates all other planes and the zero plane at every query. Formula(2) gives exactly the intended constant gradient throughout block_i, including its checkpoint query. The actual update reaches X_(i+1), completing the induction. Repeated points from zero steps cause no difficulty.\n\nThe terminal ordinary block follows omega_m in the same way. Its exact normalized value is\nW_n(h)>=\n [2(t_m-s_m-1/2)alpha_m^2-2eta_m]/(t_0 alpha_0)^2.      (9)\nEvery sampled value is explicitly\nf(X_i-u omega_i)=(t_i-u-1/2)alpha_i^2-eta_i.\nThe construction is a finite-dimensional global function, so every cross-time smooth-convex interpolation inequality and the optimum sample are satisfied.\n\nThe amplitude recursion can be written\nalpha_(i+1)=|r_i^M| (s_i+b_i-t_i)alpha_i/t_(i+1).          (10)\nThis displays the multiplicative transfer and its actual losses. The history price is(3)--(5), not an omitted all-schedule comparison.\n\n## 3. A genuinely nonstationary solved parameter range\nFix m>=1. Let every nonterminal ordinary block be the single unit step, and let\n4<=b_i<=17/4,0<=i<m.\nThe terminal ordinary block is empty. Thus n=2m.\n\nChoose\ndelta=1/100,\nt_i=2+delta=201/100 for EVERY i, including i=m,\nalpha_0=100/201,\nalpha_m=1/2,\nand choose the interior amplitudes arbitrarily in[49/100,1/2].\nThe initial amplitude also lies in this interval. Set\neta_i=(delta/2)alpha_i^2=alpha_i^2/200.\nAll offsets are strictly positive, and the squared gradient norms alpha_i^2 can vary independently through this interval, subject only to the stated initial and terminal values.\n\nFor nonterminal i,\nB_i^M=(b_i-1-delta)alpha_i.\nThe elementary bounds\n29/20 <= B_i^M <=13/8\nhold: the actual lower endpoint is(299/100)(49/100)>29/20 and the actual upper endpoint is(324/100)(1/2)<13/8.\nFurthermore\n|r_i^M|=(201/100)alpha_(i+1)/B_i^M\n <=(201/200)/(29/20)=201/290<7/10.                       (11)\nIt is strictly positive, so every new direction has a nonzero independent innovation.\n\nThe history weights satisfy\nD_i^M=B_i^M(1-|r_i^M|^2)\n >=(29/20)(1-49/100)=1479/2000.                          (12)\nFor the upper bound use\nD_i^M=B_i^M-[(201/100)alpha_(i+1)]^2/B_i^M.\nSince(201/100)alpha_(i+1)>=9849/10000>49/50 and B_i^M<=13/8,\nD_i^M <=13/8-(49/50)^2/(13/8)<21/20.                    (13)\nThe final strict comparison follows from\n(49/50)^2/(13/8)=19208/32500>23/40.\nCombining(11)--(13),\n|r_i^M|D_(i+1)^M <(7/10)(21/20)=147/200\n <1479/2000<=D_i^M.\nThus the complete-history condition(3) holds uniformly in all independent choices of b_i and interior alpha_i.\n\nFor distinct indices, |C_ij|<(7/10)^|i-j|<=7/10.\nIn particular C_ij alpha_j<=7/20<49/100<=alpha_i, proving(4).\nFor all distinct i,j,\nalpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)\n >=(49/100)^2-(7/10)(1/2)^2\n =651/10000.                                           (14)\nFor nonterminal phases delta_i=delta; for the terminal phase delta_m=1+delta, so always delta_i>=delta. On the other hand\neta_i-eta_j <=(delta/2)[(1/2)^2-(49/100)^2]\n =99delta/20000 <651delta/10000.\nTogether with eta_i=delta alpha_i^2/2<=delta_i alpha_i^2, this proves(5).\n\nThe function(1) therefore realizes the whole nonstationary schedule. The exact initial norm is t_0 alpha_0=1. The terminal point is X_m and alpha_m=1/2, giving\n2f(X_m)\n=2(201/100-1/2)(1/4)-2(1/200)(1/4)\n=301/400.                                               (15)\nHence every schedule in this parameter range has the explicitly constructed legal lower bound\nW_(2m)(1,b_0,...,1,b_(m-1))>=301/400\nfor all m>=1.\n\nThis improves the previous relay's quantitative test while using variable gradient energies, nonstationary correlations and positive affine offsets. No randomness is needed; randomly applying a common orthogonal rotation gives an equivalent legal law with the same payoff, without changing its strength.\n\n## 4. Nonseparability and the quadratic comparison\nThe gradients omega_0,...,omega_m are linearly independent by their nonzero fresh coordinates in the Markov recursion. Adjacent inner products are\n<omega_i,omega_(i+1)>=alpha_i alpha_(i+1)r_i^M,\nwhich are nonzero. The global function is not separable in any fixed orthonormal basis: isolation of the face between the zero plane and each plane i yields open smooth regions whose Hessians are the distinct rank-one projectors onto span(omega_i). The normals are independent and adjacent ones are nonorthogonal, so these projectors do not commute. Simultaneous diagonal Hessians would be necessary for orthogonal separability.\n\nFor the same schedules, the full quadratic-class normalized error tends uniformly to0. On a scalar eigenvalue lambda in[0,1], each block multiplier is\n(1-lambda)(1-b_i lambda).\nFor0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and at most exp(-lambda). On[1/5,1] its absolute value is at most4/5, because b_i belongs to[4,17/4], a subset of[4,5], and the negative minimum magnitude(b_i-1)^2/(4b_i) is at most4/5 while the positive endpoint bound is4/25. Therefore the final normalized quadratic error is at most\nmax{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)}.\nSpectral diagonalization proves the same statement in every finite dimension. This does not claim that all scalar nonlinear instances are controlled; it isolates a real nonlinear, nonseparable adversary with an explicit full-history certificate.\n\n## 5. Relation to the earlier failed local AR rule\nThe key addition is the signed-sum formula(7) and its global control by(3), together with positive face margins and the explicit cross-plane inequalities(4)--(5). Choosing only a locally valid AR covariance would not prove them.\n\nFor example, the old constant-energy rule on the single-step schedule(2,3) corresponds formally to ordinary sums0, budgets t_i=1, amplitudes1, and correlations -1 and-1/2. It is already degenerate at the first correlation. Its history coefficients would be D_0^M=0 and D_1^M=3/2, violating the paired condition0>=3/2. Thus the old nonadjacent failure is detected rather than repeated.\n\n## 6. Remaining target obligation\nEquations(9)--(10) are a genuine multiplicative high-dimensional transfer with full legality conditions. The present solved range is a stability/instability test for mixed short/long schedules, not the exact minimax theorem. No claim is made that a partition, amplitudes, virtual budgets and correlations meeting(3)--(5) with payoff(1-o(1))U_n^ZJ exist for every h.\n\nThe conditions reveal specific costs: the virtual budget t_i must cover every ordinary-block query; the history weights D_i^M must control alternating past sums; and positive offsets must remain below the actual face margin. The next search is to vary these quantities on the recursive hostile schedules, or to replace the paired sufficient condition by a sharper exact signed-sum analysis. Simply postulating the needed choice would be theorem-strength and remains BLOCKED.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_nonstationary_markov_offsets.md", "guidance": "66447c4a378fcc32", "prior_findings": ["11bf723a8eab9940", "ab7207a7c030bafb", "7806c71bf93294c6"], "status": "complete new candidate and exact nonstationary test; no verifier submission", "target_gap": "All-schedule choice satisfying the full signed-sum and offset constraints at U remains unproved"}, "glossary": {"zeta_i": "Unit Markov gradient direction constructed with a fresh orthonormal coordinate at each phase.", "omega_i": "Actual phase gradient alpha_i zeta_i.", "alpha_i": "Positive phase gradient amplitude.", "t_i": "Positive virtual phase budget at leasts_i+1.", "B_i^M": "Transfer weight(s_i+b_i-t_i)alpha_i.", "r_i^M": "Negative Markov correlation -t_(i+1)alpha_(i+1)/B_i^M.", "D_i^M": "History weight B_i^M(1-(r_i^M)^2).", "C_ij": "Product of the intervening Markov correlations, equal to<zeta_i,zeta_j>.", "X_i": "Actual point at the beginning of phase i.", "Z_i": "Residual point X_i-t_i omega_i, orthogonal to all current/future directions.", "eta_i": "Nonnegative affine-plane offset in the fixed global maximum J.", "delta_i": "Available face margin t_i-s_i-1.", "J": "The single maximum of zero and all affine planes used for Moreau smoothing."}}
{"id": "2973c2322cdee545", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:52:38.582782+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Exact conditional seed amplification completed: a certified primitive seed of length r and shifted sum A>(r+1)^rho would yield fixed relative improvement over the true ZJ profile at infinitely many horizons. Replacing all primitive leaves gives length(r+1)n and denominator at least [sqrt(A B_n)-sqrt(A)+1]^2; no benchmark convergence assumption is needed.", "evidence": "# Exact conditional amplification of a primitive seed\n\nStatus: complete unverified argument responding to master_guidance 56a30fdd80e74d9e. No seed meeting the hypothesis is known, and this is not a resolution of the unrestricted target. No additional verifier call is authorized.\n\n## Claim and interfaces\n\nUse the project's exact S_circle, S_bullet, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n=1/B_n. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2) and rho=log_2(lambda). Suppose a nonnegative primitive schedule a of length r>=1 has shifted capacity A=1+sum(a) satisfying\n\n    A>(r+1)^rho.                                      (1)\n\nThen there are predetermined nonnegative schedules H_n of length (r+1)n, constructed solely with the usual ConPP/ConPD operations and repeated copies of a, such that\n\n    W_((r+1)n)(H_n)\n      <=[sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)]^(-2).                (2)\n\nMoreover,\n\n    liminf_(n->infinity) W_((r+1)n)(H_n)/U_((r+1)n)\n      <=(r+1)^rho/A<1.                                (3)\n\nThus this stronger finite seed hypothesis is sufficient for the negative answer required by the project, along an infinite subsequence, without assuming convergence of B_n/n^rho or replacing the exact DP by a decimal profile.\n\nThe analytic schedule interfaces invoked here are the following standard Zhang–Jiang closure statements, stated to remove ambiguity about the hypotheses. At L=1 write Q_(i,star)=f_i-f_star-<g_i,x_i-x_star>+||g_i||^2/2. A schedule h with s=sum(h) is primitive if\n\n  ||x_0-x_star||^2+2sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,star)-2s(f_n-f_star)\n  >=||x_n-x_star||^2+s(s+1)||g_n||^2\n\nfor every legal smooth convex GD trajectory. A schedule h is dominant if there are nonnegative weights u_0,...,u_n summing to 2s+1 such that\n\n  ||x_0-x_star||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2(2s+1)(f_n-f_star)\n  >=||x_0-x_star-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.\n\nThe empty schedule has both properties. If a,b are primitive, ConPP(a,b)=[a,phi(sum(a),sum(b)),b] is primitive. If a is primitive and b is dominant, ConPD(a,b)=[a,psi(sum(a),sum(b)),b] is dominant. Dominance implies W(h)<=1/(2sum(h)+1), since all Q_(i,star)<=0. Sources: Zhang–Jiang, arXiv:2410.12395, definitions and ConPP/ConPD theorems; the downloaded HTML numbers these Theorems11 and12 and the dominant objective implication Theorem6 (the published/new-paper numbering is3.1,3.2 and2.6). These closure proofs were read in the preceding max4 rounds; this new amplification argument does not assert their promotion to new project facts. Any future verification package must include their proper external-reference metadata or verified predecessors.\n\nThe only accepted project fact used below is 0be8b54e0a33381c, for the explicit scalar G join and its square-root nonexpansiveness. Other ingredients are proved here. The proof of the scalar power inequality is adapted, with notation changed, from Liu–Chen–Jiang–Wang arXiv:2609.11788v1, Lemma5.1 and AppendixC.1; its full proof is included below, so no phase theorem is assumed. Two Matlas queries, including the exact title/identifier, returned unrelated results; no Matlas grounding is falsely claimed.\n\n## 1. A finite positive benchmark limsup\n\nFor shifted primitive masses x,y>=0 set\n\n  F(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2.\n\nLet q=1/rho. The inequality\n\n  F(x,y)^q<=x^q+y^q                                  (4)\n\nholds. Here is an elementary proof. Zero arguments give equality. For positive arguments write x=exp(m-t), y=exp(m+t), t>=0, using symmetry. Then\n\n  F(x,y)=exp(m+arsinh(cosh(t))).\n\nInequality(4) is equivalent to J(t)>=0, where\n\n  J(t)=q^(-1)log(2cosh(qt))-arsinh(cosh(t)).\n\nBecause 2^(1/q)=lambda, J(0)=0; direct exponential asymptotics give J(t)->0 as t->infinity. Its derivative is\n\n  J'(t)=tanh(qt)-sinh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2).\n\nDefine alpha(t)=artanh(sinh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2)). Its derivative is cosh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2), strictly increasing from1/sqrt(2) to1. Also1/sqrt(2)<q<1: lambda>2 gives the upper inequality; lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)<16 gives q>3/4>1/sqrt(2). Hence alpha(t)-qt is strictly convex, vanishes at0, initially decreases, and tends to infinity. Its derivative increases to1-q>0, so it has exactly one positive zero t_0. Monotonicity of tanh implies J'>0 on(0,t_0) and J'<0 on(t_0,infinity). The two zero endpoint limits imply J(t)>0 for t>0, proving(4).\n\nWrite P_k=1+S_circle(k). Every primitive DP tree with k+1 leaves has root capacity at most(k+1)^rho by(4) and induction from unit leaves. Thus\n\n  P_k<=(k+1)^rho.                                    (5)\n\nThe shifted dominant join is\n\n  G(x,d)=2x+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8xd)/2.\n\nSince sqrt(d^2+8xd)<=d+4x, one has G(x,d)<=d+4x. Unroll an optimizing dominant tree along its unique dominant continuation. If the attached primitive subtrees have leaf counts l_1,...,l_t, then each l_i>=1 and sum_i l_i=n. The final dominant leaf has mass1. By(5),\n\n  B_n<=1+4sum_i l_i^rho<=1+4n^rho.                    (6)\n\nThe final inequality follows by repeated(a+b)^rho>=a^rho+b^rho for a,b>=0 and rho>1; for fixed a its difference has nonnegative derivative in b and vanishes at b=0.\n\nConversely, balanced primitive trees with2^k leaves attain shifted mass lambda^k, since F(x,x)=lambda x. For n=2^k the legal dominant split consisting of this(n-1)-step primitive prefix, one ConPD bridge, and an empty continuation gives\n\n  B_n>=G(lambda^k,1)>=2lambda^k=2n^rho.              (7)\n\nConsequently the real number\n\n  K=limsup_(n->infinity) B_n/n^rho\n\nexists in the extended-real sense and satisfies2<=K<=4. In particular it is finite and strictly positive. No single normalized limit or periodic-phase theorem is used.\n\n## 2. Replace every primitive leaf; retain the terminal dominant leaf\n\nFor each n>=1 choose one fully expanded optimizing dominant DP tree T_n. It has n internal nodes and n+1 leaves. Exactly one leaf, at the end of its dominant continuation, has dominant type. All other n leaves have primitive type. This count follows inductively: at each dominant node the left subtree is a pure primitive tree and the right subtree contains exactly one dominant leaf; all-primitive nodes have two primitive children.\n\nReplace each of the n primitive empty leaves by the certified schedule a. Keep the single dominant terminal leaf empty. Re-evaluate every bridge with phi or psi from its new child sums. This specifies a concrete nonnegative schedule H_n independent of the objective. Closure gives a primitive schedule at every primitive node and a dominant schedule at every dominant node. The original n bridge steps remain, and the inserted copies contribute rn steps. Therefore\n\n  length(H_n)=(r+1)n.                                (8)\n\nPositive homogeneity of F implies that every primitive subtree's mass is now exactly A times its old mass. Let D_n=2sum(H_n)+1. Along the dominant continuation compare the true construction with an auxiliary scalar evaluation having the same scaled primitive children but terminal scalar mass A instead of1. Homogeneity of G says that the auxiliary root mass is exactly A B_n.\n\nFor fixed x>=0 let\n\n  T_x(z)=(z+sqrt(z^2+8x))/2.\n\nAccepted fact0be8b54e0a33381c proves sqrt(G(x,d))=T_x(sqrt(d)) and that T_x is increasing and1-Lipschitz on nonnegative arguments. The true and auxiliary terminal square-root masses differ by sqrt(A)-1. Propagate both through the identical sequence of T_x maps. The difference remains between0 and sqrt(A)-1. Hence\n\n  sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)<=sqrt(D_n)<=sqrt(A B_n).    (9)\n\nThe lower bound is at least1 because B_n>=1. Dominance gives W(H_n)<=1/D_n, which proves(2). This handles the unmatched dominant leaf explicitly rather than scaling a nonexistent terminal schedule.\n\n## 3. The input/output subsequences and exact comparison\n\nPut m=r+1. Choose integers n_j->infinity with B_(n_j)/n_j^rho->K. From(9) and B_n=O(n^rho),\n\n  D_(n_j)/n_j^rho->A K.\n\nIndeed the difference A B_n-D_n is bounded above by2(sqrt(A)-1)sqrt(A B_n), hence is O(n^(rho/2)) and becomes zero after division by n^rho. On the output horizons mn_j, the definition of the same global limsup gives\n\n  limsup_j B_(mn_j)/(mn_j)^rho<=K.\n\nBoth statements concern the same input subsequence and its multiplied output subsequence. Their inequality directions therefore yield\n\n  limsup_j W_(mn_j)(H_(n_j))/U_(mn_j)\n  <=limsup_j B_(mn_j)/D_(n_j)\n  <=m^rho/A.\n\nThis proves(3). Under(1), choosing delta=(1-m^rho/A)/2>0 ensures W_(mn_j)(H_(n_j))<=(1-delta)U_(mn_j) for all sufficiently large j. The lengths mn_j are unbounded, as required.\n\n## What this does and does not change\n\nThe stronger seed score A/m^rho>1 is now accompanied by a complete amplification and exact-profile comparison. A seed merely exceeding P_r, but failing this stronger score, is not settled by this argument. No such above-power primitive seed was found in the authorized600-call experiment. The numerical cap remains exhausted; this conditional result does not authorize restarting that search. The current weighted-gradient residual approach addresses a different, nonsaturated certificate class and still lacks a competitive infinite recursion.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"research_path": "research/max4_primitive_seed_amplification.md", "guidance_id": "56a30fdd80e74d9e", "fact_ids": ["0be8b54e0a33381c"], "sources": ["arXiv:2410.12395", "arXiv:2609.11788v1"], "status": "unverified conditional theorem; seed hypothesis unmet; no extra verifier allocation"}, "glossary": {"A": "One plus the physical sum of the proposed primitive seed a.", "r": "Number of steps in seed a; m=r+1.", "K": "Finite positive limsup of exact B_n/n^rho, shown here to lie between2 and4.", "H_n": "Schedule obtained by replacing all n primitive leaves of an optimal n-step dominant DP tree with seed a, retaining the sole empty dominant leaf.", "D_n": "Dominant denominator2sum(H_n)+1.", "F": "Shifted ConPP scalar join(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2.", "lambda": "1+sqrt(2); rho=log_2(lambda) agrees with project exponent."}}
{"id": "e8b0c9638c387b6b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:56:22.849570+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Closest-minimizer audit strengthens all three Moreau relays. Exact/upper distances improve the unchanged independent and Markov-state lower bounds to9/14 and8568/13595; explicit offset/terminal retuning gives W_(2m)>=2 uniformly on every(1,b_i),5<=b_i<=11/2.", "evidence": "# Minimizer-distance audit for the three Moreau relays\nStatus: complete explicit calculations, unverified; no new verifier call.\nResponds to master_guidance aded2db8c57a7eae. These are restricted schedule constructions, not the exact minimax target.\n\n## 1. The minimizer set and a projection certificate\nFor any finitely many vectors w_i and nonnegative offsets eta_i let\nJ(z)=max(0,max_i(<w_i,z>-eta_i)),\nf(x)=min_z(J(z)+||x-z||^2/2).\nThe minimum defining f is attained because its nonnegative first term plus the quadratic is coercive. Thus f(x)=0 if and only if both nonnegative terms vanish, equivalently x=z and J(x)=0. Hence\nargmin f = P := {z:<w_i,z><=eta_i for every i}.\nIn particular 0 is a minimizer but need not be closest to x_0.\n\nA direct projection certificate is: p belongs to P and\nx_0-p=sum_i lambda_i w_i, lambda_i>=0,\nwith lambda_i=0 unless <w_i,p>=eta_i.\nFor any z in P, <x_0-p,z-p><=0; expanding ||x_0-z||^2 then proves ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0-p||^2. This proves the certificate without invoking an external projection theorem. A feasible p alone gives the upper bound dist(x_0,P)^2<=||x_0-p||^2, sufficient to strengthen a normalized lower bound.\n\nFor completeness, each use of a Moreau relay below is certified by this direct rule: if the indicated affine plane is active at z=x-w_i, then w_i is a subgradient of J there, so z is the unique minimizer and grad f(x)=w_i, f(x)=<w_i,x>-||w_i||^2/2-eta_i. Subgradient monotonicity proves that grad f is1-Lipschitz. Convexity follows by partial minimization, and uniqueness plus the two minimizing inequalities gives differentiability.\n\n## 2. Original independent-state relay, unchanged\nTake m>=2, 5<=b_i<=11/2, M_i=1+b_i, rho_i=1/M_i, w_i=rho_i(e_i-e_(i+1)) for i<m, w_m=e_m/6, eta_i=1/36, x_0=e_0.\nThe legal trajectory for h=(1,b_0,...,1,b_(m-1)) has two queries e_i,e_i-w_i, then reaches e_(i+1), and terminates at e_m with 2f(e_m)=1/4.\nHere is a self-contained check. At the proximal points e_i-cw_i with1<=c<=2, the current inner product is rho_i-2c rho_i^2>=1/18. The next inner product is c rho_i rho_(i+1), using rho_m=1/6; their difference is rho_i[1-c(2rho_i+rho_(i+1))]>=0 because all rho's are<=1/6. The previous inner product is nonpositive; all others are0. Equal offsets cancel, and the current plane exceeds0. At the terminal proximal point(5/6)e_m, the terminal plane value is1/9 and every other plane is negative. This proves every prescribed gradient and the value1/8.\n\nThe point\np=(1/2)e_0+(1/3)e_1+(1/6)e_2\nlies in P: every positive neighboring coordinate difference is at most1/6, and rho_i<=1/6. The terminal coordinate is at most1/6 as well. Its squared distance from e_0 is7/18. Therefore\nW_(2m)(h)>= (1/4)/(7/18)=9/14.\nWhen b_0=b_1=5, the distance is EXACT: e_0-p=3w_0+w_1 and both constraints are active. Later b_i may still vary in the stated interval. For m=1 the feasible point p=(1/3)e_0+(1/6)e_1 gives distance17/36 and lower bound9/17, exact distance when b_0=5 by the same multiplier check.\n\n## 3. Original Markov-state relay, unchanged\nTake m>=2, r=-2/5, u_0=e_0, u_(i+1)=r u_i+sqrt(1-r^2)e_(i+1) through i=m. Let M_i=1+b_i in[5,6] for i<m and M_m=5. Set w_i=(u_i-u_(i+1))/M_i, eta_i=1/50, x_0=u_0.\nThe exact terminal value is 2f(u_m)=51/125.\nTo retain the full function certificate, at z=u_i-cw_i,1<=c<=2,\n<w_i,z>=(1-r)/M_i[1-2c/M_i],\n<w_(i+k),z>=(1-r)r^(k-1)/M_(i+k)[r+c(1-r)/M_i],\n<w_(i-k),z>=(1-r)r^(k-1)/M_(i-k)[-1+c(1-r)/M_i].\nAfter division by(1-r)/M_i, every other value is at most the maximum of\n-2M_i/25+7c/25, 4M_i/125-14c/125, 2M_i/25-14c/125.\nThe differences from the current value1-2c/M_i are minimized at c=2,M_i=5 and are respectively1/25,33/125,3/125. The current inner product is at least7/125>1/50. Thus every supporting plane is checked and both gradients equal w_i. The checkpoint gives u_(i+1) exactly. The terminal query uses c=1,M_m=5 and gives the displayed terminal value.\n\nDefine\np=(23/56)u_0+(49/120)u_1+(7/40)u_2+(1/168)u_3.\nThe four coefficients sum to1. Direct use of <u_i,u_j>=r^|i-j| gives\n<p,u_0>=11/40, <p,u_1>=7/40, <p,u_2>=3/40, <p,u_3>=-1/40.\nConsequently <p,u_i-u_(i+1)>=1/10 for i=0,1,2. For i>=3 that inner product equals\n(1-r)r^(i-3)(-1/40),\nwhose largest positive value is7/500<1/10.\nSince M_i>=5, every constraint <w_i,p><=1/50 holds. This includes the terminal plane.\nFurthermore ||p||^2=829/4200, so\n||u_0-p||^2=1-2(11/40)+829/4200=2719/4200.\nThus uniformly over every varying b_i in[4,5],\nW_(2m)(h)>= (51/125)/(2719/4200)=8568/13595.\nWhen M_0=M_1=M_2=5 (M_2 is the fixed terminal mass if m=2), this is the exact minimizer distance. Indeed\nu_0-p=(33/56)(u_0-u_1)+(19/105)(u_1-u_2)+(1/168)(u_2-u_3),\na positive combination of precisely the three active normals after multiplication of each coefficient by its M_i. No claim of exactness is made for arbitrary varying first masses; feasibility suffices for the lower bound.\n\n## 4. Exact distance for the newest Markov-gradient model\nThis calculation is independent of the relay's history inequalities. Let unit directions zeta_i have correlations C_ij, positive amplitudes alpha_i, omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i, and suppose\nC_ij alpha_j<=alpha_i for every distinct i,j.\nLet eta_i=kappa alpha_i^2 for a common kappa>=0, and take x_0=t_0 alpha_0 zeta_0 with t_0>kappa. Then\np=kappa alpha_0 zeta_0\nbelongs to P: <omega_j,p>=kappa alpha_j alpha_0 C_0j<=kappa alpha_j^2=eta_j, using the hypothesis with i=j and j=0. Hence EXACTLY\ndist(x_0,P)^2=(t_0-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2.\nFor the parameters explicitly constructed in ee5ddfe0b5f7f1f5, t_0=201/100,alpha_0=100/201,kappa=1/200, so the distance is401/402. The unchanged terminal value301/400 therefore corresponds to the strengthened normalized error\n(301/400)(402/401)^2.\nThis last lower-bound reading is conditional on that candidate's fully written trajectory proof, which remains unverified; the projection identity itself was proved here from the displayed hypotheses.\n\n## 5. Retuning the first relay's offsets and terminal normal\nThere is also a strictly stronger explicit version of Section2, with the same block trajectories:\nkeep the nonterminal w_i=rho_i(e_i-e_(i+1)), use eta_i=1/18 for i<m, and replace the terminal data by\nw_m=(2/3)e_m, eta_m=2/9.\nAll nonterminal comparisons except the final next-plane comparison are unchanged. The zero-plane comparison holds since rho-4rho^2>=1/18. At the last nonterminal query, the difference between the current and terminal affine planes is minimized at c=2 and equals\n1/6-rho/3-4rho^2>=0\nfor rho in[2/13,1/6]; the expression decreases in rho and vanishes at1/6.
