Thus grad f(e_m)=(2/3)e_m and f(e_m)=2/9.\n\nFor every m>=1,\np=(2/3)e_0+(1/3)e_1\nbelongs to the new P. Every relevant positive coordinate difference is at most1/3; rho_i/3<=1/18, and the terminal condition is equality if m=1 or has left side0 if m>=2. Its squared initial distance is2/9. Therefore this new fixed legal nonlinear function proves\nW_(2m)(1,b_0,...,1,b_(m-1))>=2\nfor every5<=b_i<=11/2.\nIf b_0=5 then e_0-p=2w_0 and constraint0 is active, so2/9 is the exact distance and the normalized value of this instance is exactly2.\n\nThese calculations strengthen constant-level instability tests, not an arbitrary-schedule lower bound. Neither a minimizer projection nor these changes of offsets supply the missing uniform comparison to U_n^ZJ.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_minimizer_projection.md", "responds_to": "aded2db8c57a7eae", "related_candidates": ["11bf723a8eab9940", "ab7207a7c030bafb", "ee5ddfe0b5f7f1f5"], "status": "complete explicit normalization calculations; unverified", "verification_allocation": "unchanged"}, "glossary": {"P": "The exact minimizer polyhedron of the max-affine Moreau envelope, defined in Section1.", "p": "A candidate closest minimizer, distinct from the benchmark exponent p elsewhere; this finding uses p only as the displayed vector.", "kappa": "Common coefficient in the offsets eta_i=kappa alpha_i^2 in Section4.", "lambda_i": "Nonnegative projection-certificate multipliers, defined in Section1."}}
{"id": "cd0f93f08a50828f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:58:45.960250+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Variable-step extension of the new interval-certificate method: explicit matrices diagonalize the GD trajectory form for arbitrary varying steps, and the entire zero-PSD-remainder certificate class admits a scalar triangular elimination. A necessary caution is exact: nonnegative interval coefficients already exclude the three-step silver schedule, but signed interval cancellation recovers a fully nonnegative interpolation certificate.", "evidence": "# Variable-step interval matrices and their first exact obstruction\n\nStatus: unverified analytic identities and a complete three-step test, not an improved schedule. This extends the matrix construction in Zhao, arXiv:2609.11784v1, Sections3.1–3.3, whose definitions and proof were read from a local downloaded/extracted copy. No external theorem is needed for the calculations below. The authorized numerical budget remains exhausted.\n\nWork at L=1 with x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, i=0,...,n-1. Define the trajectory matrix T by T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, and T_ij=0 for j>i. The symbol T is used to avoid confusion with the benchmark denominator B_n. Thus (Tg)_i=g_i+x_0-x_i.\n\nFor any matrix C with zero row sums, write b=C^T1 and H=CT+T^TC^T. Define the nonnegative interpolation quantity\n\n  I_ij=2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nIt equals -2Q_ji in the project's sign convention. Direct expansion gives\n\n  sum_(i,j) C_ji I_ij\n  =2sum_i b_i f_i+sum_(i,j) H_ij<g_i,g_j>-sum_i b_i||g_i||^2.    (A)\n\nDiagonal entries of C are immaterial to the sum because I_ii=0. If C has nonnegative off-diagonal entries, the left-hand side is nonnegative on every legal trajectory.\n\n## 1. Interval diagonalization survives arbitrary steps\n\nFix0<=a<b<=n and positive numbers w_a,...,w_b. Put\n\n  L_i=sum_(a<=j<i)w_j, R_i=sum_(i<j<=b)w_j,\n  u_i=h_i(L_i+w_i)-w_i,\n  v_i=w_i+h_i R_i.\n\nAt i=n choose any auxiliary h_n: all formulas in which it could affect the matrix cancel because R_n=0 and no entry lies above its diagonal. Define C^(a,b;w), zero outside the interval, by\n\n  C_ij=u_i w_j for a<=i<j<=b,\n  C_ij=v_i w_j for a<=j<i<=b,\n  C_ii=-u_i R_i-v_i L_i.\n\nIts row sums vanish. For i<j in the interval,\n\n  (CT)_ij=u_i(w_j+h_j R_j)=u_i v_j,\n  (CT)_ji=v_j(w_i-h_i(L_i+w_i))=-v_j u_i.\n\nFor a column before a, its entries vanish because the row sum is zero; for a column after b, its entries vanish by triangularity. Hence H=CT+T^TC^T is diagonal, with\n\n  H_ii=2[(h_i-1)u_i R_i-v_i L_i].                    (B)\n\nIf h_i>=1 for a<=i<b, all off-diagonal entries of this interval matrix are nonnegative. For the special choice w_i=1, put s=i-a and t=b-i. Formula(B) becomes\n\n  H_ii=2[(h_i-1)^2(s+1)t-s(t+1)].                   (C)\n\nThis is the constant-step paper's diagonal formula with the actual local step h_i inserted; no equality of the steps was needed for diagonalization.\n\n## 2. The primitive coefficient target\n\nLet S=sum_(i<n)h_i. The saturated primitive intrinsic potential is\n\n  V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)\n      +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nThus(A) equals V_h exactly when\n\n  b=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),-S),\n  H=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-S(S+2)).   (D)\n\nNonnegative off-diagonal entries of C then certify V_h>=0. This is a sufficient certificate class with zero PSD remainder, not a necessity claim for arbitrary primitive schedules.\n\nThere is an algebraic prerequisite for(D). Set p_0=1 and p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i. Then Tp=1, so C1=0 implies p^THp=0. For the target(D), telescoping gives\n\n  p^THp=1-(S+1)^2p_n^2.\n\nTherefore a certificate of this exact diagonal form requires |p_n|=1/(S+1). A schedule with strict slack in the full-curvature quadratic test requires a nonzero PSD remainder or another quadratic coefficient pattern. This is a structural limit of the ansatz, not a failure of the schedule itself.\n\n## 3. Positive interval coefficients exclude the basic three-step silver block\n\nLet a=sqrt(2), lambda=1+a, and h=(a,2,a). Use the unweighted modes C^[i,j] from part1 with w=1, and write their coefficients as z_01,z_12,z_23,z_02,z_13,z_03.\n\nSuppose all six z are nonnegative and the target(D) is matched. At index0 the diagonal equation gives\n\n  z_01+2z_02+3z_03=lambda.\n\nThe column-sum equation b_0=a gives\n\n  (2-a)z_01+(6-2a)z_02+(10-2a)z_03=a.\n\nSince (2-a)lambda=a, subtracting the first equation times(2-a) yields\n\n  2z_02+(4+a)z_03=0.\n\nNonnegativity forces z_02=z_03=0 and z_01=lambda. The diagonal equation at index1 becomes\n\n  z_12+2z_13=lambda.\n\nThe cumulative column sum through cut1 is a+2. With z_02=z_03=0, only the [1,3] mode has a nonzero cut flow; that flow is a per unit coefficient. Therefore a z_13=a+2, so z_13=lambda and z_12=-lambda, a contradiction.\n\nConsequently the direct positive-mode generalization of the constant-step proof is too narrow even for this basic silver schedule. This is a hostile test of the proposed proof class, not a counterexample to silver validity.\n\nSigned interval coefficients repair the problem while every actual interpolation multiplier remains nonnegative. Choose\n\n  C=lambda C^[0,1]-lambda C^[1,2]\n      +lambda C^[1,3]+2lambda^2 C^[2,3].             (E)\n\nIts only nonzero off-diagonal entries are\n\n  C_01=1, C_10=lambda, C_13=lambda,\n  C_21=2+a, C_23=5+3a,\n  C_31=lambda, C_32=7+5a.\n\nEvery listed entry is positive. Setting each diagonal to minus its row sum gives b=(a,2,a,-2lambda). Formulas(C) give H=diag(2a-2,0,2a-2,-(2lambda)(2lambda+2)), so(D) is met. Hence(A) is an exact conic interpolation certificate for the three-step silver block. The apparent negative coefficient in(E) is only a coordinate in the interval basis; it is not a negative interpolation multiplier.\n\n## 4. Explicit elimination of the lower-triangular unknowns\n\nFor arbitrary h_i>1, any zero-row-sum matrix C with diagonal CT+T^TC^T is uniquely determined by its strict upper triangle U_ij=C_ij, i<j. Write r_i=h_i-1. For a row j>i define L_(j,i)=sum_(k<=i)C_jk, with L_(j,-1)=0. The off-diagonal equation at(i,j) is exactly\n\n  r_i L_(j,i)+L_(j,i-1)\n    =U_ij+h_j sum_(k>j)U_ik.                        (F)\n\nFor j=n the sum is empty, so h_n is again immaterial. Starting at i=0, equation(F) gives the lower entries C_ji=L_(j,i)-L_(j,i-1) without matrix inversion. Conversely this recursion and the zero row sums make every off-diagonal entry of CT+T^TC^T vanish, so there are no omitted constraints.\n\nAll interpolation coefficients are nonnegative precisely when U_ij>=0 and every computed difference L_(j,i)-L_(j,i-1)>=0. The remaining column sums and diagonal conditions(D) are linear equations in U for a fixed step vector. Thus this specific zero-remainder certificate class has an exact system of scalar linear equalities and inequalities, with no PSD feasibility test left over. This does not assert that the class captures the full primitive PEP or that the step optimization is convex.\n\nThe next analytic task, if this interface is pursued, is a variable-step family and a symbolic solution of those inequalities, including aggregate positivity after signed interval cancellation. Producing the interface alone is not asymptotic progress. Any candidate must still beat the exact benchmark or meet the sufficient amplification threshold proved conditionally in research/max4_primitive_seed_amplification.md.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"research_path": "research/max4_variable_interval_certificate.md", "source": "arXiv:2609.11784v1 Sections3.1–3.3; variable-step extension proved in evidence", "status": "unverified certificate interface and exact silver test; no improved seed", "is_fragile": false, "justification_type": "calculation", "scope": "branch", "suggested_followup": "none"}, "glossary": {"T": "Lower-triangular trajectory matrix T_ii=1,T_ij=h_j for j<i, so Tg=g+x_0-x_i; not the benchmark B_n.", "C": "Zero-row-sum interpolation coefficient matrix; actual multipliers are its off-diagonal entries, which must be nonnegative.", "H": "Symmetric matrix CT+T^TC^T.", "I_ij": "Nonnegative interpolation quantity2f_i-2f_j-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2=-2Q_ji.", "U_ij": "Strict upper-triangular entry C_ij in the exact elimination scheme.", "L_(j,i)": "Cumulative lower-triangular row sum sum_(k<=i)C_jk.", "z_ij": "Coefficient of the interval matrix on[i,j], potentially signed even when C has nonnegative off-diagonal entries."}}
{"id": "b70f11135ec8688e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:08:07.158729+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "One-envelope arc relay: assemble all finite nonsmooth local pieces before smoothing, retain every old/future plane using an exact activation buffer, and compute the true closest minimizer. Gives exact product formula(1) with first scale cancelled; on repeated(1^N,3N), W grows at least(29/21)^(2m) while every quadratic error tends to0.", "evidence": "# One-envelope arc relay with exact closest-minimizer normalization\nStatus: complete constructive candidate, UNVERIFIED. No additional verifier call.\nWorker max. The source of the local circular-arc idea is Ye and Liu, \"Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent\", arXiv:2609.09152v2, Lemma3.1 and AppendixA.1. That PDF and its proof were downloaded and read. Matlas queries did not retrieve the exact paper. The necessary local statement is re-proved below; no unverified external theorem is used as a black box. The one-envelope coupling, activation buffer, exact scale and minimizer-distance calculation below are the proposed mutation.\n\n## 1. Statement and exact computable bound\nPartition a prescribed finite nonnegative schedule as\n(block_0,b_0,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),\nwith ordinary sums s_i. Fix epsilon>0,gamma>0 and require\nb_i>=1+epsilon^(-2), 0<=i<k.\nDefine c=sqrt(1+2gamma+epsilon^2), R=sqrt(epsilon^2+(1+gamma)^2).\n\nFor each ordinary block independently, the finite backward recursion in Section2 supplies horizontal and vertical displacements P_i^arc,Q_i^arc. They are exact deterministic functions of the ordered steps in that block. Define\nA_i^arc=P_i^arc+epsilon+(1+Q_i^arc)/epsilon,\nM_i^arc=P_i^arc+max{epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i^arc)/epsilon},\nrho_i^arc=1/M_i^arc.\nPut\nD_0=1, D_(i+1)=D_i rho_i^arc(b_i-1).\nHere D_i is an activation length, not the Markov residual weight from earlier findings.\n\nThere is one finite max-affine nonnegative J on R^(k+1), and f=env_1 J, whose exact GD trajectory for the whole supplied schedule starts at e_0. Its closest minimizer x_star is explicitly identified, with\n||e_0-x_star||^2\n=(rho_0^arc)^2[(epsilon+P_0^arc)^2+(1+Q_0^arc)^2].\nIts terminal value is\n2 f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).\nConsequently, for k>=1,\nW_n(h)>=\n (b_0-1)^2 / {[(epsilon+P_0^arc)^2+(1+Q_0^arc)^2](1+2s_k)}\n times product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i^arc]^2.           (1)\nFor k=0 the construction is the ordinary one-sided Huber witness1/(1+2s_0).\n\nThere are explicit mass-only bounds. Set\nT_arc=c(1+gamma)/(epsilon gamma),\nH_arc=exp(T_arc)-1,\nA_arc=epsilon+exp(T_arc)/epsilon.\nThen P_i^arc<=c s_i, Q_i^arc<=H_arc, and\nM_i^arc<=c s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_arc}.\nThus (1) implies the uniform, still restricted-checkpoint bound\nW_n(h)>=\n (b_0-1)^2 / {[(epsilon+c s_0)^2+exp(2T_arc)](1+2s_k)}\n times product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/(c s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_arc})]^2.  (2)\nThere is no factor1/2 multiplying the component gradients: all nonsmooth pieces are assembled first and one Moreau envelope gives global smoothness. The exact cost is the activation buffer, which changes each transfer numerator to b_i-1.\n\n## 2. Local arc trajectory, proved from explicit normals\nLet V(q)=(p(q),-q), where\np(q)=sqrt(R^2-(q+gamma)^2), 0<=q<=1.\nThen V(0)=(c,0), V(1)=(epsilon,-1),\nepsilon<=p(q)<=c, and 0<=q/p(q)<=1/epsilon.\nLet K be the convex hull of0 and this compact arc. It lies in the disk centered at(0,gamma) with radius R, and in the half-plane with second coordinate<=0.\n\nFor a block with m ordinary steps alpha_1,...,alpha_m, set\nr_m=(u_m,-y_m)=(epsilon,-1), v_m=V(1).\nWork backward. Given r_j, set Delta_j=y_j+gamma-alpha_j gamma.\nIf Delta_j<=u_j gamma/c, set v_(j-1)=(c,0).\nOtherwise set\nE_j=sqrt(u_j^2+Delta_j^2),\np_(j-1)=R u_j/E_j,\nq_(j-1)=R Delta_j/E_j-gamma,\nv_(j-1)=(p_(j-1),-q_(j-1)).\nFinally set r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha_j v_(j-1).\nThis is a finite textual recursion, not a numerical optimization or feasibility assumption.\n\nWe prove inductively u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon, v_j in the arc, and v_j the Euclidean projection of r_j onto K. At j=m these statements hold. In the turning branch,\ngamma/c<Delta_j/u_j<=(1+gamma)/epsilon.\nThe increasing function t -> Rt/sqrt(1+t^2)-gamma takes values0 and1 at the two displayed endpoints. Hence0<q_(j-1)<=1 and the formula indeed gives V(q_(j-1)). In the other branch it gives V(0). Adding alpha_j times a vector with p>=epsilon,q>=0 preserves u>=epsilon,y>=1; the new ratio y/u is a convex combination of the preceding y/u and q/p, so remains<=1/epsilon.\n\nIn the turning branch, direct substitution gives\nr_(j-1)-v_(j-1)\n=[E_j/R+alpha_j-1][v_(j-1)-(0,gamma)].\nThe coefficient is nonnegative: the reverse triangle inequality gives\nE_j>=sqrt(u_j^2+(y_j+gamma)^2)-alpha_j gamma>=R-alpha_j gamma,\nso the coefficient is>=alpha_j(1-gamma/R)>=0.\nThe vector in brackets is an outward disk normal and therefore a normal to K at v_(j-1). In the capped branch put mu=u_j/c+alpha_j-1. Its branch inequality gives y_j<=gamma mu, so mu>0, and\nr_(j-1)-(c,0)=mu(c,-gamma)+(0,gamma mu-y_j).\nBoth summands are nonnegative multiples of normals to K at(c,0). Thus, in both branches,\n<r_(j-1)-v_(j-1),w-v_(j-1)><=0 for every w in K.\nExpanding squared distances proves the claimed projection property directly. These formulas additionally prove u_j>=p_j at every index.\n\nSet\nP^arc=sum_j alpha_j p_(j-1), Q^arc=sum_j alpha_j q_(j-1).\nThen r_0=(epsilon+P^arc,-1-Q^arc), and P^arc<=c sum_j alpha_j.\nOnce the capped branch occurs, it persists toward the beginning: after a capped update y stays fixed, u increases by alpha_j c, and y+gamma<=gamma u_new/c; subtracting any subsequent nonnegative alpha gamma preserves the cap criterion. Thus every positive q contribution belongs to one contiguous turning portion.\n\nHere is a self-contained, crude bound for that portion. Write sigma_j=(y_j+gamma)/u_j. At a turning update,\nsigma_(j-1)=sigma_j-alpha_j gamma/u_j,\nlog(u_(j-1)/u_j)\n=log(1+alpha_j p_(j-1)/u_j)\n<= (c/gamma)(sigma_j-sigma_(j-1)).\nSumming over the turning portion gives its total multiplicative increase of u at most exp(T_arc), because the terminal sigma is(1+gamma)/epsilon and the final sigma is nonnegative. Since each turning increase of u is at least epsilon alpha_j, the sum of its alpha_j is at most exp(T_arc)-1. The cap contributes q=0, so\nQ^arc<=exp(T_arc)-1.\nThis proves all bounds stated in Section1 without the paper's more delicate turning estimate.\n\n## 3. One local max-affine piece and its proximal margins\nFor one block suppress the index i and use its exact P^arc,Q^arc,b,M^arc,rho^arc. Put\nz_circ=(1,0)-rho^arc r_0,\nU=1-rho^arc(P^arc+epsilon b)>=0,\nY=rho^arc Q^arc.\nLet V_fin consist of0,(c,0),v_0,...,v_m and define\nphi(z)=rho^arc max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.\nAll data are fixed once the block and checkpoint are given.\nThe projection inequality from Section2 remains true after restricting K to conv(V_fin), since each projected vector v_j remains in that smaller set. Therefore v_j maximizes <v,r_j-v_j> over V_fin. At\nx_j=z_circ+rho^arc r_j,\nthe point x_j-rho^arc v_j is a proximal point of phi, and grad env_1 phi(x_j)=rho^arc v_j. This follows from the subgradient optimality equation, not from an independent interpolation assertion.\n\nThe local GD path starts at(1,0), has first coordinate nonincreasing and second coordinate nondecreasing through the ordinary block, reaches\n(U+rho^arc epsilon b,Y),\nand the checkpoint reaches\n(U,Y+rho^arc b).\nThe following exact zero regions and proximal margins are needed for global gluing:\n(a) phi(x,y)=0 whenever x<=0,y>=0;\n(b) phi(U,y)=0 whenever y>=Y;\n(c) every ordinary or checkpoint-query proximal point x_j-rho^arc v_j has first coordinate>=U>=0 and second coordinate in[0,Y+rho^arc].\n\nTo prove(a), each nonzero v=(p,-q) has p>=0,q>=0,q<=p/epsilon. At(0,0),\n<v,-z_circ>=-p+rho^arc[p(epsilon+P^arc)+q(1+Q^arc)]\n<=-p+rho^arc p A^arc<=0.\nMonotonicity in the two coordinates then proves the whole region.\nAt(U,Y), each affine argument is\nrho^arc[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0\nbecause b>=1+epsilon^(-2); increasing y preserves this, proving(b).\nFor(c), the proximal first coordinate minus U equals\nrho^arc[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0.\nIts second coordinate is rho^arc(1+Q^arc-y_j+q_j), which lies between0 and rho^arc(1+Q^arc): y_j<=1+Q^arc, q_j>=0 give the lower bound, while y_j>=1>=q_j gives the upper bound.\nAll these checks concern the nonsmooth piece phi, as needed for the one-envelope construction.\n\n## 4. Global maximum and the activation buffer\nSet ell_0=0 and recursively\nell_(i+1)=D_i rho_i^arc(1+Q_i^arc)=D_i(Y_i+rho_i^arc).\nOn coordinates0,...,k define\nPhi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k.\nLet delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), and define the terminal affine hinge\nPhi_k(z)=delta max{z_k-ell_k,0}.\nNow take the ONE nonsmooth function\nJ(z)=max{Phi_0(z),...,Phi_k(z)}\nand its ONE envelope\nf(x)=min_z{J(z)+||x-z||^2/2}.\nEach Phi_i is nonnegative finite max-affine; all its nonzero affine pieces have nonpositive value at0 by Section3(a), since ell_i>=0. Hence J>=0,J(0)=0. Its envelope is globally finite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth. To verify the last assertions directly, the unique proximal minimizer z(x) exists by the quadratic's coercivity and strict convexity. The subgradient equation x-z(x) in partial J(z(x)), the two minimizing inequalities, and subgradient monotonicity yield grad f(x)=x-z(x) and\n||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>.\nThus every stated sample, including the minimum, belongs to one globally legal function.\n\nInductively, at the beginning of block i, coordinates j<i are frozen at ell_j+D_j U_j, coordinate i equals ell_i+D_i, and all coordinates j>i are0. During the current block use the local path of Section3 in coordinates i,i+1. At its candidate GLOBAL proximal point:\n- the current piece Phi_i has the prescribed local subgradient;\n- piece i-1, if present, has its first argument frozen at U_(i-1), and its second argument at least ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1), because the current proximal first coordinate is>=ell_i; Section3(b) makes it0;\n- all earlier pieces stay in their post-checkpoint zero regions;\n- piece i+1, if present, has first coordinate at most ell_(i+1) by Section3(c), and its second coordinate0; Section3(a) makes it0;\n- every later piece is also0 by Section3(a), or by the terminal hinge's threshold.\nThus the current piece dominates all other pieces at the candidate proximal point, even when its value is0. Its subgradient is consequently a subgradient of J. This proves every actual gradient and GD update, with no old or future affine plane discarded.\n\nAfter checkpoint i, the new coordinate equals\nD_i(Y_i+rho_i^arc b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_i rho_i^arc(b_i-1)\n=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1),\nand coordinate i is frozen at ell_i+D_i U_i, proving the induction.\nThe buffer D_i rho_i^arc is exactly what prevents future activation at the previous proximal query.\n\nIn the terminal block the only moving coordinate has initial height D_k above ell_k. The constant gradient delta is legal at every query and at the terminal point, because for every cumulative partial sum0<=u<=s_k,\nD_k-(u+1)delta>=0.\nAll earlier pieces remain0 by the same post-checkpoint check. Therefore\nf(x_n)=delta D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta^2=D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].\n\n## 5. The actual nearest minimizer\nThe minimizer set of f equals {J=0}: both terms in its defining minimum are nonnegative, and the minimum is attained.\nDefine x_star by its first two coordinates\n(x_star)_0=1-rho_0^arc(epsilon+P_0^arc),\n(x_star)_1=rho_0^arc(1+Q_0^arc)=ell_1,\nand all later coordinates0.\nThis is the first component's translated center z_circ. Hence Phi_0(x_star)=0. The next component has first input0 and second input0, so is0 by Section3(a); all later components are0 as well. For k=1 the terminal coordinate is exactly its threshold ell_1. Thus x_star is a GLOBAL minimizer, regardless of later block choices.\n\nIt is the closest one. Every affine piece of Phi_0 is active with value0 there. Moreover\ne_0-x_star=rho_0^arc(epsilon+P_0^arc,-1-Q_0^arc,0,...,0)\nis the following nonnegative combination of two active normal vectors:\n[(P_0^arc-epsilon Q_0^arc)/c] rho_0^arc(c,0,0,...)\n +(1+Q_0^arc) rho_0^arc(epsilon,-1,0,...).\nThe first coefficient is nonnegative since every p_j>=epsilon q_j. For any minimizer z, its inner product with z-x_star is therefore nonpositive. Squared-distance expansion proves\n||e_0-z||^2>=||e_0-x_star||^2.\nThis establishes the EXACT initial distance in Section1. Dividing the terminal value by it cancels the first local scale, and gives(1). It does not assume the origin is closest.\n\n## 6. An explicit unbounded-horizon test with all quadratic errors vanishing\nTake epsilon=gamma=1/10. Then c=11/10,T_arc=121,\nA_arc=1/10+10 exp(121).\nChoose any fixed integer N>=max{10,A_arc}. For each m>=1 consider m repetitions of a block of N unit steps followed by checkpoint3N, with no terminal ordinary steps.\nThe checkpoint condition3N>=101 holds. For every block,\nP_i^arc<=11N/10, A_i^arc<=11N/10+A_arc<=21N/10,\nM_i^arc<=11N/10+max{3N/10,A_arc}<=21N/10.\nThe initial distance factor also obeys\nsqrt((epsilon+P_0^arc)^2+(1+Q_0^arc)^2)\n<=A_0^arc<=21N/10:\nthe first inequality follows from 1+Q>=0 and epsilon<=1, since the sum P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon dominates the Euclidean norm.\nThus every normalized transfer factor is at least\n(3N-1)/(21N/10)>=29/21.\nThe explicit high-dimensional nonlinear construction gives\nW_(m(N+1))(h)>=(29/21)^(2m).                            (3)\n\nFor the same schedules, a scalar quadratic eigenvalue lambda in[0,1] has block multiplier\nq_N(lambda)=(1-lambda)^N(1-3N lambda).\nIf0<=lambda<=1/(3N), its magnitude is at most exp(-N lambda).\nIf lambda>=1/(3N), it is at most\n(3N lambda-1)exp(-N lambda)<=3exp(-4/3)<4/5.\nThe last strict inequality follows already by summing the degree5 Taylor lower bound for exp(4/3), which exceeds15/4. Therefore every finite-dimensional convex quadratic has normalized terminal objective at most\nmax{1/(2 e m N),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0.\nThe dimension of the nonlinear witness is m+1 and its sampled checkpoint gradients contain m independent directions proportional to epsilon e_i-e_(i+1), followed by the terminal e_m direction.\n\nThe function is nonseparable in every fixed orthonormal coordinate system. Each coupled endpoint normal epsilon e_i-e_(i+1) can be exposed alone against the zero plane: choose a recession direction with all coordinates negative, with t_(j+1)<epsilon t_j for j!=i and t_(i+1)=epsilon t_i for j=i, where the coordinates are -t_j. Every other local arc normal then has negative inner product with that direction, while the selected endpoint has zero inner product. Starting on the selected affine hyperplane and moving sufficiently far along this direction makes every other nonzero plane strictly negative. The terminal normal e_m is exposed similarly with final coordinate0 and sufficiently negative preceding coordinates. Near each resulting hinge, the envelope has the rank-one Hessian projector onto that normal. Adjacent coupled normals, or the last coupled normal and e_m, are nonorthogonal and nonparallel, so the corresponding projectors do not commute. A function separable in one orthonormal basis would have commuting Hessians. This proves the assertion.\n\n## 7. What remains open\nThis mutation permits arbitrary nonnegative ordinary steps and varying within-block gradient directions, retains all planes, gives exact nonzero offsets, supplies a finite real PSD Gram realization, and computes the true closest-minimizer distance. It has a concrete exponential-error family whose quadratic channel converges.\n\nIt does NOT prove the exact profile. The constants c>1,A_arc and the checkpoint threshold remain; the norm/cost bounds do not yet imply an arbitrary-schedule comparison to(1-o(1))U_n^ZJ. Taking epsilon,gamma small makes c approach1 but makes A_arc very large. No claim is made that optimizing the partition and parameters eliminates that loss. Formula(1), not a postulated covariance-feasibility assertion, is the new exact candidate interface.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_one_envelope_arc_relay.md", "source": "Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, arXiv:2609.09152v2, Lemma3.1/AppendixA.1 re-proved; mutation does not rely on that result as an unchecked black box", "local_source": "workers/max/downloads/2609.09152.txt", "mechanism": "maximum before one Moreau envelope, with exact closest-minimizer distance", "status": "complete candidate, unverified", "verification_allocation": "unchanged", "target_gap": "uniform exact-profile comparison still open"}, "glossary": {"P_i^arc": "Exact horizontal displacement of the backward circular-arc construction for ordinary block i.", "Q_i^arc": "Exact nonnegative vertical displacement of that construction.", "A_i^arc": "Local zero-region budget P_i^arc+epsilon+(1+Q_i^arc)/epsilon.", "M_i^arc": "Exact reciprocal local scale P_i^arc+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i^arc)/epsilon).", "rho_i^arc": "The positive scale1/M_i^arc.", "D_i": "Activation length in this finding, defined by D_0=1,D_(i+1)=D_i rho_i^arc(b_i-1).", "ell_i": "Absolute activation threshold for coordinate i in the global max-affine model.", "T_arc": "Explicit dimensionless turning bound c(1+gamma)/(epsilon gamma).", "A_arc": "Uniform additive scale bound epsilon+exp(T_arc)/epsilon.", "Phi_i": "The globally embedded nonsmooth local max-affine piece on coordinates i,i+1; Phi_k is the terminal hinge."}}
{"id": "8cce883b9f93f556", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:16:47.007330+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Positive checkpoint values repair a genuine signed-history failure: a parameterized alternating-energy Markov relay keeps every plane legal even when old residuals are positive. Rational repeated(1,463/135,1,131/30) has explicit W>=85/121, while all quadratic errors tend to0.", "evidence": "# Positive-value periodic Markov relay with signed history\nStatus: complete explicit construction, UNVERIFIED. No new verifier call.\nThis tests the specific repair suggested after the minimum-reset and residual-sign obstructions: allow positive proximal function values, and check actual past-plane deficits even when the signed residual is positive.\n\n## 1. A parameterized model\nFix a in(0,1), chi>=1, T>=2, and kappa in[0,T-2]. Assume\nT>=2a chi,                                               (1)\n(T-2-kappa)-(T-kappa)chi^2+T chi/a>=0.                     (2)\nFor any number m>=1 of blocks, in R^(m+1) set\nzeta_0=e_0,\nzeta_(i+1)=-a zeta_i+sqrt(1-a^2)e_(i+1).\nDefine alpha_i=chi for even i and alpha_i=1 for odd i, omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i. Thus\n<zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-a)^|i-j|\nand all omega_i are independent.\n\nEach real block consists of one unit step followed by\nb_i=T-1+(T/a)alpha_(i+1)/alpha_i.\nTake the ONE finite max-affine function\nJ(z)=max{0,max_(0<=i<=m)(<omega_i,z>-kappa alpha_i^2)}\nand f=env_1 J. Start at X_0=T alpha_0 zeta_0.\nWe prove that block i queries X_i and X_i-omega_i with gradient omega_i at both, and that\nX_(i+1)=X_i-(1+b_i)omega_i.\nThe terminal point is X_m with gradient omega_m.\n\nThe function f is legal globally: J is finite nonnegative convex with J(0)=0, and its envelope is finite convex differentiable1-smooth. For precision, the proximal minimization has a unique minimizer by the quadratic term; its subgradient equation implies grad f(x)=x-prox_J(x), and monotonicity gives\n||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>.\nAt any point where plane i is active at x-omega_i, that subgradient equation yields the claimed gradient and value\nf(x)=<omega_i,x>-alpha_i^2/2-kappa alpha_i^2.\nThus it remains to compare every affine plane at the indicated proximal points.\n\n## 2. Exact signed residuals\nLet Z_i=X_i-T omega_i and put\nB_i=(T/a)alpha_(i+1), D_i=B_i(1-a^2).\nThe update gives\nZ_0=0,\nZ_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i(zeta_i+a zeta_(i+1)).\nDirect multiplication of Markov correlations proves\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=-S_(j,i) for j<i,\nwhere\nS_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l(-a)^(l-j).                     (3)\nIndeed zeta_l+a zeta_(l+1) is orthogonal to all zeta_j with j>=l+1, and its inner product with earlier zeta_j is(1-a^2)(-a)^(l-j). This proves(3) by summing the update; no sign of S is assumed.\n\nAt a real query x=X_i-u omega_i with u=0 or1, its proposed proximal point is\nZ_i+d omega_i, d=T-u-1>=T-2.\nThe terminal query has d=T-1 and obeys the same lower bound.\nThe current affine plane has value(d-kappa)alpha_i^2>=0.\nThe current-minus-j-th plane difference is\nd alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)\n +alpha_j S_(j,i)-kappa alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2          (4)\nfor j<i, with C_ij=(-a)^(i-j); for j>i the same formula has S=0.\nEvery coefficient of d is positive: at even separation the amplitudes agree and C<1, while at odd separation C<0. Hence it suffices to check d=T-2.\n\nFuture planes are already controlled. At even separation their offsets agree and their deficit is d alpha_i^2(1-C)>=0. At odd separation the deficit is\n(d-kappa)alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2+d a^|i-j| alpha_i alpha_j>=0.\nThe substantive point is the full past history.\n\n## 3. All past planes, including positive residuals\nFor even separation i-j=2q, q>=1, let z=alpha_j=alpha_i and y be the opposite amplitude. Formula(3) is the exact geometric sum\nS_(j,i)=(T/a)(y-a z)(1-a^(2q)).\nSubstituting into(4) gives, at d=T-2,\n(1-a^(2q)) z[-2z+(T/a)y]>=0\nby(1), since z/y is either chi or1/chi.\n\nFor odd separation i-j=2q+1, q>=0, let z=alpha_j and y=alpha_i be the opposite amplitude. Now\nS_(j,i)=(T/a)[y-a z+a^(2q)(a z-a^2 y)].\nSubstitution into(4), still at d=T-2, gives\nF_infty(y,z)+a^(2q)[T z^2-2a yz],                       (5)\nwhere\nF_infty(y,z)=(T-2-kappa)y^2-(T-kappa)z^2+(T/a)yz.\nThe second term in(5) is nonnegative by(1).\nAmong the two choices(y,z)=(1,chi),(chi,1), the first has smaller F_infty: the difference is\n2(T-1-kappa)(chi^2-1)>=0.\nIts nonnegativity is precisely(2). This proves every old-plane comparison, at every lag, with the signed residual retained. Together with the future and zero-plane checks it proves the entire trajectory in Section1.\n\nThe exact sampled values are\nf(X_i-u omega_i)=(T-u-1/2-kappa)alpha_i^2.\nAt a checkpoint query u=1, the nonsmooth proximal value is\nJ(X_i-2omega_i)=(T-2-kappa)alpha_i^2.                    (6)\nIt is strictly positive whenever kappa<T-2. Thus these checkpoints are not minimum resets.\n\n## 4. Closest minimizer and payoff\nThe minimizer set of f is exactly\nP_min={z:<omega_i,z><=kappa alpha_i^2 for every i}.\nThe point y_proj=kappa alpha_0 zeta_0 lies in P_min: for even i the amplitude equals alpha_0 and the correlation is at most1; for odd i the correlation is negative. Constraint0 is active, and\nX_0-y_proj=(T-kappa)omega_0\nis a positive multiple of its normal. For any z in P_min this gives\n<X_0-y_proj,z-y_proj><=0,\nso squared-distance expansion proves that y_proj is the unique closest minimizer and\ndist(X_0,P_min)^2=(T-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2.\nThe normalized terminal value of this one instance is therefore exactly\n2(T-kappa-1/2)alpha_m^2 / [(T-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2].        (7)\nFor even m, alpha_m=alpha_0 and this is\n[2(T-kappa)-1]/(T-kappa)^2.\nAll smooth-convex interpolation inequalities, including the optimum sample, hold because all samples have been checked on this one explicit envelope.\n\n## 5. Rational parameters, quadratic stability, and a strict failure of the old test\nChoose\na=9/10, chi=6/5, T=23/10, kappa=1/10.\nCondition(1) has positive margin\nT-2a chi=7/50.\nThe left side of(2) is exactly37/375>0.\nThe alternating checkpoint sizes are\nb_even=463/135, b_odd=131/30,\nboth in[3,5]. Therefore for m=2r and every integer r>=1 the length4r schedule\n(1,463/135,1,131/30) repeated r times\nhas the explicitly realized normalized error\nW_(4r)(h)>=85/121.                                     (8)\nAt every checkpoint its proximal value is alpha_i^2/5>0.\n\nThis model violates the earlier nonpositive-residual criterion. For any even j with j+2<=m,\nS_(j,j+2)=(T/a)(1-a^2)(1-a chi)<0\nbecause a chi=27/25>1. Thus\n<Z_(j+2),zeta_j> >0,\nand D_j>=a D_(j+1) is false at every such pair. Nevertheless the exact plane comparisons above hold. This is an explicit repair, not an assumption that local correlation checks suffice.\n\nFor comparison, each quadratic block multiplier is(1-lambda)(1-b_i lambda).\nFor0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and at most exp(-lambda).\nFor1/5<=lambda<=1, its positive value is at most8/25, since b_i>=3; the magnitude of its negative minimum is at most4/5, since b_i<=5 and max_(b<=5,b>=3)(b-1)^2/(4b)=4/5.\nHence the full finite-dimensional quadratic normalized objective after m blocks is at most\nmax{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0.\nThe nonlinear persistent value is not a large quadratic product.\n\nThe omega_i are independent, adjacent ones have negative inner product, and their nonnegative-offset max-affine system has isolated zero/plane hinges. At such hinges the envelope has rank-one Hessian projectors onto omega_i. Adjacent projectors do not commute, proving that this function is nonseparable in every fixed orthonormal basis. Independence permits exposing each hinge by solving for one affine value0 and all others strictly negative. The dimension m+1 grows with the horizon and the gradient energies alternate between36/25 and1.\n\n## 6. Scope\nThis is a parameterized, fully signed-history construction with positive checkpoint values and a concrete rational periodic test outside the earlier residual-sign interface. It does not claim that(1)--(2), or a generalization of them, can realize an arbitrary schedule at the benchmark profile. The endpoint values of the exact all-past comparison now show where positive residuals can be paid for by real face margins and offsets. A future extension must solve those inequalities quantitatively for nonperiodic data, rather than imposing S>=0 again or assuming feasibility at target strength.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_positive_value_periodic_relay.md", "repairs": ["5525a7637862ffe3", "4ff405e904d7da62"], "mechanism": "positive proximal values with complete signed Markov history", "status": "complete candidate, unverified", "verification_allocation": "unchanged", "target_gap": "all-schedule profile comparison still open"}, "glossary": {"a": "Positive magnitude of the common negative Markov correlation in this finding.", "chi": "Ratio of alternating gradient amplitudes, at least1.", "T": "Common virtual budget in the periodic relay, at least2.", "kappa": "Proportional affine offset coefficient; eta_i=kappa alpha_i^2.", "P_min": "The exact minimizer polyhedron of this relay.", "F_infty": "The explicit odd-lag limiting affine-plane deficit from Section3, not an objective function."}}
{"id": "8c998d29efd3da99", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:16:53.829661+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Actual fixed-length joint descent for triangular full certificates: transporting one star-outgoing multiplier column and rescaling the suffix preserves every mixed PSD entry and adds a diagonal gradient energy. The required cut-profile condition is automatic for two remaining steps.", "evidence": "# Joint suffix deformation with exact PSD preservation\n\nStatus: complete conditional derivation, UNVERIFIED. It is a new J1 candidate,\nnot a claim that all global minimizers have the hypotheses. No further\nverification allocation has been made.\n\n## Full certificate and the additional triangular hypothesis\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be\nnonnegative and finite. For distinct indices in I={star,0,...,n}, set\n\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nAssume a complete upper certificate (q,lambda,h): q>0, lambda_ij>=0,\nout_i-in_i=2 1_(i=n) at finite indices, and the quadratic form\n\nM=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n\nafter x_j=x_0-sum_(t<j)h_t g_t is positive semidefinite. It is a\nquadratic form in the independent formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n);\nflow cancels every function-value coefficient. Thus 2f_n<=q||x_0||^2\non every legal trajectory.\n\nFor this candidate ONLY, additionally assume\n\nlambda_ij=0 whenever 0<=i<j<=n.                         (T)\n\nAll finite-index multiplier flow goes backward. Star edges may go either\nway. Write mu_j=lambda_(j,star), nu_j=lambda_(star,j), and, for t<j,\n\nc_(t,j)=mu_j+sum_(l=0)^t lambda_(j,l).\n\nEvery c is nonnegative. The complete matrix entries are\n\nM_(x_0,x_0)=q,   M_(x_0,g_j)=-mu_j/2,\nM_(g_j,g_j)=out_j-1_(j=n),\n2 M_(g_t,g_j)=h_t c_(t,j)-lambda_(j,t),   t<j.          (1)\n\nTo check (1), the squared-gradient terms contribute\n(out_j+in_j)/2=out_j-1_(j=n) on the diagonal. A backward\nedge j->t contributes -lambda_(j,t)<g_j,g_t> from the squared\ndifference, and h_t times the total flow from j to star or an index at\nmost t contributes h_t c_(t,j)<g_j,g_t>. The star pair contributes\n-mu_j<x_0,g_j>. This accounts for every term; no principal submatrix\nreplaces M.\n\n## Deformation\n\nFix k<n with nu_k>0. Define C=sum_(j>k)c_(k,j). By summing flow over\n{k+1,...,n}, hypothesis (T) gives C=2+sum_(j>k)nu_j>0.\nPut p_j=c_(k,j)/C for j>k, so p_j>=0 and sum p_j=1.\n\nAssume the following explicit proportionality condition on later cuts:\n\nfor every k<t<n there is r_t>=0 such that\np_j=r_t c_(t,j) for all j>t.                           (P)\n\nZero p entries are allowed. If all p_j vanish beyond t, one may take\nr_t=0. Set r_k=1/C.\n\nChoose any 0<eta<=nu_k. Change only these multipliers:\n\nnu'_k=nu_k-eta,\nnu'_j=nu_j+eta p_j             (j>k),\nlambda'_(j,k)=lambda_(j,k)+eta p_j   (j>k).             (2)\n\nKeep every mu_j and every other finite edge unchanged. Change the schedule by\n\nh'_t=h_t                                      (t<k),\nh'_k=(h_k+eta r_k)/(1+eta r_k),\nh'_t=h_t/(1+eta r_t)                           (k<t<n). (3)\n\nAll new steps and multipliers are nonnegative. At k the increased\nincoming backward flow eta cancels the removed incoming star flow eta;\nat j>k the new outgoing edge eta p_j cancels the new incoming star\nedge eta p_j. Thus every flow equation stays exact.\n\nThe NEW full matrix, on the same formal vector coordinates, satisfies\n\nM(q,lambda',h') =\nM(q,lambda,h)+eta sum_(j>k)p_j ||g_j||^2.               (4)\n\nProof: diagonal j>k increases by eta p_j, while all x_0 entries and\nall other diagonals stay fixed. For t<k, c_(t,j), h_t and lambda_(j,t)\nare unchanged. At t=k,\n\nh'_k(c_(k,j)+eta p_j)-(lambda_(j,k)+eta p_j)\n=h_k c_(k,j)-lambda_(j,k),\n\nusing p_j=r_k c_(k,j). At t>k the edge lambda_(j,t) is unchanged and\n\nh'_t(c_(t,j)+eta p_j)=h_t c_(t,j),\n\nusing (P). Therefore EVERY mixed entry is unchanged, proving (4).\nIn particular the whole new matrix remains PSD, including every old\nkernel direction. No forward edge is created and no preexisting edge\nis decreased except the explicitly available star multiplier nu_k.\n\n## Strict worst-case improvement\n\nIdentity (4) gives, on every trajectory of the NEW schedule,\n\nq R^2-2f_n >= eta sum_(j>k)p_j ||g_j||^2,               (5)\n\nwhere R=||x_0||>0. Here the old M on the right of (4) is PSD on arbitrary\nformal vectors; the new transcript need not be a trajectory of the old\nschedule.\n\nSet B=product_(t=0)^(n-1)(1+h'_t) and\nD_j=product_(t=j)^(n-1)(1+h'_t), with D_n=1.\nThese finite positive quantities are deliberately crude.\n\nSince g_star=0 and the gradient is 1-Lipschitz, ||g_t||<=||x_t||, hence\n||x_n||<=B R. Convexity gives f_n<=<g_n,x_n><=B R||g_n||.\nThe same Lipschitz property gives\n||g_(t+1)||<=||g_t||+||x_(t+1)-x_t||=(1+h'_t)||g_t||,\nso ||g_n||<=D_j||g_j||.\n\nWriting E=2f_n/R^2, these inequalities and (5) yield\n\nq-E >= gamma E^2,\ngamma=eta/(4B^2) sum_(j>k) p_j/D_j^2 >0.              (6)\n\nConsequently the actual fixed-length new schedule obeys\n\nW_n(h') <= 2q/[1+sqrt(1+4 gamma q)] < q.               (7)\n\nThis is a uniform bound over every dimension and every legal function,\nnot a calculation on the old active instance. Neither stationarity nor\nq=1/(2sum h+1) was used. In particular, if the old certificate is tight\nat a globally minimizing schedule, hypotheses (T),(P),nu_k>0 cannot\nhold simultaneously.\n\n## Two remaining steps: condition (P) is automatic\n\nTake k=n-2. Define\n\nc_1=mu_(n-1)+sum_(l<=n-2)lambda_(n-1,l),\nc_2=mu_n+sum_(l<=n-2)lambda_(n,l),\nc_3=mu_n+sum_(l<=n-1)lambda_(n,l)=2+nu_n>0.\n\nThen C=c_1+c_2, p_(n-1)=c_1/C, p_n=c_2/C, and\nr_(n-1)=p_n/c_3 satisfies (P), including c_2=0. Formula (3) changes\nBOTH of the final two steps:\n\nh'_(n-2)=(C h_(n-2)+eta)/(C+eta),\nh'_(n-1)=h_(n-1)c_3/(c_3+eta p_n).\n\nThe complete slack gains exactly\neta p_(n-1)||g_(n-1)||^2+eta p_n||g_n||^2.\nThus for a triangular tight certificate at a global minimizer,\nnu_(n-2) must vanish. At horizon n=2 this excludes nu_0>0 under (T).\nThis is a genuine earlier-step joint deformation, not the already\nrecorded final-step convex blend.\n\n## Precise limitation\n\nFor k<t<n, if positive ratios p_j/c_(t,j) differ as j varies, no single\nh'_t can satisfy h'_t(c_(t,j)+eta p_j)=h_t c_(t,j) for all j>t when\nh_t>0. Thus this one-column transport cannot simply be declared to\nwork on arbitrary longer suffixes. Additional multiplier changes,\na non-diagonal PSD residual, or a different mechanism would be needed.\n\nNor is (T) established for arbitrary optimal certificates. Forward\nedges produce additional mixed terms when suffix steps change.\nThe candidate does NOT establish global Huber tightness or the\nZhang--Jiang exact-profile lower bound.\n\n## Hostile checks within the derivation\n\n- No division by h_t, h_t-1, p_j, or nu_j for j>k is used.\n  Zero steps, zero weights, and steps on either side of1 are covered.\n- If h_k=1, that coordinate stays fixed; positive later r_t can still\n  change the suffix. If every affected coordinate were unchanged,\n  (5)--(7) would show that q was not a tight certificate.\n- Even p_n=0 leaves gamma>0, since some earlier p_j is positive and\n  D_j is finite.\n- The old matching primal is never reused as if it satisfied new GD\n  recursions. Uniform legality enters only in (5)--(7).\n\nRetrieval: fact_search found no matching result. Targeted Matlas search\nreturned unrelated statements; no external theorem is used in this proof.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_joint_suffix_deformation.md", "plan_id": "max2_J1_J2", "subgoal": "J1 actual full-feasible joint schedule/certificate deformation", "status": "complete conditional candidate; unverified", "finding_ids": ["3957f090cc8d310d", "27cbc4f5a2e9be3c", "1350be2cea28858c"], "scope": "Triangular finite-index multipliers plus explicit proportionality of later cut profiles; no claim these cover arbitrary global minimizers.", "verification_allocation": "unchanged"}, "glossary": {"c": "c_(t,j)=mu_j+sum_(l<=t)lambda_(j,l), the backward cut coefficient.", "p": "Nonnegative transport distribution p_j=c_(k,j)/sum_(l>k)c_(k,l).", "r": "r_t is the nonnegative proportionality factor p_j=r_t c_(t,j) for every j>t.", "eta": "The positive transported amount, at most nu_k.", "B": "The product of 1+h'_t over the complete changed schedule.", "D": "D_j is the product of 1+h'_t from j through the last step.", "gamma": "The explicit positive coefficient in inequality q-E>=gamma E^2."}}
{"id": "4be2737d7e8764a6", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:16:54.894053+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Whole-tail residual transport now has an exact nonlocal certificate and a quantitative failed calibration. Summing all tail-to-bridge interpolation inequalities yields a 3x3 objective-residual update; a rational/radical three-step test passes. But the closed constant-nu balanced-doubling subrule certifies denominator at most L0/k times silver capacity, with L0<sqrt(2), while the exact benchmark is asymptotically at least twice that capacity. The forced reset of the terminal residual coefficient is the precise loss.", "evidence": "# Whole-tail residual transport: exact rule and balanced-doubling failure\n\nStatus: self-contained unverified certificate algebra, a finite valid test, and a quantitative limitation of one explicitly specified recursion. The conclusion concerns the bound produced by this certificate rule, not the true worst case of the resulting schedules. No new numerical evaluation or verifier call.\n\nWork at L=1. For any block h with nonnegative proof weights u on its iterates define D=sum u_i, d=x_start-x_end, U=sum u_i g_i, e=U-d, and\n\n  E_h(u)=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_end)+sum_i u_i||g_i||^2\n         +2sum_i u_i<g_i,x_start-x_i>-||U||^2.\n\nFor every minimizer x_star, expansion gives\n\n  ||x_start-x_star||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D(f_end-f_star)\n     =||x_end-x_star-e||^2+E_h(u),\n\nwhere Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. Therefore E_h(u)>=0 implies W(h)<=1/D.\n\n## 1. An aggregate interpolation identity across the full tail\n\nLet a prefix finish at y, with g=grad f(y), function value f_y, and certificate state(D,e,E_a). Take a bridge step tau>=0 to y'=y-tau g. Run a tail block from y' to z. Write its nonnegative proof weights as v, its mass as D_b, its gradient aggregate as U_b, and its intrinsic energy as E_b. Summing all Q_(i,y) over the tail, with weights v_i, gives exactly\n\n  2D_b(f_y-f_z)\n   =E_b+||U_b||^2+2(tau-1)<U_b,g>+D_b||g||^2\n       +2sum_i v_i(-Q_(i,y)).                        (1)\n\nTo check this, substitute y-x_i=tau g+(y'-x_i) into the Q expression. The tail terms 2sum v_i(f_i-f_z)+sum v_i||g_i||^2+2sum v_i<g_i,y'-x_i> combine to E_b+||U_b||^2; the remaining cross terms are2(tau-1)<U_b,g> and D_b||g||^2. All interpolation terms on the right of(1) are nonnegative.\n\nUnlike the adjacent-only update, this identity uses every tail iterate and retains the actual aggregate U_b.\n\n## 2. Exact matrix transport with a primitive tail\n\nAssume the prefix has the residual certificate\n\n  E_a>=nu||e-K g||^2, nu>0.                         (2)\n\nLet b be primitive with shifted physical capacity A=1+sum(b). Use its standard proof weights v=(b_0,...,b_(ell-1),A). Then\n\n  D_b=2A-1,\n  U_b=(y'-z)+Aq, q=grad f(z),\n  E_b=V_b>=0,\n\nwhere V_b=2sum b_i(f_i-f_z)+sum b_i(1-b_i)||g_i||^2-(A-1)A||q||^2 is precisely its primitive potential.\n\nIncrease the old terminal proof weight by eta and scale all tail weights by beta. Require old_terminal_weight+eta>=0 and beta>=0. The new weights have mass\n\n  D'=D+eta+beta D_b,\n\nand their residual satisfies\n\n  e'-Aq=e+(eta-tau)g+(beta-1)U_b.                   (3)\n\nDirect expansion of E for the joined block gives\n\n  E'=E_a+beta E_b+2(D+eta)(f_y-f_z)\n      +eta(1-eta)||g||^2-2eta<e,g>\n      +beta(1-beta)||U_b||^2\n      +2beta<U_b,(tau-eta)g-e>.                     (4)\n\nChoose c=(D+eta)/D_b>=0 and nu'>=0. Substituting(1) into(4), and subtracting nu' times the squared residual in(3), gives the exact identity\n\n  E'-nu'||e'-Aq||^2\n  =E_a-nu||e-Kg||^2+(beta+c)E_b\n     +2c sum_i v_i(-Q_(i,y))+(e,g,U_b)^T M(e,g,U_b),\n\nwhere the symmetric3-by3 matrix is\n\n  M_ee=nu-nu',\n  M_eg=-nu K-eta-nu'(eta-tau),\n  M_eU=-beta-nu'(beta-1),\n  M_gg=nu K^2+D+2eta-eta^2-nu'(eta-tau)^2,\n  M_gU=c(tau-1)+beta(tau-eta)-nu'(eta-tau)(beta-1),\n  M_UU=c+beta(1-beta)-nu'(beta-1)^2.\n\nThus M>=0 suffices for another residual certificate, with new K'=A and mass D'. In particular it gives an actual objective bound1/D'. This algebra does not assume that D=2sum(a)+1.\n\n## 3. A closed scalar subrule and an exact finite test\n\nKeep nu'=nu fixed and set\n\n  k=1+1/nu, beta=1/k, eta=(tau-K)/k.\n\nThe e row and column of M then vanish. The remaining matrix is\n\n  [ D+((K-1)^2-(tau-1)^2)/k    c(tau-1) ]\n  [ c(tau-1)                  c         ].\n\nPut X=kD, r=tau-1, and X'=kD'=X+r+1-K+D_b. Subject to c>=0, this matrix is positive semidefinite exactly when\n\n  r^2 X'<=D_b[X+(K-1)^2].                           (5)\n\nFor c>0 this is its Schur-complement condition after multiplication by kD_b. When c=0, X'=D_b and(5) is exactly the remaining diagonal condition. The state update is K'=A.\n\nFor a genuine finite example, use the prefix a=(5/3), weights u=(3/2,3/4), D=9/4, K=1, nu=27. Its identity is\n\n  E_a=3(-Q_10)+27||e-g_1||^2.\n\nChoose the primitive tail b=(sqrt(2)), whose capacity is A=1+sqrt(2), and use tau=1. The primitive tail certificate is explicitly\n\n  V_b=2(1+sqrt(2))(-Q_10)+2(-Q_01),\n\nwith local tail indices. Then eta=0, beta=27/28 and the remaining transport matrix is diagonal with entries9/4 and(9/4)/(1+2sqrt(2)), both positive. Consequently the actual three-step schedule\n\n  h=(5/3,1,sqrt(2))\n\nhas nonnegative proof weights (3/2,3/4,27sqrt(2)/28,27(1+sqrt(2))/28), and the certified bound\n\n  W_3(h)<=14/(45+27sqrt(2)).\n\nThis is weaker than the exact ZJ benchmark. It tests the block rule but provides no improvement.\n\n## 4. Balanced primitive-tail doubling cannot be competitive under this subrule\n\nLet lambda=1+sqrt(2), and start with any finite valid state of length1. At round j append a bridge and the standard silver primitive tail of the same length n_j=2^(j+1)-1, so n_(j+1)=2n_j+1. Its shifted capacity is A_j=lambda^(j+1), obtained by repeated ConPP using F(x,x)=lambda x. Use the fixed-nu subrule of part3 with any parameters satisfying its actual weight and PSD conditions.\n\nFor j>=1 the reset K_(j+1)=A_j gives K_j=lambda^j=A_j/lambda. Write X_j=kD_j. The recurrence and(5) become\n\n  X_(j+1)=X_j+2A_j+r_j-K_j,\n  r_j^2 X_(j+1)<=(2A_j-1)[X_j+(K_j-1)^2],\n  X_(j+1)>=2A_j-1.                                 (6)\n\nThe last inequality follows from c>=0. Hence r_j<=sqrt(X_j+(K_j-1)^2), and since K_j>=1,\n\n  X_(j+1)<=X_j+2A_j+sqrt(X_j).\n\nChoose M sufficiently large that X_1<=M A_1 and(lambda-1)M>=2+sqrt(M). Induction gives X_j<=M A_j, so x_j=X_j/A_j is bounded. Let L=limsup_j x_j. If L=0 there is nothing to prove. Otherwise take a subsequence with x_(j+1)->L. Put t_j=r_j/A_j. From(6),\n\n  lambda x_(j+1)=x_j+2-1/lambda+t_j,\n  t_j^2 lambda x_(j+1)\n    <=(2-1/A_j)[x_j/A_j+(1/lambda-1/A_j)^2].         (7)\n\nAlong a further subsequence x_j converges to a number at most L; thus t_j has a limit at least(lambda-1)L-2+1/lambda. Passing to the limit in(7) yields\n\n  L*[(lambda-1)L-2+1/lambda]_+^2<=2/lambda^3.        (8)\n\nLet L_0 be the unique solution, above(2-1/lambda)/(lambda-1), of the corresponding equality. The left side is strictly increasing on that interval, so L<=L_0. At L=sqrt(2), its value times lambda^3 equals lambda sqrt(2)=2+sqrt(2)>2; hence\n\n  limsup_j D_j/lambda^(j+1)<=L_0/k<sqrt(2)/k.        (9)\n\nThe exact benchmark along these same dyadic horizons has liminf B_(n_j)/lambda^(j+1)>=2. A short scalar proof avoids importing any phase theorem. A complete balanced primitive tree with2^K unit leaves has capacity lambda^K. Replace one leaf by zero and collapse its parent using F(x,0)=x; the resulting legal tree has2^K-1 leaves. The map F is2-Lipschitz in each coordinate (differentiate its explicit formula, whose partial derivatives lie in[1,2]), so this single leaf replacement decreases the root by at most2^K. Thus P_(2^K-2)>=lambda^K-2^K. The admissible dominant split with that prefix and an empty tail gives\n\n  B_(2^K-1)>=G(P_(2^K-2),1)>=2lambda^K-2^(K+1).\n\nSince2/lambda<1, the claimed normalized lower limit follows. Combining with(9), the value1/D_j certified by this transport satisfies\n\n  liminf_j (1/D_j)/U_(n_j)>=2k/L_0>sqrt(2)k>1.\n\nTherefore this specific whole-tail rule, under the displayed constant-nu and equal-horizon recursion, cannot certify an improvement even over the basic primitive rate. This does not lower-bound W(h_j), does not exclude other parameter recursions, and does not show that all residual certificates fail.\n\n## 5. What the next block interface must retain\n\nThe aggregate-only expression depends on(e,g,U_b) but not independently on q. Therefore a positive new residual coefficient forces K'=A unless another q-dependent certificate is retained: subtracting nu'||e'-K'q||^2 would otherwise give a negative q^2 coefficient in a variable absent from the original matrix. This explains the forced reset in the tested rule.\n\nA source-supported extra piece is the primitive dual residual\n\n  2(f_start-f_end)-2(A-1)||q||^2\n     >=||g_start-(d_b+q)/A||^2.                     (10)\n\nThe proof in Grimmer–Shu–Wang arXiv:2410.16249, AppendixA.1 Lemma17, was read in full; before adding the optimum interpolation inequality it gives exactly(10), using the primitive potential plus nonnegative interpolation sums. That derivation uses the primitive potential itself and A=1+sum(b), not an assumed equality of primitive and dominant ambient classes. A future proof may rederive this combination explicitly rather than appeal to the final gradient-norm statement. No verifier promotion is claimed here. The next test is to retain both this dual residual and the objective residual through a join, with the bridge gradient and tail initial gradient distinct. A coarse sum of the bridge descent estimate and(10) has not recovered the basic large silver bridge, so it is not yet a replacement recursion.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_nonlocal_tail_residual_composition", "attempt_type": "direct", "research_path": "research/max4_nonlocal_tail_transport.md", "status": "finite certificate valid algebraically; constant-nu equal-horizon recursion screened out as an upper-bound method", "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "refuted", "summary": "The concrete update fails the required benchmark calibration by a uniform asymptotic certificate-mass gap, not by an asserted lower bound on the schedules."}, "key_stuck_points": ["Aggregate-only state forces K'=A and loses accumulated endpoint information.", "Retaining the primitive dual residual requires an enlarged joint boundary certificate and a successful silver calibration."], "verification_status": "unverified; no new submission"}, "glossary": {"D": "Sum of nonnegative proof weights for the objective-prefix certificate; independent of physical2sum(h)+1.", "e": "Gradient aggregate minus physical GD displacement for the prefix.", "nu,K": "Coefficient and terminal-gradient scale in E>=nu||e-Kg_end||^2.", "A": "One plus the primitive suffix's physical step sum.", "D_b": "2A-1, the suffix standard proof-weight mass.", "U_b": "Suffix weighted gradient aggregate with weights(b,A).", "eta,beta": "Added prefix terminal proof weight and scale on suffix weights.", "k": "1+1/nu in the tested constant-nu subrule.", "L_0": "Unique positive solution above(2-1/lambda)/(lambda-1) of L*((lambda-1)L-2+1/lambda)^2=2/lambda^3."}}
